- mr. president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to point out to you a problem that, with the accession of croatia to the european union, has become a european problem. twenty years ago, and a little more than that, there was a war in croatia, as you know, and after that there were a lot of unexploded ordnance, or mines. today, nearly one fifth of croatia's population lives near suspected minefields. although the croatian governments have allocated over eur 320 million to this problem since 1998, through the croatian mine action centre, the problem is still not solved. so that leaves another 400 square miles of suspect land and about 74,000 registered mines. these mines are primarily a safety problem, but of course they're also an environmental and economic problem. that is why i want to raise this issue in the european parliament so that it can be resolved as soon as possible. - thank you. - thank you.

vice-president of the commission. − mr president, firstly i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr milana, for this very important contribution. the commission welcomes parliament’s reiterated support for a strategy for the adriatic and ionian region. as you know, this strategy and its action plan address, inter alia, maritime and marine issues, fisheries, aquaculture, marine environment protection and the related research and innovation dimension. ownership of the strategy by local communities and authorities will be critical to its success. as regards fisheries, the strategy should support action to improve the culture of compliance and enhance cooperation on the control of fishing activities. the commission is convinced that, to ensure the sustainability of shared fish stocks, all countries exploiting marine resources in the area have to play by the same rules. the commission agrees on the need to improve scientific knowledge on the state of stocks. action in this field should be supported by the new data-collection framework. the commission intends to adopt a communication on the eu strategy for the adriatic and ionian region, accompanied by an action plan, by the end of the first semester of 2014, and it is clear that the suggestions made by the rapporteur and parliamentarians will feed into this work.

- commissioner mimica, ladies and gentlemen, while reciprocity, i.e. the reintroduction of the visa regime for those countries which introduce it for eu citizens, can be understood and accepted, i would like to draw attention to the possible consequences of the alleged application of the so-called safeguard clause in cases of emergency. the suspension of the visa-free regime for third countries, which would occur as a consequence of daily political assessments by a member state, could further jeopardise the political and economic environment needed to implement structural reforms, in particular in the eu candidate countries. on the contrary, i support the positions that show that shortening the asylum procedure would be a good solution. we should follow the example of countries like belgium, which recently introduced changes to the asylum procedure and thus avoided imposing non-selective sanctions on citizens of any country and in particular, i repeat, on citizens of eu candidate countries. in the european year of citizens, it would be good to send a message of encouragement to all europe's citizens, a message that the eu's borders will not become insurmountable barriers drawn by unprincipled interests. - thank you. - thank you.

- someone well said that only to the shallow analyst can it seem that concern about sea travel in the age of the internet sounds like an anachronism. the global trading system still depends on the transportation of vast quantities of the world's most important food and energy by ship. there are at least three bottlenecks at the moment; one is the gulf of aden where a large multinational fleet mr president, i would like to thank the rapporteur in particular for this report, which highlights the importance of the active participation of local and regional authorities in cohesion policy. i particularly welcome the emphasis on the importance of linking strategic priorities and objectives adopted at eu level with national, regional and local objectives. partnership as a principle of cohesion policy is particularly promoted and emphasis is placed on local action groups, which should be developed and encouraged as they are the drivers of innovative and creative solutions. the report specifically stresses that projects financed by the structural instruments should not be included in the structural deficit calculation, which i fully support. it is necessary to strike the right balance between the need for fiscal consolidation on the one hand and the need for strong investment in growth and development supported by structural instruments on the other. without this, we will not be able to launch the less developed member states.
as a representative from croatia, where local government units have major problems with financial sustainability, i welcome the efforts to raise awareness on this issue. however, i do not think that there is a need to insist on the application of a single model at union level, given that member states do not have the same systems of local self-government and no single level of decentralisation. i agree that we need to work to make sure that the less developed regions and countries catch up with the more developed ones. solidarity is a fundamental european value, but we should avoid in time the danger that richer regions and countries feel exploited by poorer ones. i therefore believe that the union should, above all, promote reforms of local government in member states where this is necessary to ensure that the money from the funds is used as efficiently as possible. for us from poorer countries, the richer members can offer much more than money, above all, constructive advice and help in implementing reforms that would make our system as efficient as theirs.
the current crisis has indeed affected the situation in local and regional self-government. on the one hand, they have a problem with decreasing revenues as central authorities cut transfers and ask local authorities to contribute to austerity measures, and on the other hand, they have to ensure increased demands for social services. so i think it's imperative that local government and self-government units are geared towards investments that are sustainable and smart. it is important that the committee of the regions has accepted this proposal unanimously, by a large majority, and so has the committee on regional development. so, to focus everything on competitiveness, growth and job creation and to allow the flexibility of the stability and growth pact. local and regional authorities must not be punished for the difficulties faced by member states at national level and i therefore join those who advocate simplifying the rules and increasing transparency in the management of the structural funds, thus enabling better and faster implementation of projects, especially for croatia, which has just joined and is just on the threshold of using the funds.
cohesion policy is the european union's main instrument in the fight against crisis and recession. but the arrangements arising from the stability and growth pact could undo some of the positive effects of cohesion policy. the national savings obligation and the consequent difficulty for local and regional authorities to co-finance projects would significantly restrict the possibility of using the structural funds. as is usually the case, of course, the poorer and less developed regions would be the hardest hit. for this reason, i support this report which proposes a series of measures to exempt the co-financing of cohesion policy programmes either partially or fully from the obligations of the stability and growth pact. i therefore consider it a step in the right direction, as it is a resource that is geared towards new jobs, growth and competitiveness.

i would like to start by congratulating the rapporteur on her work and highlight two elements which i consider to be of particular importance. the first is, of course, the respect of international law in the resolution of a maritime boundary dispute as the rapporteur has both pointed out and included in article 3 of the motion for a resolution. the observation of the provisions of the un convention on the law of the sea is also of particular importance. the european parliament, by adopting this resolution, will also support the work of the arbitration court, which is required to determine the maritime border between croatia and slovenia on the basis of the 1982 convention, and therefore on the basis of legal criteria rather than political criteria. the second important point is article 15 of the resolution which encourages member states to declare their exclusive economic zones or protected zones. i believe that this confirms once again the european agreement, also at this level, at the level of the european parliament, that after the arbitration court's ruling and in accordance with the accession agreement, croatia will fully apply its ecological and fishing zone and thereby contribute to the protection of the adriatic sea.
in his report, rapporteur salavrakos correctly notes that the mediterranean accounts for almost a third of the world's maritime traffic, that three quarters of fish species are overfished, and that the situation will be further aggravated by the projected strong increase in urban development and population on its coasts. at the same time, the rate of sea recovery in the mediterranean is consistently low, clearly indicating the need for integrated coastal and maritime management. the integrated approach should also involve the public and private sectors at national and european level between member states, as well as in relations with third countries. this is the only way to ensure sustainable development and avoid conflict. in the adriatic sea, where most of the exploited fishery stock is shared between several fishing fleets, an integrated approach and the establishment of dialogue is particularly important and a prerequisite for long-term sustainable development. despite the projected increase in population settlement on the mediterranean coast, certain areas, such as the smaller islands, are facing depopulation, with croatia being particularly affected. it is in these areas that the economic dimension of fisheries is particularly important. in this way, i want to support efforts to bring the integrated approach into line with environmental protection rules, but, in addition to the ecological dimension, the economic dimension of the sector's conservation and the economic benefits that fisheries bring, especially to local communities, must also be taken into account.
as a representative of croatia, the country that dominates most of the eastern adriatic coast, but also as a resident of a coastal region that lives largely on fishing, i am particularly interested in the issue of sustainable management of marine resources and the protection of fish stocks from overfishing. i am glad that parliament is proposing the same methods of protection against overexploitation as the study on the maritime zones in the mediterranean sea. commission, the designation of exclusive economic zones would give all coastal states greater control over resources, and would significantly reduce the scope for abuse. through close intergovernmental cooperation and joint surveillance, we would eliminate the possibility of exploiting the existing fragmentation of the system that allows particular interests to prevail over the vision of sustainable development and the future that vision provides. i call on the commission to make its position clear on this issue and to accept the recommendations of the expert study and this resolution.

in writing. − corruption affects human rights as is stated in numerous conventions and in the charter of the un and the charter of fundamental rights of the eu. fighting human rights violations in third countries is one of the roles of the eu in promoting democratisation. violation of human rights through corruption is one of the factors that is detrimental to the political development of a country and the establishment of an unbiased judicial system. bribery also has an economic side-effect: according to world bank estimates, corruption represents 5 % of global gdp, and it also increases the costs of doing business on a global scale by 10 %. the single vote of support shows that efforts to combat corruption in third countries should focus on supporting the education system, a free and independent media, and groups fighting corruption already active in those countries. efforts should also be directed towards fighting the alleged involvement of eu institutions (public banks) in corruption scandals in third countries. this shows that there is a need for coherence between internal and external policies within the eu and for increased accountability and transparency in external aid and public budgets.
in writing. − under private international law in the eu, the recast brussels i regulation decides which court settles employment disputes. the rome i and rome ii regulations deal with employment contracts and industrial action. furthermore, an employee may only be sued in courts in their place of residence, while on the other hand, an employee may bring a case against their employer in the courts of a member state where the employer is resident, or where ‘the business which engaged the employee is or was situated’. this is potentially hurtful to employees, especially where there is no stable place of work. moreover, an employee may be sued in the member state where the ‘harmful event’ took place, but this incorporates both the location causing the damage and the place where the firm experienced a loss. thus, a single vote on abolishing the factor of the ‘engaging place of businessʼ and accepting a reference to the ‘place from where the employee receives day-to-day instructions’ will strengthen the relationship between the courts and re-establish the balance in employment law.
written. − mr president, the purpose of the new maritime labour convention (mlc) of 2006, which entered into force on 20 august 2013, is to ensure the dignified life and work of seafarers, both on board and ashore. it also aims to ensure a fair race among the world's sailors. the convention regulates almost all aspects of seafarers' work and life, including safety standards, working conditions, employment, and medical care. the convention is to be observed not only by ships flying the flag of states parties to the convention but by all ships calling at ports of states parties. the very act of adopting the convention means little without the introduction of surveillance measures by the signatory states. i therefore welcome this legislative resolution of the european parliament which commits member states to the introduction of a system of monitoring compliance with the standards laid down in the convention on ships flying the flag of eu member states, as well as the obligation of all member states to carry out inspections in ports. this legislation, which lays down control mechanisms and obligations for member states, will ensure that seafarers have a decent working and living environment. this is a very important issue for croatia, known for its long maritime tradition and officers who are among the most respected in the world, and which has some 17,000 registered seafarers and several thousand more who are not required to register in croatia.
in writing. − the maritime labour convention (mlc) aims to reduce the likelihood of accidents due to failing standards through a global and comprehensive monitoring system. this directive is intended to ensure that member states start implementing mlc provisions with the help of the eu and would make member states responsible for all the ships flying their flag. one warning however is that this directive should not reduce the level of protection currently in place and enjoyed by seafarers. flag states can adapt monitoring mechanisms on all ships of less than 200 gross tonnage not engaged in international voyages. states need to make sure that seafarers have access to this agreement. authorised monitoring staff should be competent to conduct these inspections. the inspections may be carried out by public institutions and member states will provide the international labour office with a list of these institutions. there should be a procedure for dealing with on-board complaints. member states are to notify the commission of the text of the main measures of national law which they adopt in the field covered by this directive.
written. − while i welcome the initiative to align european legislation with the seafarers' labour convention and support many of the points in this report, i must stress that i share the concerns of my club of meps about the union's competence when it comes to the application of the directive at global level. i am thinking of the more than 25 000 active croatian seafarers and the growing number of those seeking their fortune on the high seas, given the lack of jobs on land. the need for any income forces them onto ships that sail under flags of convenience and often have rather low standards of living and work. i agree that we must find a way to protect them from exploitation and provide them with decent living and working conditions. i am also aware of the fact that the union has no mechanism to enforce this directive on third countries that have not ratified the convention. the imposition of additional burdens on shipping companies flying european flags could lead to an increase in the number of vessels flying flags of such states and flags of convenience and thus further reduce the possibility of affecting the living and working conditions of european seafarers, thus having the opposite effect to the one desired. the union should therefore undertake a series of diplomatic activities towards third countries, aimed at adopting certain standards to protect european seafarers.

on behalf of the ecr club. i would like to thank the rapporteur for her quality report. i will personally support the motion for a resolution with a great deal of regret that transparency in the management of public money is being called into question in the european union institutions. the economic crisis and the growth of social insecurity have seriously damaged the union's image in the eyes of its citizens, and irresponsibility and lack of transparency in the management of taxpayers' money further tarnish the community's tarnished image. i myself come from a country that faces the problem of corruption and lack of transparency in public procurement procedures, but i will proudly state that croatia has done a lot to improve the situation. i also expect progress from the european union, so i hope we won't have this dilemma in the next discharge. the union must be a model of transparency and financial accountability and use its authority to influence member states and candidate countries to make progress on this issue themselves. if it is not able to do so, the idea of the union becomes worthless because the principles on which it is based will not be applied.
the european research area is a highly relevant topic today for strengthening the european union's global competitiveness. i am thinking in particular of our target of a few years ago to invest up to 3% of the european union's gross domestic product in research, which is still an unrealized target and presents us with the task of trying to achieve, both at european level and at the level of national budgets, and in combination with private investment in research, precisely that level which will make us competitive with other countries and other parts of the world. there was already talk today in the lectures about which are the european universities ranking. i think we can all agree that there's a lot of room for improvement in that regard. what i think is particularly important to complete the era and as one of our main projects in the context of innovation and in the next multiannual financial framework and the 2020 target under horizon 2020 is to remove as much as possible of the barriers to the movement of scientists and researchers within the european union, to give them similar rewards, at least if not the same, to avoid brain drain because it is precisely staying at our universities, at our research centres that is one of the essential preconditions for development and for merging what is done in science with what is useful in the economy, in the business world and ultimately for the good of all citizens of the european union. in the past few years, through various other european union programmes, croatia has managed to participate with its approximately 309 partners in as many as 248 projects with over 70 million euros in total benefit in research projects. this was all preparatory time; there is a great interest of the academic community, and especially cooperation between the croatian academy of sciences and arts and ministries and other stakeholders to make the most of all the benefits of the european research area.
the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's research and innovation strategy, and will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's research and innovation strategy. in these conditions of unemployment and crisis in europe, every member state, and my country croatia, needs the best research with the greatest impact on growth and development. the investments need to be efficient because there are not enough funds and it is difficult to reach the target of 3% of gross domestic product investment. the exchange and dissemination of research knowledge between member states is very important because it multiplies the effects of investment and that is why programmes such as horizon 2020 and fp7 are important both in croatia and in all member states. at national level, it is important to involve scientific and research institutions in local action groups in order to actively participate in the adoption of smart specialisation strategies and subsequent research to help the growth and development of smes and the whole country.
mr president, the fact that there is a billion euros of trade between europe and china every day is enough to show how strategically important this issue is for all of us and how much potential there is to increase the level of investment which is currently not satisfactory. i cannot agree with lord darmuth's statement that individual countries can negotiate with china better than the european union. i think that only together, as the eu, can we reach a favourable agreement for each of our countries, our economy and especially for our small and medium-sized enterprises for which we must ensure better conditions for access to the chinese market, and of course a level playing field with chinese competition. what's missing is perhaps a strategic approach. china certainly has it in europe, and the case of croatia shows it. when the chinese realized that croatia was entering the eu before our neighbors, they intensified relations and the result is that croatia is today china's first foreign trade partner in southeast europe. so we have one strategic approach from the chinese side and that is the commission's message. this agreement, which concerns, of course, investment, must be part of a larger story, a larger package of measures, a long-term sustainable industrial policy of the european union. finally, we must also consider in this context the respect for social rights and environmental standards mentioned in this resolution, and our ultimate aim must be to achieve balanced trade with china, to preserve production in europe and, of course, to create new jobs in europe.
mr president, over two million refugees, including 400,000 children under 11, six million people without a roof over their heads; these are not just numbers, they are a reflection of human suffering which has its own weight and therefore it is not necessary to link the syrian tragedy with that of lampedusa for europe to react. in fact, it would be completely wrong to bring the syrian tragedy into the debate on the european union's immigration policy. i come from a country that alone took in 800,000 refugees and exiles during milosevic's aggression. i also remember my father here, who was himself a refugee from world war ii. and i learned something from all of this: the most important thing for refugees is to go home. and that has to be the focus of our debate and our policy. so, of course, we support this resolution, but, above all, this parliament must communicate more with those democratic forces in the syrian opposition, those who will not persecute but protect christians, and especially our goal, that is, the goal of our policy must be that every syrian has the right to return to his home.

written. - the single market is the basis for the functioning of the eu, so the epc can only increase competitiveness in the labour market and provide employers with easier access to the labour force and workers with a quicker and easier procedure when it comes to working in another member state. the european parliament has also called for the eu to make the eu's labour market more accessible and to improve working conditions. the importance of continuing education in the specific fields of their professional activity for certain medical professionals (general practitioners, dental practitioners, nurses, pharmacists, etc.) should be emphasised. this measure should not be a pretext for certain member states with high unemployment to portray the removal from their registers of their registered workers who have gone to work in another member state as skilled workers as a successful measure to combat unemployment. member states must continue to implement all measures aimed at reducing unemployment.
written. − sustainable development and environmental protection are at the heart of the europe 2020 strategy. one of the important instruments for ensuring the implementation of sustainable projects is the environmental impact study. the proposed amendments are intended to improve and follow up on new trends and requirements such as those for transparency and smart specialisation. environmental impact studies are time-consuming and costly, which affects the dynamics of project preparation and implementation. it is therefore important to find a balance between the studies that need to be done and the added value that is obtained by doing the studies. the requirements to be met when drawing up the estimate should be proportionate to the size and stage of the project for which it is being drawn up. it is important to simplify procedures in terms of fewer forms and administrative procedures. in particular, the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the environmental impact assessment and the implementation of the environmental impact assessment.
mr. chairman, i was restrained because as a representative of croatia i cannot ignore the duplicity of this initiative. it builds on personal freedoms through greater freedom of movement and employment opportunities, but it also facilitates the further demise of poor member states. i agree that the citizens of the union as individuals must be allowed the freedom to decide their own destiny and that the national state should not interfere in matters of a personal nature in order for us to enjoy freedom and democracy to the full. but i have a feeling that the union does not understand mobility with the degree of responsibility that it should. in theory, it is a practical solution that facilitates the needs of the common labour market and allows greater individual freedom, but in practice it condemns poor member states to economic and demographic ruin. if on the one hand we talk about solidarity and stress the need to reduce the gap between the richest and poorest members, then we should be able to understand what it means for the poorer members to be left without a young population and national experts.
mr president, first of all i would like to express my satisfaction that we have voted today on a very important document of secondary legislation of the european union, namely amendments to the 2005 directive, which will contribute to better mobility of our citizens, greater security in the recognition of professional qualifications and, ultimately, benefit the important aspect of employment. i particularly commend commissioner barnier's efforts on all elements relating to strengthening the european union's single market, and i am also very pleased that we have just made an important step forward in confidence in those professions where we require specific minimum criteria to be met, such as doctors, doctors of mental medicine, such as pharmacists, such as nurses, midwives, veterinarians and architects. i think this directive is an extraordinary step forward in the functioning of the single market of the european union.
in writing. − the issue of professional mobility is of fundamental importance for competitiveness and employment in europe but unfortunately, nowadays, not all citizens have the same opportunities, sometimes due to bureaucratic problems. i therefore voted in favour of simplifying bureaucratic procedures and favouring all those citizens who wish to move within the european union in order to continue or start their profession abroad, while ensuring a high level of quality and safety for workers and consumers/patients (in the case of medical professions). the creation of a european professional card could thus help to simplify the recognition of the various qualifications and reduce administrative costs.
written. − freedom of movement is one of the four fundamental freedoms of eu citizens and includes the right of workers to move and reside, the right of family members to enter and reside, and the right to work in another member state. it shall entail the abolition of any discrimination based on nationality between workers of the member states in respect of employment, remuneration and other conditions of work and employment. these amendments to the professional qualifications directive further simplify the free movement of workers within the internal market and focus on the untapped potential of those workers who have the specific knowledge and qualifications required. the complexity of recruitment procedures outside the borders of one's own country is simplified by the introduction of the european professional card. this appropriation is intended to cover the financing of the european schools in the member states and the european schools in the union. this will allow for automatic recognition of the profession, without additional procedures for the award and verification of diplomas and titles, for doctors, nurses, midwives, dentists, veterinary surgeons, pharmacists and architects. for all these reasons, i have supported the amendments to this important directive.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, i want to speak on this problem from a specifically croatian perspective. right-wing, as well as left-wing extremism in croatia is not a mass phenomenon, and incidents occur sporadically and are mainly related to fan groups and similar groups. however, it should be noted that croatia has been ruled by a totalitarian communist regime for 50 years, which has left deep divisions and painful wounds in society. many young people cannot reconcile themselves to the fact that their family members have been persecuted for years for simply having different opinions, and they are distressed by the increasing glorification of the failed communist regime, which is being reinforced by the current economic crisis. an additional problem is that the state has never clearly condemned the regime or fully confronted its totalitarian legacy. adding fuel to the fire are irresponsible politicians who, when it suits them, do not hesitate to fan base passions and further deepen social divisions. it especially, unfortunately, affects young people.
madam president, first of all, i would like to express my condolences on the great tragedy in lampedusa where over 200 migrants from north africa were killed. i think it's just another tragedy in a series that's happened over the last 20 years or so. more than 20,000 people have ended their journey to a better future in the mediterranean. it's a huge problem that concerns all the members of the european union, all the mediterranean countries. croatia is a mediterranean country, but it's not as exposed to this wave of migration as italy, malta or our other neighbouring countries. it seems to me that we have three levels of this problem. the first is that we try to resolve relations with partners in north africa through the union for the mediterranean, through other frameworks, to prevent the problem through dialogue and to reduce the number of illegal migrations. the second level of the problem is that we have a clear legal framework that regulates the issue of irregular migration and that strengthens and empowers member states in this regard. and the third is that by using eurosur, by using frontex, by using all the mechanisms, we are raising the technological capacity of the european union to a level that will allow us to prevent tragedies like this one that happened a few days ago, and in that sense i think coordinated joint action at both the union and the member states level is crucial to preventing such events in the future.
madam president, let me first of all express my deep regret at the recent tragedy at lampedusa, which is a reminder that unfortunate people, over 1 500 of them from north africa, lost their lives last year in their attempt to reach europe. so the mediterranean is increasingly becoming more than a cradle of civilization, it's becoming synonymous with the blue abyss. it is also a very serious alarm that the areas of immigration and asylum seeking in the european union are not properly coordinated and defined. i am particularly and personally interested in this subject since i come from croatia, a euro-mediterranean country, whose geostrategic position, together with its specific coastal configuration, puts it in a particularly vulnerable position which could be further accentuated by its recent accession to the european union. the trend is that the number of immigrants and asylum seekers is increasing in countries joining the european union. in croatia, a significant increase occurred even before entry: the number of asylum seekers as well as the number of illegal border crossing attempts doubled in 2012 compared to the previous year. the strong increase in the number of immigrants and asylum seekers caused by various economic and political issues, along with concerns for their safety, is one of the main reasons why existing regulations and practices need to be reorganised to be more effective. to protect the lives of its citizens and those who want to become one, the european union must not spare under the pretext of the continuing economic crisis.
on behalf of the ecr club. - madam president, as a representative of the coastal state with the longest external land border, i fully support the introduction of a system of stricter controls at the union's external borders. i believe that enhanced surveillance is a key measure with primarily security but also socio-economic implications. the european union is the leading donor to third countries and represents a place where many people have found happiness in search of a better life. but in order to guarantee prosperity and security to those we have welcomed, as well as to the indigenous population, a stricter immigration policy is imperative. it must also be accompanied by controls of the external borders to stem the new waves of illegal immigration that some member states are finding increasingly difficult to cope with. i stress that the creation of eurosur, while good in its spirit, will not have the desired effect until all eu member states are admitted to the schengen area. with a number of countries on the external borders not in the schengen area, the schengen area itself is debased because its borders, because of the recession, because of good neighbourly relations, are no longer subject to the controls that membership of the area implies. as a coastal state with a very long land border with third countries, croatia is a special case and needs to be further supported. the eu will continue to support the implementation of the schengen acquis and the union's policy on asylum, in particular by providing the necessary financial and logistical support to the member states and the commission to ensure that the necessary capacity is built up in the asylum reception centres.
madam president, i sincerely hope that the european border control system will help to solve tragedies such as the lampedusa case. the reasons why i support this project are multiple. in addition to enabling the detection of ships, this system will save lives and avoid tragedies at sea in the case of illegal immigration and will improve the management of migration flows. therefore, a balanced sharing of responsibilities between all member states on this issue is also necessary. in order to achieve the objectives of eurosur, croatia has a national coordination centre, which is scheduled to open in december this year. the key moment for the surveillance of croatian territorial waters is the operational imagery of the state of the maritime system, which will allow the monitoring of the entire maritime system with two key objectives, namely the detection of illegal immigration and the saving of human lives at sea. in conclusion, it should be noted that croatia will apply to schengen in two years and for its implementation some material support is reasonably expected. croatia is making significant efforts to meet the strict schengen conditions for which the control of maritime and land borders is equally important and this is a priority issue of security policy in the coming period for croatia.
madam president, i would like to say that by coming from croatia and by our membership, which is being completed at the moment, we are in fact extending the european economic area and i can draw a parallel between the european economic area, efta and our membership of cefta, which we had to leave on 30 june last year. it seems to me that these are countries which are practically integrated into the internal market of the european union, which are economically highly developed, which are either surrounded by or dependent on the member states of the european union and which, in the context of eventual enlargement, would not represent a major obstacle for the european union, and we are a little sorry that iceland, which negotiated with us in parallel for a while, has abandoned this objective for a number of reasons which my colleagues have mentioned. i was therefore pleased with commissioner malmström's analysis of the options for strengthening cooperation with the european economic area, and in particular the three options for further structuring relations with andorra, san marino and monaco as small but respectable states that also have a proper place in the framework of cooperation with the european union.
mr president, the european economic area since 1992, when the agreement was signed, has been producing positive results and is growing stronger despite the major changes over the past 20 years. the union has expanded considerably and the rules governing the european economic area have been continuously changing, with the number of rules increasing significantly since 1992 to more than 6 000 today. this shows that it is a very flexible and robust system that is important for the functioning of the european union and the realisation of fundamental freedoms. however, what we need to ask ourselves is how to respond to the shocks currently taking place in the economic area and the internal market due to high unemployment, social insecurity, public debt and weak public finances. we need to continue to show flexibility, to change regulations and institutional mechanisms and thereby enable a more efficient and meaningful use of national and european budgets to strengthen entrepreneurship, the entrepreneurial environment, reduce unemployment and poverty.
mr president, apart from all the understandable and acceptable reasons of a humanitarian nature, the protection of basic human rights and so on, there are other reasons why i voted for this resolution. the european union must not be a training ground for american politics, especially not for american intelligence. european foreign policy and american foreign policy sometimes coincide, sometimes not. america has its interests, europe has its interests, its principles of functioning and sometimes it has to have a foreign policy and a policy altogether different from that which our friends across the ocean have. if we're dealing with criminals illegally, i won't say criminally, but with illegal methods we become very similar and it's hard to find that thin line between "bad guys" and "good guys". all of this has made this resolution necessary, and i hope that all the countries of the european union will respect it.
madam president, as we hear, this is about the future of the planet, and certainly the warsaw conference will be a preparation for the paris conference. however, i had the chance to participate on behalf of the council of europe in a conference in copenhagen which we know did not bring any progress. so we have to be firm in saying that all actors must fulfil their obligations, first of all the heads of government, who should be on the european commission's side, but also all local and regional heads because this is about human rights, even though it may not seem so at first glance. so energy policy and climate change policy must be complementary. if we don't get that in sync, we're not gonna get very far. we need to invest in innovation, we need to change the economy, we need to create economic growth, but i'm not sure that the changes in the energy system will come if we have too high a price on carbon dioxide. so there's a period of adjustment, but if that paris agreement in 2015 doesn't have a legally binding, doesn't have concrete numbers, doesn't have concrete actions - and i agree with everyone so far who has said that it has to have sanctions - we're not going to get very far. so i ask the question: what is the signal that the european union is sending to the world? it was once a leader, a model, but what is the european union doing today?
madam president, it seems to me that the warsaw conference is an important interim step in the discussions we are having in the run-up to the new big deal in paris which will be a global agreement and a legally binding agreement on climate change. i think this report, the fifth report of the intergovernmental panel on climate change, is a great impetus for the preparation of this debate, because it clearly indicates the key aspects that are the challenges to climate change in the world at this time. the european union has also been a leader and a role model for all other global actors in meeting ambitious greenhouse gas reduction targets. i think we need to continue this policy, and in doing so it's important to balance both our economic development and our social issues with what are the major issues of climate change and environmental protection in the years ahead. so i think it's good for warsaw to take a common position and slightly modify the resolution that's currently on the table.
mr president, trade policy is a policy and cannot be separated from other policies, at least not by states and associations that consider themselves democratic and that have human rights as an important agenda. the european union certainly is and must be. in this context, i think we should look at the events and the situation in the countries we are talking about, so i would take ukraine and armenia as examples. ukraine has made significant, tangible progress in the area of justice, in the area of election law, and it looks like the tymoshenko case will be resolved in a sort of compromise way, at least according to the speaker of the ukrainian parliament, which of course does not mean that the law should be written for one person. the law should be comprehensive. on the other hand, it should not be overlooked that russian threats also exist against ukraine and these threats include various measures that may be painful for the ukrainian economy. the european union must know where it stands in this situation and how to react and how to send a message to russia that what is happening is in russia's interest. on the other hand, armenia, under pressure from russia, has apparently accepted membership in the eurasian union. the door to these neighbors of ours should never be closed. neighbors are not chosen, they are given, and they are the ones to live with and work with. but on the other hand, a contract without a trade part, and the trade part can't be included, of course, i don't see how it could work. in this situation, the commission must, it seems to me, take a very clear position.
mr president, the european union's trade policy towards the eastern partnership countries is indeed more than just a trade or economic dimension, it has a strategic dimension and that is why we must do everything we can to make it a success in vilnius at the end of november. success, which, of course, means respecting the standards we set, means respecting the values we stand for, and nothing we can, must or should do jeopardizes that. success, however, in terms of a strategic breakthrough at this point, does mean the expansion of economic freedoms, free trade, eastward. it is a major move that has also stirred up spirits in moscow, which has embarked on a policy of pressure on the eastern partnership countries, and even on lithuania itself, which holds the presidency of the union. the european parliament and the european union have rightly reacted against such a policy of pressure, but it must also be said that despite all these challenges, the european union has not lost sight of the strategic objective that we want to achieve by signing and initialising the vilnius agreement, and that strategic objective is precisely the expansion of freedom, the expansion of prosperity and the expansion, ultimately, of security in europe, not in opposition to russia, but open and cooperative with russia through a policy that will replace the old logic of division and confrontation with a new policy of dialogue and cooperation.
mr president, the european union's trade policy towards the eastern partnership countries is an important part of the overall political and security and economic relationship with armenia, azerbaijan, georgia, belarus, ukraine and moldova and i think it is very important for us that the vilnius summit, which is extremely important for lithuania as the council presidency at this moment, as i myself was convinced by our recent visit to vilnius by the budget committee, should be a success. but it can be successful by not allowing the countries in our immediate neighbourhood to drift in some way away from our focus on brussels, on what will both expand democratic values and strengthen the rule of law, and ultimately be beneficial to those countries' trade relations with the european union. i believe that the content of comprehensive and integrated free trade agreements will significantly strengthen trade trends which are currently not so significant in the overall volume of european union trade with third countries and here i believe that there is a great deal of scope for our eastern neighbourhood to really become a political priority for brussels.
on behalf of the ecr club. mr president, the synthesis of drugs from legally traded precursors has made the fight against drugs more difficult for european society. while the substances criminals need to operate have become more available than ever. i fully agree that we need to act swiftly and create an effective system of control of drug precursor trade and facilitate the exchange of data between member states. i believe that all our efforts must be directed towards being one step ahead of the criminals, and while we do not achieve this ideal i certainly support the rapporteur in her quest to at least be one step ahead of the drug dealers by making it faster and easier to add their new drugs to the list of precursors. but i feel it necessary to point out that even this better regulation proposal will not be enough without quality cooperation between the police and judicial authorities of the member states and the candidate countries. for croatia this is particularly important because of the close links between the criminal structures of the former yugoslav republics dating from the pre-war period when state borders did not exist as a barrier. today, these borders exist and must serve a purpose as the foundation of future good-neighborly relations, but also as an impassable barrier to drug smugglers and other criminals. to achieve this we must provide croatia with all the logistical and financial support and prepare it for accession to the schengen area as soon as possible. croatia, because of its location and shape, is the first line of defence against drug traffickers coming into western europe via turkey and the balkans, and we should not allow it to be the weakest fortress in the european union.
member of the commission. − mr president, i would like to thank you for the views expressed during this debate. these views confirm the convergence and the very similar – or even identical – approach that we have on the importance, need and impact of the proposed regulation. there were some good examples and proposals that would actually provide for an even stricter and better regulation, especially the proposal that i heard here – and we also heard it from the council – to include medicinal products containing ephedrine or pseudoephedrine in the definition of scheduled substances. the commission would approach this issue favourably. my only comment today is that we need to find a wording that needs to be aligned in order to avoid any confusion during implementation. in any case you will soon vote on the amendment to the regulation on the monitoring of trade between the european union and third countries in drug precursors. with this new regulation, the eu will continue to set the gold standard at international level in the control of drug precursors. it will make a major contribution to next year’s review of the united nations plan of action on drugs. once approved, it will be for the member states’ authorities to implement the regulation’s new features. the commission will support this process and monitor the regulation’s effectiveness in tackling new trends in the diversion of drug precursors. i look forward to a positive vote in this house.
mr. president, lately all over europe and beyond, we've been seeing manifestos of discrimination. from xenophobia, hate speech to other forms of racial and national discrimination. since this is a violation of the general principles of the european union, i believe that there is a need for a strong condemnation of such events right here in the european parliament. most member states have detailed rules on promoting diversity and prohibiting discrimination. for example, in croatia, hate crime is considered a particularly serious form of crime, hate speech is criminalized, and the rights of national minorities are elaborated in detail, from representation in representative bodies to the free use of language and letters, and this is not just a dead letter on paper. although the young croatian democracy in a short time has profiled itself as a country with high democratic standards, today it can stand alongside the pioneers of democracy in this matter. it would be hypocritical to have laws and not enforce them and it would also be hypocritical to stand by and watch discrimination and not condemn it. let us send a message that europe is the home of everyone who wants to feel that way and that we want to actively fight for it.
mr president, the european union was created to promote freedom, not to stifle it. that is why i do not think this proposal is in the european spirit. this motion for a resolution systematically challenges the freedom and right of citizens to conscientious objection. the right of citizens to say, "no, i don't want to participate in an abortion. no, i don't want to be a part of ending the life of an unborn child. we must stand here on the side of the weakest. we have to take a stand here for example, the cousin of jage stojak from the knin hospital in croatia who refused to participate in an abortion this summer and was fired for it. it was only thanks to the charter of fundamental rights of the european union, thanks to the work of ngos that mrs stojak was able to return to work, but after severe torture. we have to stand with these women who are afraid that if they don't participate in abortion they will lose their jobs. so we have to say "yes" to freedom, "yes" to conscientious objection, and we have to say "no" to this resolution.
mr. chairman, i thank the rapporteur. partnership and decision-making in cooperation with different stakeholders are at the heart of european policies. i support the stronger involvement of civil society in the adoption of strategic documents and the definition of priorities at the level of local units. working together, not only with civil society, but also with scientific institutions, entrepreneurs, social partners, contributes to better decision-making and to building a more transparent and accountable society. the commission should also be able to provide information on the implementation of the union's policies and to provide information on the implementation of the union's policies and to provide information on the implementation of the union's policies and to provide information on the implementation of the union's policies and on the implementation of the union's policies and strategies. civil society can very successfully raise awareness of inconsistencies and demand a greater degree of accountability for the efficient use of public revenues, all for the benefit of citizens.
mr president, like other colleagues, i congratulate our rapporteur, mrs creţu, on her interesting report on local authorities and civil society in the context of the european union's development policy. the european union is a global leader in the context of development policy, and i am therefore pleased that you have highlighted in your report the role of our partners in the non-governmental sector and of other non-state actors, of which there are a number, as our auxiliary agents in promoting our millennium development goals together with the un, as well as other objectives of the european union's development policy. when it comes to local authorities, i think they are particularly important, precisely because they are also responsible for regional development, because they are closest to the people, because they are essential for strengthening social cohesion, and it is the actors at local level who can best transpose the ideas we have within the european union with our partners on other continents to strengthen, i would say, the structures of society and thus help both their development and our development policy.
- mr president, the problem of unemployment is becoming an increasing burden at union level, and in a few member states, including croatia, extensive structural reforms are needed to address this burning issue. some of these reforms are related to the education system. i will particularly emphasise the problem of adapting public educational institutions to the real needs of the labour market, which leads to the paradox of producing too many highly qualified personnel with public money, who then have to be supported by the state because they are unable to find a job in croatia, but neither to enter the process of re-qualification. for this reason, i support the proposed introduction of partnerships between educational institutions and the private sector, but i will stress once again that the mobility that is insisted on is not the solution, as it will leave poorer member states without professionals and young people. croatia would thus become a nature park on the edge of the union, which is unacceptable. i believe that the best solution is to encourage reform of the public education system to make it complementary to the labor market of the home country, thereby keeping national experts in their home countries.
- mr president, i fully support this report and consider it extremely important not only because it identifies the problems facing young people but also because this report proposes concrete measures to national governments and parliaments to raise the standards of young people in both education and employment. as a representative from croatia, and aware of the alarming fact that croatia has a terribly high rate of youth unemployment, i particularly welcome the efforts made by the committee on culture and education to affirm the importance of internships through this report. i also welcome the fact that this report stresses the importance of vocational guidance, and that it is of the utmost importance to adapt structural education and investment in it to the needs of the labour market, while, of course, taking care to ensure that everyone has the right to choose. it must not happen that people with high professional skills in which the state invests many years cannot establish an employment relationship. this is why systematic communication between educational institutions on the one hand and employers on the other should be encouraged. finally, i welcome the chapters in the report which stress the need to encourage research and science. investment in science definitely means economic progress of all kinds.
- mr president, this report by mrs macovei, in its main messages, follows a new approach to enlargement policy, whereby it is the judicial chapters that are the first to be opened in accession negotiations. i think that, although croatia may no longer belong to this way of reporting, but indeed the experience of croatia in this regard confirms the justification of such a new approach due to the importance of judicial reform. in her report, ms macovei also confirmed a number of other issues, from political recruitment in public administration and public enterprises to the very roots of organised crime in the countries of south-east europe. it has linked, or established, the link between politics and crime since the wars, or the aggression of slobodan milosevic against slovenia, croatia, bosnia, kosovo, but of course there is also a link between politics and crime that goes deeper, even from the structures of the former yugoslav system. so it's important that these countries face this and really bring justice reform to life in all the candidates, that they can (interrupted).

mr. chairman, as a member of the committee on budgets i congratulate colleagues jensen and hohlmeier on the work that has contributed to today's report before us. i would stress how important it is that this budget, which is 6% smaller than the 2013 budget, should still send a positive message ahead of the final adoption of the multiannual financial framework and that, in the first year of the new financial perspective, programmes relating to young people, smes and all those that contribute to economic growth and employment should be put in place, because this is a key priority for the european union and what the european public and our citizens expect from us at this moment. i congratulate mrs hohlmeier for demonstrating the european parliament's rationality in reducing certain items in our budget for next year, and i am pleased that additional appropriations are provided for in chapter 5 to increase the appropriations for administration in view of croatia's membership of the european union, and in chapter 6 for compensation, which will enable croatia to continue to be a net beneficiary of the european budget in the first years of membership, and in particular we expect the effective use of funds from cohesion policy.
written. − since 2000, the eu has given the 'western balkans' unrestricted duty-free market access for almost all products. the decision was aimed at revitalizing the failing economies of the countries concerned and bringing them closer to european integration. serbia, bosnia and herzegovina, montenegro, macedonia and albania have signed the stabilisation and association agreement with the european union. the above mentioned countries benefit from the council regulation imposing exceptional trade measures in accordance with that agreement. the tariff quotas for wine are also regulated by that regulation in such a way that a total tariff quota of 50 000 hl for wine is made available to all users on a first-come, first-served basis. kosovo, due to the specificity of the situation surrounding the recognition of independence, has not signed a stabilisation and association agreement. this puts kosovo's wine exporters and economy in a discriminatory position compared to surrounding countries, as they do not have individual tariff quotas. the commission notes that the eu has not provided any evidence that the export of wine to kosovo is likely to have a significant impact on the level of export sales to the eu. the introduction of an annual tariff quota of 20 000 hectolitres for the export of wine to the eu corrects such an unfair situation. it is important to emphasise that this regulation does not affect the union wine sector as the scope of the benefit is not changed.
in writing. − the goal is to harmonise the collection, processing and transmission of statistical information on demography through a common framework and monitoring provisions to ensure that demographic developments are noted and can be used as indicators of the objective statistical picture of progress on improvements in the quality of life. local, regional and national demographic trends should be included in the data collected and regional data should be forwarded to eurostat. the report does not indicate how much data should be collected or how often. it proposes that ‘usual residence’ should mean the ‘place where a person normally spends the daily period of rest, regardless of temporary absencesʼ, meaning therefore the place where a person normally spends the daily period of rest, regardless of temporary absences for purposes of recreation, holidays, visits to friends and relatives, business, medical treatment or religious pilgrimage. to ensure the proper distribution of votes for qualified majority voting in the council, member states will provide eurostat with their data on total population within 8 months of the end of the reference year (2013-2027). starting in 2018, the commission will submit a report to parliament concerning the implementation of this regulation.
- madam president, the european union has pursued a highly constructive policy towards its near and far neighbours, but sometimes, when trade and other circumstances permit and require it, it has ignored some fundamental principles. this is called realpolitik, and for the people of the sahel, realpolitik means the neglect of some of the fundamental rights: the right to use and enjoy the wealth of one's own country, the right to preserve and enhance one's own culture and national heritage, the right to express, publicly and in protest, one's own opinion. so these fundamental rights of the sahel people and, ultimately, their right to self-determination through real politics are being pushed aside. all of these, along with other human rights, are things that we take for granted here in europe. we should never allow ourselves to be silent about these rights when it comes to other people, no matter how much fish there is in the sea to be caught and how much phosphate there is in those lands that can be exploited.
in writing. − the tools for good governance are transparency, participation and accountability; therefore the european union and its institutions must work together in achieving those goals. in order to fight huge global challenges like poverty, gender inequality and valuation of human rights, we need to shift from an international community approach to a ‘democratic ownershipʼ approach. democratic ownership is a system in which citizens are at the centre of development, through civil society organisations (csos) and local authorities (la). cooperation, communication and synergy between csos and las are fundamental aspects needed to achieve better local democracy and in order to reach more sustainable outcomes. furthermore, the eu should support the diversity of civil society and especially the involvement of marginalised groups. the european union is under an obligation to promote an effective system of knowledge-sharing and capacity-development techniques between las within new and old member states. croatia, as the newest member state in the eu, should absorb all the knowledge and experience from other member states in order to achieve a better civil society facing political, social and economic challenges.
in writing. − the europe 2020 programme has as one of its main objectives to reduce the school drop-out rate and thus to promote education at least up to the diploma. to quote the title of the commission communication of 20 november 2012, member states need to invest in skills to achieve better socio-economic outcomes. it is for these reasons that i voted in favour of the report adopted today, to promote quality education, to modernise schools and to make them as responsive as possible to the new market requirements. we must also work hard to provide students with the skills and knowledge required for the increasingly global labour market, giving greater space and importance to the study of foreign languages, which are a key factor in the future of our young people.
written. - europe is facing rising unemployment, especially among young people. in march this year, more than 23.5% of young people under 25 were unemployed, in some member states more than 50%. the labour market continues to be at odds with the education system. it is therefore important to plan the development of education in the long term and to adapt it to the needs and challenges brought about by technological developments and new occupations. curricula should be geared towards vocational training of pupils and students so that they have the necessary skills for employment at the end of formal education. education should be brought closer to the real working environment, and the training of teachers is a prerequisite for this. young people in croatia work for eur 1.5 an hour and after a year of this model, they rarely get a permanent job because it is more convenient for the employer to hire a new trainee who will be paid by the state. governments should stimulate employers by facilitating the employment of young people through lower taxes and contributions. money intended to encourage the employment of young people should be directed towards the development of the labour market and a favourable business environment which will thus create a favourable atmosphere for the creation of new jobs, and should in no way be social assistance.
written. - mr president, unemployment among young people under 25 in the european union is as high as 23.5%. in some countries, like croatia, that black number is twice as high. on the other hand, we have almost 2 million unfilled jobs in europe. in many of our member states we have a shortage of skilled workers with technical training. i therefore welcome this report, which sends a clear message about the need to reform the education system in many of our countries in order to strengthen europe's human potential in technical occupations and new technologies. this is particularly true for the ict sector. despite the crisis, ict jobs continue to be created and the digital economy is growing by up to 8% per year in developed countries. the report, particularly in article 4, recognises just such a need for a modern education system that will focus on information and communication technologies. investments in this sector, in my view, should not be taken into account in the calculation of the 3% maximum rate of fiscal deficit. these are the investments that will put our economies back on the path to growth. there is no increase in competitiveness and no new growth without investment in education and science.
written. − the economic crisis of recent years calls for a critical rethinking of the education systems of the eu member states. the high proportion of young and educated people in the unemployed structure is of particular concern. the above represents a burning problem that the republic of croatia is failing to address. i therefore welcome the adoption of the resolution because it correctly identifies the problems and proposes adequate measures to address them. it is necessary to monitor labour market trends closely because they change rapidly from year to year. adapting education to these changes is necessary to align the supply and demand curves of the workforce. the creation of an optimal education system must involve all relevant actors: educational and scientific institutions, employers, trade unions and relevant ministries. such a holistic approach enables the broader mission of education because it produces strong and socially engaged citizens. the resolution recognises the importance of learning foreign languages as the language barrier reduces mobility opportunities. mobility is important because it increases employment opportunities, as well as understanding of other cultures and intercultural relations. however, i believe that there is also a danger of 'brain drain' from less developed eu member states in terms of mobility. therefore, it is necessary to encourage people's mobility, but in such a way that people can take advantage of the positive experiences gained from studying and working abroad when they return to their home country.
madam president, the importance of judicial reform and the fight against corruption need not be explained to anyone. but these figures, which are in the report, 2.87 percent for judicial reform and 0.52 percent of all funds received in these countries, which are in question, for the fight against corruption, do not show that the importance of these moves is well appreciated. are we really sure what we want and how we want to do it? some countries co-finance their projects, like croatia did or turkey did, and that shows their attitude to these issues, some don't and nothing happens. there is also a lack of public regional rules of the game. in some countries, the corruption committee, the parliamentary corruption committee, is responsible for conducting investigations, in some countries it's not. in any case, money spent on this purpose is certainly well spent provided that the results are seen, the efficiency and transparency of the system and evidence that corruption cases are indeed resolved, detected, investigated, tried and convicted.
written. - i'm voting for the patient safety report because quality and safe healthcare is something that should be taken for granted today. despite this, 8-12% of hospitalized patients in the european union become victims of inadequate healthcare. the damage caused by poor health care should be seen primarily in terms of protecting the individual, but also as a social problem. for example, doctors often prescribe antibiotics without being sure that they are necessary. similarly, patients are dissatisfied if they leave the office without a prescription for antibiotics. in this way, they contribute to the development of antibiotic resistance, which is very dangerous in the long run. it is necessary to include patient safety in the education and training of healthcare professionals, and to work on action plans and strategies to control infections. health is a matter for the member states, but the institutions of the european union should encourage member states to take action that will result in greater patient safety and lead to european standardisation of healthcare.
written. - the processes of globalisation and rapid technological development create conditions in which most markets can be described as places of perfect competition. this means a large number of manufacturers offering almost identical products to a large number of customers. the commission considers that the measure is selective because it does not fulfil the conditions laid down in article 107 (1) tfeu. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any evidence that the aid granted to the beneficiaries was granted in breach of the rules on state aid. advertising, however, is an industry in which various forms of sophisticated fraud are possible. as the committee on the internal market and consumer protection mentioned in its report, business names, fraud involving internet domains, 'homeworking', and get-rich-quick schemes are the most prevalent forms of fraud. such dishonest acts cause immense damage to the union economy and particularly affect small and medium-sized enterprises, which should be the main drivers of the recovery of the eu economy. consumers are also affected by such fraud, as they are burdened by the increase in the final price of goods and services. in view of the above, i support the commission's efforts to eliminate such deviations. i welcome the idea of harmonising directives 2005/29/ec and 2006/114/ec so that member states do not implement these provisions differently. it is also necessary that member states establish contact points where injured business operators can report fraudulent activity.
mr. chairman, organized crime, corruption and money laundering are a big problem because they directly harm and traumatize the community. fighting this scourge is also a great challenge because it requires almost daily adaptation to the growing innovation of criminal groups. i fully support the parts of the report that recommend closer cooperation between member states, the exchange of data, knowledge and experience and better coordination of justice systems. i also agree with the rapporteur on the importance of confiscating illicitly acquired assets and of compensating the community through programmes and financed by confiscated funds. i believe that we need to facilitate this procedure in cases where the assets acquired illegally are located in several member states. but the reporter is not careful enough to recommend confiscation of property without a final court order. the available evidence alone, with the absence of a final judgment, in an ideal world might be sufficient, but that's not the world we live in. the risk of misuse of this model for political pressure and retaliation in those member states where the rule of law is still not ensured is real. i also believe that decriminalizing libel is a step backwards because such a practice would easily serve as a witch hunt. the commission has also proposed to extend the scope of the existing rules on the protection of personal data to include the protection of the rights of individuals and the protection of the rights of third parties.
mr president, when we talk about this subject it is good to ask what each member state can do to make this effort as effective as possible at the level of the european union, because we constantly emphasise what should and must be done at the level of the european union. the only way to fight organised crime is through the cooperation of each member state, the exchange and collection of information and the continuous updating of the system. criminal organizations are often organized as associations or corporations. sometimes they're even one step ahead of the rule of law and its instruments. organized crime, as we all know, is closely linked to illegal immigration. we recently had a debate on the tragedy of lampedusa when italian leaders asked for the support of the european union in addressing this problem of migration and human trafficking. it's not just italy's problem, it's all of ours, we all have to take greater responsibility in combating human trafficking and exploiting desperate people who want to escape poverty and misery. i hope that cooperation between police and judicial authorities, cooperation between national and european institutions and the establishment of eurosur will help to combat this scourge and all forms of organised crime.
mr. chairman, we know that organized crime, corruption and money laundering are activities that have long transcended national borders and have a global reach. the modalities of crime are constantly changing, the areas of action are different, from sports, economy, education to politics. the victim is always the same. he's an honest citizen. today, economic crime is responsible for the irreversible disappearance of vast amounts of money. evidence in criminal proceedings is difficult, requires a high level of knowledge and a cautious approach by law enforcement authorities, and often cases fail because of a lack of cooperation between authorities of different states. the commission should also be able to provide the necessary information to the member states in order to ensure that the investigation is carried out in accordance with the principles of good administration and fair competition. if we want to put an end to money laundering, tax havens, corruption and organised crime, it is essential to raise awareness of the harmful nature and social condemnation of economic crime, but also to continue working to improve judicial cooperation between states in criminal matters, because economic crime speaks all languages and knows no borders, and we are obliged to fight it in this way.
