on behalf of the ecr club. mr president, as a representative from croatia, i would like to take this opportunity to express my regret that the election of a member of the european court of auditors from my country has led to dissonant tones between parliament and the council. parliament's position on the candidacy of mr neven mates is clear, as reflected in the opinion of the committee on budgetary control of 27 may 2012 and the vote in plenary on 12 june of the same year. i understand the concern of the fellow mps who raised this question, although i must say it is legally irrelevant. the council shall only request parliament's opinion when it selects the members of the court of auditors and shall not be obliged in any way to agree with it or to act on it. the rules are clear, and whether they are good will need to be the subject of a much wider debate at union level. but despite the legal irrelevance of this question, i do not consider it to be pointless or futile because it brings into focus the credibility of a senior european official. in this particular case, the colleagues who asked the question rightly point out that mr mates has stated both orally and in writing that he will not go against the opinion of parliament but will withdraw his candidacy if he receives a negative opinion from this house. we have witnessed that he has not done what he promised and therefore his moral integrity is rightly called into question. the european union is a highly complex community and its survival and prosperity depend on many factors, including similar issues. it is becoming increasingly clear that the union's greatest enemies are not the eurorealists or the eurosceptics, but those who swear allegiance to the european community while acting irresponsibly and unprincipled, thus damaging the union's reputation and that of all its citizens. i would therefore urge the governments of all the member states, if they really want citizens to have confidence in the european institutions, to delegate only the best national experts to them. i call on the council to take parliament's advisory role for granted, because it marginalizes this upper house and the members of parliament as the only democratically elected officials of the union.
mr president, this case is actually a question about one of the deadly sins of parliamentarism - lying, but it has two aspects. one is personal and professional, and the other is political. on a personal note, the person who was elected during the committee hearing said she would resign, we know the whole story, if parliament didn't confirm her. parliament did not confirm it and the gentleman in question did not withdraw his candidacy. "my word is my bond", says the proverb. and how can you trust a man in such a high position if his word which he has given publicly, not in the company at the table but publicly in this house, if that word does not bind him. at a professional level, the committee and parliament have assessed that mr. cohn-bendit is not competent for the job, but those who proposed him and those who appointed him do not take into account the opinion of the experts from the committee on budgetary control which is composed of experts or the representatives of this parliament. what professional authority could this person have? and third, politically. some national governments consider the parliament a voting machine, as is the case with the croatian government, and their views are seen, often successfully, being pushed through the european institutions. fortunately, there are enough lawmakers here who are seriously disturbed by this and who will not condone such reckless recruitment and practically recklessness. i hope that in the future we will find the time and the means to prevent and stop such practices. again, this is not about legality, but it's definitely about the legitimacy of this man.

mr president, the appointment of mr neven mates was one of the first appointments on behalf of the republic of croatia to the institutions of the european union and the croatian government has successively proposed persons of a high level of expertise who fully meet the requirements of those institutions. it is indisputable that mr mates was a qualified candidate for the court of auditors with many years of international experience. it is also a fact that, despite the outcome of the hearing and the result of the vote, he decided to continue his candidacy, which was unanimously confirmed by all the member states of the european union in the council. regardless of the justification for dissatisfaction with its procedures, it is important to emphasise that the cont committee should first of all assess the competence of any candidate and not at any cost bring him into the space of a balance of power with the other institutions of the european union. mr mates, as far as i know, wants to maintain a dialogue with the committee and the european parliament. for example, when doubts about his election began to arise and a request for an explanation as to why he continued to run, mr mates sent a letter of his own initiative expressing his willingness to address the cont committee. that exchange of views is scheduled for november 4th of this year. so it seems to me that it would be more appropriate to have this discussion after this meeting, rather than before. finally, mr mates has been a constructive member of the court of auditors for more than three months and has not in any way compromised his expertise and knowledge.
mr president, last week in a study published in brussels on inactive or unemployed migrants within the european union who are on welfare in other countries we could see that such inactive eu migrants are less than 1%, and even in some countries like the uk where this issue has clearly become a matter of political debate the number of such eu migrants is only 2.6% of the total number of unemployed. all the figures show that respect for the fundamental human right to freedom of movement within the european union does not jeopardise the social welfare systems of the member states. so the main reason for the mobility of european citizens is not to get social assistance in the richer member states of the european union, but the main reason is to get a job in those member states. mr president, the welfare state as we have known it in europe is in crisis, but it is not the fault of migration within the european union, on the contrary, intra-eu mobility and even a smart immigration policy can be part of the solution for the european model that is in crisis because of the delay in structural reforms, because of the relativization of fundamental social values and, of course, as a consequence of this, because of the extremely bad demographic picture of europe. these are key issues that we need to take more care of and discuss without taboos. populist attacks on eu migrants and on immigrants actually take us away from a real debate on structural reforms, take us away from a stronger, for example, family policy in europe. these are the debates we need to open in this parliament, these are the solutions we need to get europe on the road to a new wave of economic growth, and, moreover, these are the solutions we need to restore hope to europe and to younger generations.
mr president, let me focus here on the part concerning the mobility of eu citizens in search of employment. as a representative of this institution, i believe that at this level, as a legislative and political level, we need to be realistic about facing certain problems and finding the best way to initiate their solution. we are all very familiar with the european union's policy on the mobility of its citizens, but the practice of certain member states is in fact in some cases contradictory to that policy and restricts, for example, the very principle of the movement of persons for the purpose of employment. the commission has also been asked to provide information on the implementation of the new rules on the right to work and the right to strike. i would point out that this is one of the examples of a contradiction, regardless of its legitimate basis or economic justification, which is due to the economic crisis but which in reality, by its outcome, actually constitutes a breach of one of the fundamental principles on which the union is founded. i think we should think about this and similar questions.
mr president, i agree with commissioner andor when he says that mobility means unemployment. so people don't move to other countries because they're living well in their own country, but because they need a job and they're not a burden on the welfare system. freedom of movement is very important, one of the fundamental freedoms. i would ask one question. how come erasmus is not a problem, but employment migration is? so it's about different attitudes of different countries. countries are not always consistent in their policies and i welcome a situation where the commission will help to use european funds to local and regional authorities to help with this alignment of the social system. so it is important that there is coordination of social systems in all the member states and i believe that you will succeed in this your intention, otherwise we will be talking about different criteria and two different archons.
written. - reforms of social assistance and pension systems are the most common recommendations to member states under the european semester. it is important to implement them in a way that does not compromise the fundamental principles of the european union. the free movement of people is one of the four fundamental freedoms of the european union. recent studies show that the number one reason for intra-eu migration is work, which is not surprising given the current crisis. studies show that the young population is the one that migrates more and faster, while it is the working population that migrates for employment and work to maintain the social systems of the countries in which they live. social benefits and pensions account for the largest part of the member states' expenditure side of the budget. over 50% of the budget is allocated to pensions and social benefits in health, while 7.7% goes to children and families. given that retired people migrate more slowly, it is clear that reforming pension and social systems at eu level to restrict people's movement would put a burden on young people in particular and contribute to the crisis in the long term. for all these reasons, i am opposed to restrictions on the free movement of persons, which is inextricably linked to the free movement of goods, capital and services. to restrict one freedom would mean restricting others, and then the development of the crisis is unquestionable.
madam president, i congratulate the rapporteur who can certainly be pleased that some expectations have been debunked that there will be above-average tensions in eastern and south-eastern europe in 2013. however, it seems that these bad predictions are not coming true. after the breakup of yugoslavia, some 4,000 km of new state borders appeared on the political map of europe, but today the area of southeastern europe is gradually being integrated into the most important political project in all of european history. stabilisation, cooperation and, in the long term, accession should be desirable for the eu's eastern neighbours. it is clear that there is no more pressure from within the european union to enlarge than there was before 2004 and 2007. now there is much more pressure from outside the european union. economic migration across the mediterranean, especially after the fall of dictatorial regimes in north africa. but there are positive political pressures from the western balkans, just as there are negative ones from russia, which need to be countered with convincing political and economic instruments. it is now clear that the european union will not repeat the ambiguous experiences it has gained during the multiple enlargement cycles, but the union must remain a community open to new members, partners and initiatives. the history of the union is the history of its enlargement. the success of the european project depends on whether the union will be a successful medium of democratic change in the future in east, south and south-east europe, as it has been in western europe for the last 60 years.
madam president, i think that the success of the european union's neighbourhood policy is a kind of indicator of the attractiveness of the european political project and that is why i think that our colleagues panzeri and saryusz-wolski have well detected the key elements of the situation both within the eastern partnership and within our southern neighbourhood. i think it's extremely important, commissioner füle, that our partners also experience these two projects as jointly owned. i know that this ownership has helped us a lot in the case of croatia in the context of enlargement, because it will also be important for the success of the summit in vilnius, where ukraine is in my view the key to success. that is why i am pleased that there is progress after the intensive work of former presidents cox and kwaśniewski to resolve the preconditions for signing the association agreement. 12 billion euros in aid, 230 billion euros in trade, 3.2 million visas with our partners in the south and east are proof that this policy must be the priority of the european union's foreign policy and that is why i support your efforts, and i hope that when the time comes we will be able to revitalize the union for the mediterranean in the right way.
written. - mr president, i would like to thank first of all the european commission, and then in particular mr jacek saryusz-wolska and mr piero antonio panzeri, for the excellent work done so far and the many useful proposals which are directing us towards strengthening our relations with the eastern partnership countries. i consider it our duty and obligation to continue to provide the countries belonging to the said partnership with maximum support on their path towards full democratisation, the rule of law, and the development of a free and open civil society whose full flowering can only be possible in a dynamic and mature democratic atmosphere. in the context of the eastern partnership summit in vilnius at the end of november, i would like to stress that we must give our full support to our friendly countries in the eastern neighbourhood in a practical way, especially as we all know very well the position russia has taken on our efforts. our message to russia and the world should be clear: the european union is always for peace, economic prosperity and democratic development. as i have said before, we are helping the eastern partnership countries in a practical way: facilitating trade with them, helping them on their way to energy independence, liberalizing visa arrangements and allowing the widest possible freedom of movement for citizens of these countries. it's the key to success. let us be as actively involved as possible in their development and progress. - thank you. - thank you.
mr president, coming from a country where islands and coastal towns outside the tourist season are largely dependent on fishing, i consider this issue to be of the utmost importance for my country and the union in general. i am a resident of one of the croatian islands myself, and i want to emphasize how deeply i believe in protecting the sea, the marine resources and the coastal belt from over-exploitation and pollution. however, i believe that we must find a compromise between the ecological and economic components when considering aquaculture. it is a very important activity for the survival of the coastal area, which in croatia is systematically declining due to economic underdevelopment and depopulation. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a new regulation on the protection of the environment and the protection of the environment from harmful organisms. the commission notes that the amendment to the cfp was adopted by the council on 14 december 2012 and that the commission has not yet adopted the amendment to the cfp.
mr president, i would first of all like to congratulate the rapporteur and many other colleagues on the preparation of this important and complex document, which contains a whole series of proposals useful to fisheries. however, i also want to draw attention to provisions that directly harm the fisheries sector in croatia, which directly employs 14,000 people, while the sector as a whole employs 25,000 people. the commission notes that the aid granted to the beneficiary is limited to the minimum necessary to ensure that the aid is limited to the minimum necessary to ensure that the beneficiary is able to meet its obligations under the scheme. the commission considers that the proposed measures are in line with the principles of the common fisheries policy and the principles of the common fisheries policy. the protection of the environment in the service of preventing overfishing is also an argument worth considering, but it is precisely controlled aquaculture and mariculture that is one way of preventing overfishing without reducing fish consumption. the next observation concerns the rearing of alot species. some of the so-called exotic species have long been domesticated, so that the california trout, for example, is the main one throughout europe, and in croatia it is the only one that is farmed. i support the view that this issue should be addressed by environmental regulations, not by withholding aid.
(without the microphone) i support the common fisheries policy and, of course, the principles of sustainable fisheries which you have recently adopted. the fact is, this is taxpayer money that we have to manage responsibly, but, also, this fund has to function. the fisheries sector must be competitive and that is why we should not, in my opinion, build new vessels with this money. as for croatia, where i come from, also from the croatian coast, from the adriatic sea, i want to say that i support aquaculture and i do not agree with this provision that talks about protected areas. so we have to talk about controlled aquaculture because it's about stable employment, it's about safe food, it's about good environmental management, it's about innovation, and all of these are pillars of an aquaculture industry that is capable of growing and that can affect the fish deficit. so, i ask that we think about this tomorrow before we vote.
mr president, i am well aware of the inconsistency of the union's foreign and security policy, because we have not moved on this issue since the serbian aggression against croatia and bosnia and herzegovina. and then europe was timid and indecisive in its political and external reaction, and its military reaction, and it is the same today. the rapporteur rightly points out that we must protect our citizens and their interests around the world. and when it comes to bosnia and herzegovina, we have done little or nothing to protect the political rights of the croats of bosnia and herzegovina, who are also citizens of the republic of croatia, and therefore citizens of the european union. obstruction of the return of croatian refugees to republika srpska and anti-croatian electoral engineering in the federation of bosnia and herzegovina are good indicators of what croats are experiencing in their own country. if we want to pass the credibility test and be a global political actor, it's time to start cleaning up our european backyard. i therefore ask the institutions and political bodies of the european union to finally take concrete steps to ensure the equality of croats in bosnia and herzegovina.
mr. president, thank you very much for your understanding and for this opportunity to speak a little earlier than scheduled. i am one of those who are aware that the european union's common foreign and security policy is the policy which has undergone the most qualitative and powerful evolution in the last 20 years or so. this is particularly clear to those of us who come from croatia and have witnessed the limitations of both the institutional, financial and legal aspects of european foreign policy since the early 1990s. based on the lessons learned from that time, we now have mechanisms, budgets and policies, and mrs ashton elaborated very well in her speech on how important the european external action service, the european defence agency, is today. i therefore think that the report by our colleague and chairman of the committee, mr brok, is very good in terms of identifying the key strategic objectives of european foreign policy, which are coherence, effectiveness, effective multilateralism and, above all, the european union's global positioning in relation to the key crisis hotspots in the world where we must have a role that is always clear and consistent. i also think we need to address the issue of certain parallels between the activities of the european union and some of our larger members, particularly in the g20, in the quinta, in the contact group. these are the elements that must complement the overall efforts of the european union to strengthen its external policy.
mr president, i welcome the report by elmar brok, which sets out a clear framework for promoting both the values and the interests of the european union on the global stage. when the report talks about the leadership and coherence of the european union's foreign policy, one prominent point is the role of the european union in south-east europe. and the role of the high commissioner in reaching an agreement between serbia and kosovo is rightly highlighted. so i'm going to focus on this region, and i'm going to emphasize that there's unfinished business here, and that our policy towards this corner of the european continent is going to be the measure of the success of the eu's foreign policy, because how are we going to talk about the european union as a global player if we can't show leadership in policy towards an area that is surrounded by european union members. i therefore welcome the efforts of both the european commission and my colleague from the european parliament, especially in relation to bosnia and herzegovina. i'm singling out bosnia and herzegovina in particular because it's the biggest challenge at the moment to achieving our goals of stability and prosperity in southeastern europe. commissioner füle's efforts to reach an agreement in bih and the approach he has chosen this time around give hope that it has finally been recognised that separatism and centralism are two equally dangerous extremes for the stability and survival of this multinational state. however, i would urge that further work be done on the coherence of member states' actions in this area, which will certainly reflect on the overall strength and credibility of european policy at the global level.
mr. chairman, many climate change panels point to the alarming escalation of the impacts of climate change, but also to the fact that preventing climate chaos is still possible. in the arctic and elsewhere in the world, climate change is more than obvious. as the permafrost thaws, oil companies are moving north in search of fuel to cause the same ice to melt. the arctic 30 acted out of a deep conviction that such a business was a high risk because an oil spill would have catastrophic consequences for the entire region. this is a manifesto against the dangerous attempt to industrialize this sensitive area. we are witnessing the vigorous efforts and struggles of greenpeace activists and on many other issues so that the real protection of rights and property would not be reduced to a mere formality and a dead letter on paper. if we do not publicly warn about this controversial practice of russia and on this extremely important issue of the legitimate grounds for the detention of activists, tomorrow the agenda will include activists from other organizations, for example, those who advocate and protect human rights.
madam president, it is difficult to say all i have to say on this subject in two minutes. the question is how can we be both environmentalists and economic prosperity creators? it just seems uncompromising, especially today in a time of great crisis when we think that protecting the environment should not be a priority. however, that is short-term thinking. there is certainly a need to reconcile environmental protection, sustainable development and economics and economic growth. so that's our key mission. however, if we do not put in place a legal framework which will impose on the member states, and not just the member states, but also on those other countries, third countries, as we like to call them, then only if we have reliable partners will we be able to implement the 7th environment action plan. but it's not just third countries, it's not just the european union, it's also individuals, it's also citizens. and what better moment than 2013, which has been declared the european year of citizens? so, we need to educate citizens, we need to maximize citizen engagement on a daily basis. if they're not involved in all of this, there's no point in this program. however, i would like to congratulate both mr potočnik, who is the commissioner, and the commission, as well as mr franz, who is the rapporteur, on the excellent work they have done, but we must send a strong political message. and because i'm from croatia, i want to say that it's the country that's at the top of the world in terms of drinking water, so i would like my grandchildren in 2050 to be able to drink tap water. and i will repeat once again today that old indian saying: "this land is not inherited from our ancestors, it is borrowed from our children, our grandchildren and our heirs". and we need to be aware of that at all times.
madam president, i would like to welcome this programme which has one universal objective: a better quality of life for all the citizens of the european union and our planet. the main priorities highlighted in this programme, such as reducing co2 emissions, protecting and providing drinking water, developing bioenergy and renewable energy sources, developing environmentally friendly infrastructure, etc., and their successful implementation are of long-term interest to all eu member states. the main policy-makers aiming to ensure sustainable development and a green economy should therefore be those actors who assume their responsibility on the basis of the principle of subsidiarity. in this case, we need to highlight the positive examples of local communities successfully implementing green economic policies, provide them with support, and ensure that other communities have the best possible conditions for such development. as some global economies see through the short-term impact of such policies on their profits, it is important to keep stressing that long-term growth in gross domestic product and the progress of our society in general can only be ensured through the sustainable protection of our living space. it used to be generally accepted that any economic growth was necessarily bound to and only possible with an increase in material and energy consumption, and that such growth also had very unpleasant consequences, commensurate with the increase in emissions and pollution of all kinds. fortunately, that trend is changing. by aligning with the eu's current environmental standards, generations of outdated, dirty and energy-inefficient technologies are being abandoned in favour of new solutions that will ensure not only an environmentally sustainable planet but also its economic progress in the long term.
vice-president of the commission. − mr president, thank you for this debate. by the way, this is the first time that i have had the experience in my nine years in office in brussels/strasbourg of being blamed for being too speedy. mostly the reactions of our citizens are ‘my goodness, they believe in eternal life in brussels/strasbourg!’ having said that, and being aware that there is much that we have in common, one issue i would like to underline is that i have been appointed for five years, provided that i follow the rules and behave properly. that means that i am paid to fulfil my responsibilities, and i can assure you that it is an appointment for five years and that i will be active until my last day in office. so to those who are saying that it is nearly time to think about what is next, i would say that i myself am not thinking of my future after this period. i am just concentrating on what has to be done – and i repeat – up to my last day in office. i am glad that most of you share the view that bringing back economic growth is at stake. europe needs competition and competitive high-speed broadband markets. our proposal addresses new challenges in a fast-changing world. we are talking about the global scene: there are no more ring-fenced national states; there is no more ring-fenced europe. this is a global stage. therefore, it is absolutely essential to act quickly, as our competitors will not wait until we are ready. if we want europe to have the economy and the technology to thrive, we cannot wait around. our proposal is pragmatic, but it is focused. it is focused on clear objectives: removing the most important barriers to the single market so that the market can evolve. we have acted fast to respond to a changing market and to changing technology so that the current framework continues to meet its objectives. the impact assessment sets out – and i was listening to the remarks made about the impact assessment from both sides – our evidence and analysis on how we are achieving that. yes, the speed with which we have had to act has created challenges for my people, for the commission and for all those involved, and we are communicating intensively and keeping an eye on all those who are interested in this matter. i am counting my blessings that so many people and organisations are interested in what is at stake. but to those who complain about lack of consultation, i would say: come on! we have been speaking to stakeholders for years: market players, national regulators, berec, investors, members of parliament, you name it – on issues ranging from how to stimulate investment to net neutrality. i promise and guarantee that we are listening to those who are discussing these issues. this does not mean that we are following 100 % what they are in favour of. we listened and we responded, but let us not use procedure as an excuse for inaction, and i am repeating myself here. that is exactly what we are so often blamed for: using procedures as an excuse. we cannot afford it, and i am always saying to those i am in discussions with that i want to be open and honest to those youngsters who do not have a job and who have no prospect of a job in the coming years. i do not want to tell them: ‘well, we need to have more time for procedures; well, we need more discussions’, and so on. they are only interested in a real effort to do whatever would give them the prospect of a job. we cannot afford to do it differently. i would like to reassure you that national regulatory agencies will remain fully responsible for fact-finding, analysing markets and identifying remedies nationally. mr president, i can claim to have a bit of experience in national policies as well, and i do not buy it when people are not telling the truth. i want to argue, but not on fake arguments, and not on issues that are not correct, for then we will never come to a decent, final result. so i would like to reassure all of you that that the national regulatory agencies will remain fully responsible for the issues i have mentioned. the commission proposes a new power concerning the legal compatibility of remedies imposed on european operators before those remedies are implemented. but that power is subject to safeguards, guaranteeing legal predictability and certainty. these proposals foster competition and consumer choice. they will end the problems with roaming charges in europe once and for all. i am ready to fight for that. i think citizens deserve a decent bill, not one based on historical situations in europe. we have a single market and we should use it, using a market-based approach that helps operators to internalise their costs, thereby protecting citizens from domestic price increases. in an open and competitive single market with fewer barriers and greater economies of scale, companies will only succeed by offering the best deals at the best prices. and that is positive – it is positive for every european. in the longer run, some consolidation might be a consequence – it is not up to us. i am not the one sitting in the business chair; i am not making their business models. that is up to them, because operators will see different opportunities, plans and strategies in the single market. they have to seize that opportunity. but make no mistake: competition law continues to apply, and if someone is there to defend competition law, it is me. european and national competition authorities continue to keep a close eye on this, and our proposal will bring many benefits to european citizens and businesses. let me mention just one, and then i shall talk about jobs. the internet creates five jobs for every two jobs lost. in fact, a single market in telecoms will boost gdp by up to eur 110 billion. that is exactly what i was talking about. if we do not speed up, what is the answer? if we are aware that this could be the real result of our actions, what is our answer to those who are looking forward? is it that we are failing in our promise to do our utmost to restore the economy and create a climate in which creating jobs is at stake? a growth boost would more than offset the negative impacts. remember that the digital sector is crying out for talented workers, but it relies on fast broadband. i know that we share many common objectives for a digital single market, with fast broadband underpinning a more competitive europe. we need to work together, and i am grateful to those who have mentioned that. i am grateful for the clear statement that ms del castillo vera foreseen will include assessing the economic and societal impact of various scenarios of universal service obligations and the potential inclusion of broadband in its scope. this is what is at stake, and the honourable member matias mentioned it: to get every european digital. this was an issue when i first took responsibility for this portfolio. if we are to ensure that every european goes digital and is aware of the positive advantages of the internet, then we need to create trust and security, otherwise people will not be convinced that that is the way they should deal with it. i am also talking about an ageing population. i am not only talking about the youngsters: they are well aware of what is at stake for them in the development of the internet. we badly need the ageing written. - in view of the recent changes in the legal framework concerning the pricing of telecommunications services in other countries, i would like to express my satisfaction with the reduction achieved in the price of roaming services. the commission notes that the price changes made the services more economically acceptable and accessible to the end-user. however, i would also stress that there is still a lot of room for improvement and better regulation of the regulatory framework for electronic communications services, particularly in the part concerning the development of the single market in telecommunications. i would also like to stress the importance of applying the principle of net neutrality. this principle, which requires operators not to block certain applications from users, is unfortunately not applicable. the commission considers that the principle of non-discrimination in the provision of services to end-users is not a relevant criterion for the application of the state aid rules. the commission considers that the aid is compatible with the internal market within the meaning of article 107 (3) (c) of the treaty. the very meaning of the eu in its original and fundamental sense is to encourage the dismantling of borders that are unnecessary, economically acceptable only to a few and harmful to the majority of citizens. this will be a further step in increasing consumer rights.
mr president, i would like to start by thanking mrs lulling for raising this issue after the 2008 reform because i think it's important to talk about this sector. the commission has also made good proposals in the framework of the common agricultural policy. however, since i come from croatia, where wine production and viticulture is one of the interesting, or more interesting, branches of agriculture. there is a long and rich tradition of wine production, and somewhere, in some parts of croatia, it is the only possible branch of agriculture. wine production can be characterised as a production which tends to develop in quality. and our goals are in line with european goals. it is about increasing the quality of wine production, increasing the competitiveness of producers, but also modernising winegrowing techniques through new investments in winery infrastructure and processing capacity. it also concerns increasing the promotion and sale of wines on third country markets. but it's not easy being competitive here. the reasons are the small size of the estate, so the big problem is small vineyards, especially in dalmatia, and a large number of small producers. it is therefore very important to ensure the survival and development of rural areas and thereby to increase the quality of life. i would also like to mention the situation during the negotiations between croatia and the european union regarding our accession. it was difficult, but important, to protect individual species, to protect geographical origin and to protect originality. so we had a situation where we came into a conflict between two names, between our dessert wine called lattes and an italian wine, which is a sparkling wine called prosecco. i believe that this problem has now been resolved, but there have been some other situations like this, so i want to point that out and ask that we maybe have another bilateral discussion about it.
mr president, as emphasised in the question, the wine sector is constantly undergoing reform and concern was expressed about new measures being planned without having previously determined the effects of the existing measures. among many other activities, croatian winegrowers naturally have high expectations of more free access to the single market as they join the european union. this includes grape variety producers, which account for almost a third of croatian wine production. in the last five to six years, producers have invested around eur 20 000 per hectare and significantly modernised the plants with large investments to adapt to the european union. the commission also found that the union industry had a significant market share in the union market. i stress that i am not against reforms, but we must first think of the people who need to implement them in a way that makes their work easier and simpler.
- mr president, as a resident of the wine-growing region, i am concerned about the future of croatian wine-growing and viticulture within the european union. the case of croatian companies prošek and teran, which faced a potential sales ban on croatia's entry into the european union, best illustrates the delicacy of the current situation. i believe that we should not abandon the production model based on geographical origin and i hope that the commission does not intend to promote the production of wines without geographical origin. in fact, the strong branding of top quality wines precisely according to their geographical origin is the starting point of the wine strategy for many small countries such as croatia. i also believe that the excessive corrections and rapid reforms of the common organisation of the market in wine are counterproductive because they do not allow us to see the results and consequences of previous reforms, given that the vine is a perennial plant, which makes the whole wine sector rather resistant to rapid change. the president agreed to answer the question by raising a blue card under rule 149 (8) of the rules of procedure.

member of the commission. - mr president, i would like to thank astrid lulling and paolo de castro again for giving us the opportunity to debate this question. i shall try to answer the various questions as to the points that have been raised. mr. dantin wants to know when the quality signs will be promoted. we have provided for this in the proposal for a regulation which the commission will shortly adopt for promotion on the internal and external markets and indeed our intention was to be able to provide financial support for strong communication on the internal market on the whole of agri-food quality policy, including quality signs. some of you have mentioned plantation rights. i just want to remind you that these plantation rights were included in the 2008 reform, which was adopted by the council of ministers. but despite this and beyond, in the framework of this 2013 reform, we have returned to this issue, also taking into account the evaluation report of the 2008 reform. this report was submitted in 2012. some of you are saying that maybe the timeframe was too short to see the effects. some positive effects have already been observed, but it has also been possible to correct elements that were being questioned by some, including the sector. as regards trade agreements and wine, i want to assure you that, as i have done so far and as i intend to do until the end of my term and my work in the commission, in every trade agreement i defend geographical indications as a whole as a sine qua non of such trade agreements. we will no doubt have the opportunity to talk about the success we have just had with canada, including in the wine and spirits sector, because we not only protect our geographical indications, but we have also clarified certain conditions for the sale of these protected products on the canadian market. we're going to do the same with the united states. we also have agreements in sight with japan and india. in all these markets, geographical indications will be a key point of discussion, and we are also negotiating with china a bilateral agreement on the recognition and protection of geographical indications in the chinese market, and wine will be the main beneficiary. mr meyer, on the defence of small and mediumsized enterprises and cooperatives (he is no longer here, i believe, but i will reply anyway): if you are watching, mr meyer - i hope you are listening to us where you are - the reform of the common agricultural policy which is in the final stages of adoption, you will probably find many measures which support small producers and all forms of collective organisations, including cooperatives. mrs klaß, as regards the delegated acts for the wine sector, that is to say for planting authorizations: in our calendar we have planned for the year 2016, since it is from 2016 that this new system will come into force. however, in the course of 2014 the commission will have a lot of urgent matters to deal with very quickly, which will have to be implemented from 2015. we must therefore concentrate, with the human resources we have in dg agri, on what is really a priority. i would also like to remind you that we in dg agri are in the midst of a process in which there will be a lot of work over the next three years, with the implementation of the reform of the common agricultural policy and the gradual reduction of human resources, because of the commitment made by the commission, including before parliament, to tighten up its ranks in order to save money. that is why we need to plan and because the planting authorisation systems will come into force in 2016, we have committed ourselves to having these delegated acts before 2016, that is, before these measures come into force. mrs zuber, on planting authorisations and aocs: i can assure you that aocs will be protected under this new system of planting authorisations, as aocs still have a separate regime for planting rights and the extension of these planted areas. mrs garcía pérez, on overproduction: the national programmes for the wine sector in each member state, including spain, still include, following the 2008 reform, measures that in principle allow for this type of situation to be managed. however, spain will have to ensure a balance between the various measures it intends to finance to meet its needs in the context of this national programme for the wine sector. but beyond that, spain has already made a great effort and more efforts are still needed to ensure that all wines without quality signs can be oriented towards a quality policy, so that they are better identified on the market and to avoid the overproduction of low quality and low price wines. several members of the croatian parliament have raised this issue of prosek. i can understand your political position in parliament, but rules are rules. in the same way that in croatia you need to protect certain geographical indications which are your geographical indications, which are names, which are linked to your tradition, to your history, the other member states have the right to protect their names and geographical indications. the principle applies to everyone and there can be no exceptions. in the event of a dispute, as between prosecco and prosek, the commission always proposes to the states and producers who are in dispute over a name conciliation periods to discuss them at table. but once we have concluded the matter, on the basis of rules which are transparent rules, we must not, in my view, try to politically revive a subject, to give false hopes about things which are very clear on the european market. i hope that croatia will make full use of the instruments now at its disposal, including financial instruments, to promote its products on the large european market of over 500 million consumers. i think that is the way to help croatian producers, based on rules which are very clear and which protect all producers, including croatian producers, but with honesty and without selling them illusions. on the subject of grape wines, i would like to say to mr andriessen and the other person who raised the subject that we do not intend to return to this subject. we believe that the rules we have at present for the definitions of the different types of wine are sufficient to cover the problem at european level. we are not planning to go back to that kind of subject. i want to thank you again for this debate. as you mentioned, yes, we're gonna screw peace in the sector, as long as the sector is peaceful. but when there are problems in the sector, we must intervene to solve those problems and so that the sector can continue to develop. because i'm sure that in a crisis situation, the sector doesn't ask us to leave it alone, but to intervene. and we need to have the tools to be able to intervene when we need to.
written. - eu wine production is declining as in the last year only three member states have recorded an increase in production while all the others have recorded a decline, which is a clear signal that changes in the wine sector are needed. the commission has also received comments from the croatian authorities, which have not provided any evidence that the measures in question are in line with the union's competition rules. the expansion of the market has led to significantly increased competition for croatian winegrowers. in addition to the fierce competition, a significant problem faced by croatian winemakers is the systems of protection of originality and regional origin. the new eu member states have also introduced a new wine tradition, but in many cases it overlaps with existing wine brands. existing protection systems therefore need to be adapted. in croatia, 62% of the total production is wasted on quality wines. add to this another 5.5% of premium wines, and it is clear that the quality of croatian wines is at a high level. therefore, a new demarcation of protection is necessary to take into account the existence of species with a similar name but with completely different oenological qualities, thus allowing consumers to consume a wider range of quality wines with a clearer geographical origin. the european union must, above all, protect the quality of wine and its distribution, while respecting the cultural and oenological specificities as well as the similarity between the names of wine varieties of all the member states.

written. − the budget of the european union is an important instrument for implementing common policies. the two most financially viable are the agricultural and cohesion policies, which account for over 70% of the eu budget. these two policies are of particular importance for the republic of croatia, which has been allocated over eur 8,5 billion from the cohesion policy and an additional eur 3,3 billion from the agricultural policy in the financial envelope for the period 2014-2020. the 2014 budget allows for continuity in the implementation of commitments from the 2007-2013 financial perspective and as much as 52% of the payments planned for 2014 relate to the earlier perspective. the 2014 budget is 6% lower than the 2013 budget in the area of possible commitments and the reduction in relation to the 2013 payment plan is 6,6%. the percentage reduction in cohesion policy allocations was the smallest, which is important for all member states whose gdp is below 75% of the average gdp of the member states, and for the republic of croatia as well. the largest reduction is in the competitiveness programme items, with horizon being reduced by 13,6%, cosme by 20,6% and erasmus+ by 0,8%. the erasmus+ programme aims to meet important objectives related to education and youth mobility and it is positive that the amounts allocated to this programme have not been significantly reduced.

written. - the state of the seabed and the fishery on the eastern croatian side of the adriatic is by all indications better than on its western side. new regulations to maintain or correct such a situation were welcomed. however, some provisions and amendments to the proposal of the european maritime and fisheries fund would have direct negative consequences on the marine and freshwater aquaculture sector in croatia and therefore cannot be supported. aquaculture is one way to meet the demand for fish without increasing catches and further depleting the fishery. the sector is experiencing steady growth and employs an increasing number of people and contributes significantly to croatian and european exports. the definition of intensive aquaculture and exotic species is ambiguous and unclear and may leave a large part of croatian aquaculture farms without support from the european maritime and fisheries fund. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any evidence that the measures in question were necessary to restore the viability of the beneficiary.

mr president, i support the rapporteur's report on the european semester. the european semester is an important mechanism for coordinating economic policies between member states and for directing economic policies towards strategic objectives and priorities set at the level of the european union. as part of the european semester, the republic of croatia will also need to undertake important structural reforms from 2014 to improve its economy and also achieve higher rates of economic growth, which are the benefits of participating in the european semester. all member states, in aligning their objectives with the implementation of structural reforms, can in fact learn from each other and this is one of the positive aspects of the semester, in essence the exchange of experiences and joint work in a way that best practice examples are applied.

written. − the european semester is a mechanism for initiating structural reforms in the member states, and at union level it is a mechanism for coordinating and directing the economic policies of the member states towards the objectives defined at eu level through strategic documents. i support the recommendations made to member states aimed at growth and development, tackling unemployment, strengthening competitiveness and improving the business climate. the implementation of the proposed measures and recommendations, the implementation of structural reforms and the results achieved should be monitored in more detail. examples of best practice should be guidelines for other countries on what to do and how to do it. the most important reforms relate to public administration, which needs to be simplified and administrative procedures reduced, and to introduce models for monitoring performance through target setting and measurement of results. pension, social, labour market and entrepreneurship reforms are important areas for exchanging knowledge and good practices. in this way, the european semester also becomes a mechanism for mutual assistance between member states. the republic of croatia shall be bound by the european semester mechanism from the beginning of 2014. participation will be accompanied by the active implementation of the recommendations made by the eu council, the adoption of realistic economic programmes and a sustainable budget that shows the effects of the measures implemented and the accompanying gradual reduction of deficit and debt within the framework set.
mr president, i voted in favour of the joint motion for a resolution, although i think it emphasises too much the humanitarian and not enough the criminal dimension of illegal migration in the mediterranean. the tragedy of lampedusa has left us all in a state of shock, but despite the unfortunate circumstances, the union cannot allow our policies to be governed solely by sadness and resentment. the mediterranean member states have had major problems with illegal immigration for years and this problem will not disappear because of an emotional approach but solely as a result of the implementation of an effective solution. i therefore welcome above all the part of the resolution which encourages the sanctioning of people smugglers, but even if we succeed in reducing the number of smugglers by sanctions, the problem will remain unresolved without close cooperation with the countries from which illegal immigrants come and increased control of the financial resources allocated by the union to help these countries. we must stop illegal migration at its source, not at its destination.
written. - i would like to thank the colleagues who participated in the preparation of this resolution. recent tragic events serve as a reminder that the current regulation of immigration and asylum in the european union is not adequately regulated. the increase in the number of immigrants and asylum seekers needs to be seen in a broader economic, social and political context, and concern for their safety and fundamental human rights must be the underlying reason why existing regulations and practices need to be organised differently. as the resolution rightly warns, changes are also needed to respect one of the eu's fundamental principles the principle of solidarity with a view to increasing the degree of coherence. in addition to solidarity, member states should respect the principle of legal and moral responsibility to help migrants at sea. a comprehensive approach is needed, in which the roles of all stakeholders are clearer and member states cooperate more closely with each other, with the european institutions and with third countries. in addition to reorganisation, existing regulations and practices need to be supplemented. in this regard, i strongly support the recent announcements of reinforced control activities in the mediterranean under the leadership of frontex as a first step towards a more integrated policy of the european union and the prevention of future lampedusa-like tragedies. in the context of all of the above, i would like to invite the competent institutions to take the content of the resolution into account when discussing the proposed amendments.

mr president, as a member of the committee on budgets and the conciliation delegation, i congratulate all the participants on the compromise reached, which i consider to be a good step forward in terms of a single budget, which is 6% smaller than last year's, which opens up the multiannual financial framework in a qualitative way for the next seven years, especially when it comes to the priority areas, namely agricultural payments, which are very much emphasised in this budget, and the funds for the cohesion policy. i also believe that the funds earmarked for combating unemployment, especially among young people, are very important and that it is therefore good that they have been put earlier in the overall multiannual perspective. i also think it is important that there is a special budget line in this budget for the danube strategy, and this is also important for the easternmost part of croatia, the croatian danube, for vukovar and the port of vukovar, for the development of this part of croatia, which suffered a lot during the war, and i am also glad that we have set aside special funds for frontex and for the humanitarian disasters that have hit the eu in the last few years and in recent days.

in writing. − under the 2014-2020 multiannual financial framework (mff), the eu institutions agree to work together with the objectives of cost saving and better synergies at national and european levels in order to improve the effectiveness of public spending in matters subject to eu action. reducing costs in some sectors will ensure more resources for areas such as research and innovation, external relations and energy, transport and infrastructure projects. cost saving is the only proper and possible way of increasing revenue and establishing sustainable economic development in the member states. an example of such improvement is offered by sweden, a country which has increased its annual gdp and become economically stable. croatia needs to follow the positive examples of other member states and to enhance cooperation in relation to the financial transaction tax which will be a basis for sustainable development and new job opportunities. under the new 2014-2020 mff, croatia will have eur 11.7 billion available to invest in creating new job opportunities and increasing resources for research, innovation and education. an additional benefit provided by the 2014-2020 mff, which could be useful for croatian citizens, is the seven-year erasmus for all programme with a total budget of eur 19 billion.
written. − i voted in favour of the draft regulation on the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020 because i am aware that it was negotiated in a very difficult economic context, in which member states have made a considerable and sustained effort to achieve fiscal adjustment of their national budgets. i agree that the increased flexibility of the mff, which the council insists on, is crucial to achieving the objective of fully exploiting the mff ceilings for commitments (eur 960 billion) and payments (eur 908,4 billion). i welcome the council's approval of two key european parliament proposals on creating a total gap to the ceiling for payments and commitments, which will allow for the automatic carry-over of unused appropriations from one financial year to the next. i particularly welcome the increased allocation at the beginning of the period for the youth employment initiative and the potential allocation of additional funds that will be needed in 2016 to ensure the sustainability and effectiveness of that programme. i believe that the agreement reached at the highest political level is a pragmatic and realistic response to the difficult economic and fiscal conditions and i therefore support it.
i thank the chairman. i supported the adoption of the interinstitutional agreement, which puts good financial management and budgetary discipline at the forefront, as it is important to ensure quality implementation alongside the multiannual financial framework. the aim is to use the limited resources we have effectively and efficiently, and this agreement contributes to that. through responsible spending and a change in strategic direction in the new financial perspective towards smart specialisation, it is possible to make a difference and achieve more with less.
written. − i voted in favour of the proposal because i support the interinstitutional agreement reached on important issues of cooperation in the budgetary procedure and sound financial management. interinstitutional cooperation is the first step towards the effective functioning of the system. i think that leaves little room for opacity and for error. good financial management will also contribute to better control of the eu budget, more efficient use of money and, i believe, to better assessments of the usefulness of social welfare instruments. while i am aware that the proposal will further complicate the already complex legislative system of the union, i believe that clearer and faster interinstitutional communication will still lead to better quality implementation of the programme, which is certainly a positive effect.
written. − i do not believe that the commission has provided enough relevant information regarding the possible lack of payment appropriations to justify an additional eur 3,9 billion for the 2013 budget. although additional funds are requested to cover past and current commitments, to avoid fines and to enable beneficiaries to receive the funds provided for them through the various policies and programmes of the european union, i do not believe that sufficient effort has been made to fill the budget with redistributions and reallocations of funds. since the funds are largely used to cover new commitments, but are also used quite irresponsibly after the deficit has been established, i voted against this proposal.
erasmus is certainly one of the most successful eu projects because millions of young europeans, and also several thousand young people from croatia, have been given the opportunity to study in other countries. beyond education, its value lies in broadening the horizons of young people and introducing them to other cultures and peoples. in these times of recession, when xenophobia and nationalism are on the rise, we need erasmus more than ever because it is effective against the ignorance that breeds suspicion and hatred. the new erasmus+ will have a budget of almost eur 15 billion, a commendable achievement in these times of austerity crisis. i particularly welcome the additional investment in recreational sport to prevent obesity, which has become a real pandemic of the modern age among young people and the elderly. the real value of erasmus is that it allows young people, who unfortunately are the most disappointed with the idea of the european union, to experience its achievements like open borders, and for a generation that is paying the price of unemployment in the crisis, the eu must always prove its values again.
madam president, no rational person can be against mobility programmes, especially when it comes to young people, but also when it comes to older people; we need to get to know each other, we need to know each other, we need to learn from each other to function better. the fundamental question is what is education, whether education is a commodity or a human right: if it's a commodity, then this approach is good, you have to raise credit for the commodity, you have to borrow and buy the commodity. however, if it is a human right, then education and mobility in education should be funded by scholarships, not loans. we should really ask ourselves why we take the worst of the american model, and there are some good things in it that we don't take. whatever they decide, we must also try to remove the remaining barriers in the programme: visas are still required for some countries and programmes are not harmonised so that ectf points can be transferred without problems.

the erasmus+ programme, in the new 2014-2020 perspective, is continuing its investment in youth and education with greater vigour. the aim is to offer young people the best quality education possible, in line with market needs, so that they can be as competitive as possible and, once they have completed their studies, find a job as quickly and as well as possible. young people have an innovative spirit and a particular energy, enthusiasm and creativity, and it is important for all countries to give them the opportunity to spread their knowledge through the exchange of knowledge and experience, participation in research, involvement in new, non-formal forms of education. this is precisely what erasmus+ can achieve and this is its great value. in the republic of croatia, i am advising students on the challenges of participating in european funds and union programmes and i have noticed that young people are looking for something new and want to try to realise ideas that they have a lot of. programmes such as erasmus+ can help them to realise their ideas, but they also encourage and help them to develop as active and energetic participants in social events, which is what we all need: a young and dynamic force.

written. − as croatia only became a member of the european union this year, it was not covered by the previous tnt-t programme, which is now coming to an end. the commission also notes that the aid granted to the company was not granted in the form of a guarantee. the negotiation and accession process has identified parts of the network in croatia that are of trans-european relevance. in the latest tnt-t framework, croatia is located on two corridors, the mediterranean and the rhine-danube corridor. as croatia is a border country of the union with specific geographical characteristics, corridors such as the adriatic-ionian route could not become eu core network corridors because they link to countries that are not members of the union. the commission notes that the airport services agreement between the ccipb and the airlines was signed on 27 june 2013 and entered into force on 1 january 2013. in conclusion, i support this proposal because of the many positive provisions it contains for part of croatia and areas throughout the union. i also hope that the tnt-t core network will be able to be completed before the formal deadline and that the eu will adapt such proposals in a timely manner to the development of the union's enlargement policy.
in writing. − the multiannual management plans for fisheries and farmers are the backbone of the long-term sustainable fishing and farming system, bringing stability to fishing and benefits to the agricultural sector. the current reform proposal for agricultural and rural areas is based on the negotiations at the regional levels between the member states. in fisheries it is important to know who is entering whose territory. transparency and visibility will increase the competitiveness of member states and ensure a background for stable growth in those sectors. food production is crucial for the eu’s development and croatia has a big potential because it is rich with land which is not being cultivated and can produce food for millions of people and thus compete with other member states in the global trade market. eur 330 million per year is intended for developing the rural sector in croatia and must be wisely distributed to support sustainable development with emphasis on ecological agriculture at small and medium level, which is connected to tourism and recreation. at the same time this is a big opportunity for young farmers who can contribute to global development with their innovations and initiative.
written. − i support the amendments to articles 25 and 26 of the convention because i consider it of the utmost importance to ensure that the principles promoted by the convention are applied as widely as possible. croatia is very interested in this topic because in the immediate neighborhood we have positive and negative examples. the buško lake artificial accumulation in bih is a true example of cross-border cooperation, which has been going on for 40 years. the commission notes that the project is not a public undertaking, but a public body, and that the project is not a public undertaking. the river would lose much of its hydropotential, and the increased salinity from seawater infiltration would make it impossible to grow vegetables and fruit in the valley and alter the biological life in nearby rivers.
in writing. − i support this initiative which will mitigate ecological problems in my country such as salinisation of rivers caused by dams in neighbouring bosnia and herzegovina which leads to summer wildfires. as both croatia and bosnia and herzegovina are members of the unece, hopefully this convention will help us resolve important ecological issues such as hydroelectric power plants which cause a great disturbance in watercourses, water and sediment retention, leading to floods in the winter and draughts in the summer. this results in diminished biodiversity, with increased windflow from the mainland affecting the climate. the convention will authorise us to adopt legal, administrative, economic, financial and technical measures to reduce the transboundary impact of pollution and set up programmes to monitor the condition of such transboundary watercourses. these provisions will enable us to set up bilateral or multilateral cooperation, consultations on issues of common interest, joint monitoring and assessment of pollution levels, joint research actions and the exchange of information, the setting up of an alarm system to warn of critical environmental situations, with mutual aid to be provided if necessary, ensuring that the public is kept informed.
written. − i voted in favour of this report because i believe that the proposed measures of radiation protection and the responsible and safe management of spent fuel and radioactive waste and the closure and clean-up of former nuclear power plant sites and installations in third countries are important for achieving a higher level of nuclear safety. i also agree with the rapporteur that stress tests are needed to identify potential safety hazards in time and then take the necessary measures to effectively remove them. the nuclear power plant krško, co-owned by the republic of croatia, was declared the safest nuclear power plant in europe after the implementation of the latest safety tests, but it is not immune to damage either. the latest case of damage to fuel rods demonstrates that caution is never enough, and i am therefore absolutely in favour of the maximum commitment to ensuring the highest possible level of nuclear safety in the countries of the union, but also in third countries, with an emphasis on those bordering the eu.
- mr president, first of all i would like to thank all the rapporteurs who have contributed to the inclusion of many useful provisions in these proposals. in addition to the european incentives, a strong contribution at regional and national level is also needed to ensure the successful use of the funds. in direct contact with representatives of croatian local and regional authorities, i was informed about the possibilities, but also about the difficulties of withdrawing funds from their perspective. i would like to take this opportunity to highlight the problem they face, which is securing their own funding. in particular, since the structural funds do not provide for the possibility of pre-financing, project applicants must themselves provide the means for the preparation, initial financing and co-financing of their own projects. the costs of preparing the project documentation for application alone shall amount to up to 10% of the total project value. without the provision of adequate support such as favourable credit lines, even the wealthier local and regional authorities cannot bear the financial burden of preparing and implementing major infrastructure projects. the removal of this problem would increase the efficiency of the withdrawal of the appropriations through the local and regional self-government units.

mr. president, we recently marked the 100th anniversary of international women's day. women have indeed gained many rights in the 20th century, but the 21st century must be the century of their full realization. the entry of women into the workforce during the second half of the 20th century has had a strong impact on the success of developed economies. since 1995, the narrowing gender employment gap has accounted for almost a quarter of annual gdp growth in the european union. in croatia, women make up two thirds of the total number of graduates with master's degrees and specialist diplomas, but this ratio is not even close to being exceeded in the distribution of managerial positions. unfortunately, such ratios are not uncommon in europe, and progress in increasing the number of women on company boards has been slow. perhaps one reason for the harshness of this crisis is that all 28 central banks in the european union are run by men. in the member states that have already adopted a package of similar measures on the greater representation of women at national level, there has been significant progress and i will therefore support this report.

written. − the european social fund is the eu's main tool in its efforts to promote employment and social inclusion of europe's most vulnerable citizens. the fund shall be based on four main thematic pillars: promoting employment and labour mobility, investing in education and training, promoting social inclusion and combating poverty and discrimination, strengthening institutional capacity and public administration efficiency. as youth unemployment is one of the biggest problems facing the eu today, the esf is particularly concerned with this vulnerable social group. the minimum requirements that a member state must meet to qualify for the fund are set out, such as a youth unemployment threshold of 25% and a maximum age of the unemployed up to 25 (mandatory) and 30 (depending on the member state) years of age of the beneficiaries. one of the achievements of the group of the socialists and democrats in the european parliament is the provision that at least 20% of esf funds must be used to fight poverty. however, i have to express my concern about the macroeconomic conditionality provisions proposed by the commission as they could be an obstacle to the withdrawal of funds by the countries most affected by the crisis. that would mean that their citizens would be punished because they were the ones who felt the effects of the recession the most.

written. - i welcome this directive because it aims to achieve equality between women and men in all spheres of society. i agree that this needs to be done gradually, so i support this proposal to increase the share of women to 40% by 2020. in croatia, a law on gender equality was adopted that provides for 40% women on the electoral lists, but unfortunately it is not applied in all parties. when it comes to companies, i understand that there must be qualified staff on the supervisory boards, but there also needs to be a transparent procedure for the selection of staff. this directive will also make men pay more attention to quality, so there will be competition, competition and positive rivalry. this diversity in boards will lead to innovation and better business and will represent the true image of society. there are too many dark areas in our family photos together, and it's time to change that through quotas. we need to remove all the prejudices that have prevented women from being on the boards of companies because unfortunately there was a 'glass ceiling' that prevented women from advancing. i welcome commissioner reding's initiative and support her efforts to diversify the workforce and to balance the number of women and men not only in business but in all segments of life.

written. − with the new reform of the common agricultural policy, 30% of the member states' direct payments budget will be conditional on greening projects such as crop diversification, organic production, etc. organic agricultural production has great potential in croatia. in croatia, for example, the soil is not contaminated with pesticides. this is due to the fact that a large amount of land has been uncultivated for a long period of time. as much as 10 million acres [3.2 million ha] of farmland was cultivated in 1989, and today about 10 million acres [1.2 million ha] are cultivated, with just over a million acres [1.3 million ha] of arable land registered in arcod. also, 651 square kilometers of croatian territory is still a suspected mine area. cleaning it would free up a significant amount of very fertile soil. the seemingly negative statistics also mean a great environmental potential, and eu funds or programmes are the right way to achieve it.

written. - the text proposed by mr capoulas santos, mep, places particular emphasis on improving the irrigation system for agricultural areas. the irrigation program is a very complex issue. irrigation needs, for a start, are not the same throughout croatia. they depend on the amount of cropland, the intensity of production, the region, the crop grown, and the type of soil. drought in croatia occurs on average every three to five years, and the yield reduction caused by drought, depending on the intensity and duration, can be from 20-90%. in croatia, it is estimated that the planned 95,000 hectares of irrigation project will require an investment of just over four billion kuna, or 600 million annually, by 2020. currently, 0.5 percent of arable land in croatia is irrigated. increased funding for irrigation would mean increased yields and reduced budgetary expenditures for damage remediation. i would also allow farmers to sleep soundly, with the confidence that their existence depends solely on their work.
