i thank the chair, i warmly congratulate the rapporteurs on a job well done and with the undisputed legal quality of this report the reason for my support or endorsement is simple. this report does not deal with whether our parliament should sit in strasbourg as it has done so far or move to brussels as a certain number of members want, this report does not even contain an economic component on how much will be saved in the constant travel between brussels and strasbourg, this report stresses that the european parliament itself should, i repeat myself, decide where it will work, how it will work and in what way it will work. this report emphasises that the treaty of lisbon gives parliament the right to be a full co-legislator and that it is therefore entitled to attribute its working principles to itself. this is not only in accordance with common sense but also with the generally accepted principles of the separation of powers and legislative duties in any mature democracy. i believe that in the struggle between the council, the commission and parliament, a struggle which is not an end in itself but is there for the sake of balance and the well-being of our citizens, parliament should reaffirm its place in this process, and that is the place of the only directly elected body of the eu whose primary objective is to represent the citizens and their interests.

vice-president of the commission. - madam president, as promised, i will try to be brief, although the subjects raised during the debate were very many and added comparative points with regard to cosme and the questions contained in mr rübig's question. first of all, i would like to reassure the house, and in particular mr jäätteenmäki, of the european commission's attitude, which is that small and mediumsized enterprises are a priority. it is no accident that the small business act exists or that a large part of the whole internationalisation effort is devoted to small and mediumsized enterprises, thanks to a communication and strategy of the european commission, adopted during this legislature, dedicated precisely to this objective. it is not by chance that i have signed cooperation agreements in the field of small and mediumsized enterprises in different parts of the world. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we are therefore paying close attention to this issue. i can already announce to the portuguese members who have spoken in this debate, mr correia de campos, mr carvalho and mr zuber, that at the end of the month we will be in portugal with a delegation of over 150 european companies who want to invest in the country. we will also organise the third mission to greece to support small and mediumsized enterprises, with new investments by european enterprises, to show that europe does not only require sacrifices, but also supports growth and the real economy. the other big issue under discussion is access to credit. as you know, as soon as i was appointed commissioner for industry, also with the vote of this parliament, i set up an sme finance forum to deal with all these problems. thanks also to the work of parliament, we have adopted the late payment directive, which came into force on 16 march this year, and i am strictly monitoring its transposition and correct application in all member states. it is no coincidence that it has launched two infringement procedures against as many countries as a series of eu pilots to seek clarification on the way in which the community directive is being transposed. so, as regards access to credit, we are working with the support of parliament to make the banking union a functional instrument as soon as possible. the banking union is not something for the banks but a tool to make access to credit easier. in my previous speech i highlighted the agreement on loans to small and mediumsized enterprises of up to eur 1.5 million, as a derogation from the basel iii agreement, thanks to a joint decision by parliament, the commission and the council. in addition, there is the large package approved by the council, which we are working on with european investment bank funds and community budget funds to facilitate access to credit for small and mediumsized enterprises. mr allam raised the problem of late payment of advances, i.e. before the entry into force of the community directive on late payment, of which italy is a striking example. on 18 march the european commission issued a note, signed by vice-president olli rehni and myself, explaining that italy could pay all its previous debts without violating the stability pact. italy cannot therefore justify its failure to pay its debts, because europe would forbid it to do so. following this communication from the commission, the italian government started a new season by paying off part of the unknown amount of previous debts. the country needs to move in this direction because that means getting the real economy back on track. as regards the common currency, mr allam, i do not agree with you on the question of the euro: my personal position on this is very clear. but this is a theoretical discussion of economic policy which is currently taking place outside the house and i shall not dwell on it. i conclude by responding to the request made by mr andrés barea. i agree with mrs andrés barea. there is no much money in cosme, but we can't do more. i would like to have more money, but this is the decision agreed by the council, the commission and parliament. the next time, with the next commissioner, in the next financial perspective, if you can do much more, but we start with this project, which i think is very positive. it is a message to european smes that, for the first time, there is a political decision by the commission, the council and the european parliament whereby we clearly say that there is money for the development of european smes, which are the heart of our economy.

we've talked a lot today about military capabilities, but anyone who's worked in defense planning who's done a strategic defense review knows that it comes at the end of the process, that you have to first identify what the threats are, so a threat assessment, and then from that comes the missions and the tasks of the armed forces, and from that comes the military capabilities that we want to develop or that we might want to develop together. that is why i think it is extremely important that europe gets a white paper, a white paper which will really define precisely at that highest political level what those threats are which europe perceives as common and which then demand a common european defence as the answer. what are the threats that we're going to be working on with the americans in nato and what we might be working on on a national level. so that's my expectation for december when the prime ministers in the council will have to answer these questions as well.

member of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, i would like first to thank the committee on economic and monetary affairs and in particular the rapporteur, mr stolojan, for the constructive and efficient work done during the negotiation process. the commission broadly welcomes the outcome of these negotiations. the eu added value of the fiscalis 2020 programme is confirmed by the proposed agreement, particularly in relation to the fight against tax fraud and tax evasion. the fiscalis 2020 programme is the successor to the current fiscalis 2013 programme. for almost two decades the fiscalis programme has supported the development and operation of the it systems that enable tax authorities to exchange information. it is the fiscalis programme which finances the vat information exchange system (the so-called vies) enabling vat administrations to control the flow of intra-eu trade and to detect irregularities. it is the fiscalis programme which assists member states in implementing legal developments in relation to the automatic exchange of information. the programme also supports cooperation at grassroots level by allowing tax officials to learn from each other and to exchange expertise, notably on electronic auditing, fraud with cash registers and other types of fraud. this is why i consider the new fiscalis 2020 regulation as an essential instrument in improving cooperation among tax authorities and making further progress in the fight against tax fraud. that said, the compromise on the table contains elements that the commission did not initially support. the budgetary ceiling of 5% for administrative expenditure is one of them: it restricts the commission’s necessary flexibility in implementing the budget. however, given the political importance of achieving progress on eu tax policy, the commission does not oppose it. the budget for the programme is relatively modest. however, it provides crucial support in improving the operation of taxation systems in the eu and in fighting tax fraud and tax evasion more effectively. this in turn will help to preserve the welfare of europe, its member states and its citizens.
on behalf of the ecr club. the importance of this programme for strengthening the internal market is exceptional and, given the evident satisfaction of national tax administrations with its implementation in 2013, i consider it necessary to continue its implementation until 2020. the strengthening of cooperation and effective exchange of information between national tax authorities, the establishment of joint training for tax agents, it modernisation and the involvement of third countries in the prevention of tax fraud and evasion can only be welcomed and absolutely supported, but i believe that the effectiveness of the tax system cannot be measured only by technical achievements and the removal of administrative obstacles to cross-border cooperation. adapting the system to citizens, through respecting their financial freedom, is the key to any tax policy. we should be aware that the most effective tax system is one that is invisible and that does not stifle the entrepreneurial spirit of citizens with excessive taxes.

i thank the chairman, i thank this report, basically the 2012 audit report, points to the weaknesses and errors in the system that were most expected in the implementation of the cohesion and agricultural policies and, when looking processically, in the public procurement procedures. the report shows that the quality of the system is still not reliable and that the level of irregularities and errors is increasing. in addition to these findings, the court should more specifically indicate with recommendations the measures to be taken to achieve improvements and solutions as the commission tries to respond to the findings but the responses are mainly in the direction of new levels of control and audit. the system thus becomes more complex, and the commissioner himself said that the very reason for errors and irregularities is the complexity of the system and the many instructions that are often unclear and insufficiently precise: and indeed when we look at the levels of control in the system, there are many, many, many, from the managing and enforcing bodies that carry out control, and then there are internal auditors, external auditors, in the member state, and then auditors from the commission, from the court of auditors, i sometimes get the impression that people are just working to copy the documentation for carrying out audits and controls. that's why we need to focus, to really try to change the system, because i'm sure we all have a common goal, both the member states and the eu institutions, which is to use budget funds legally and purposefully and to achieve results by using resources optimally.
i support the adoption of this report by my colleague, mr riera madurell, on the eu parliament and council regulation establishing horizon 2020. i believe that innovation and research, for which we will allocate up to eur 80 billion to selected projects in the next multiannual financial framework, is a really big contribution to the eu and member states, and entrepreneurs in particular, becoming global leaders, to keep pace with those who are most innovative. this is an initiative that is one of our strategic goals and priorities in europe 2020 and i think its strategic focus is precisely to link research with the market, with companies, with those who need to create jobs and growth. we are particularly interested in those projects that relate to croatia, we have good experience with the competition and innovation programme, as well as with framework programme 7, and i believe that such high-quality cooperation from our scientists will be achieved in the next seven-year period.
i am pleased to support horizon 2020 because it is of particular importance and supports research and innovation, which is precisely what every economy, every country and the union as a whole needs to boost growth and development through new creative solutions. the budget for this programme is substantial and amounts to eur 80 billion. horizon 2020 in combination with the cosmo programme will help technology transfer and the development of smes by raising competitiveness. that is what is important and what we all need, to link science, scientific innovation with practice with entrepreneurship and to put scientific research in the function of growth and development. horizon will also provide funding for entrepreneurial research and development by providing favourable funding instruments.
written. − i supported the programme 'horizon 2020 - the framework programme for research and innovation (2014-2020) ' because i believe that the three priority areas it defines - scientific excellence, industrial leadership and societal challenges - are very much alive, current and pervasive challenges of globalisation, both in the eu and in my home country, croatia. within the programme, i particularly welcome the provision that puts a clear focus on global social challenges, by funding research and innovation that addresses citizens' concerns about today's crucial issues, such as climate change, food security, energy scarcity and drinking water. i also welcome the provision that puts the emphasis on the competitiveness of european industries, because increasing competitiveness is a roadmap for economic recovery and growth. this programme will play a central role in ensuring smart and sustainable development and implementing a strategy aimed at excellence in research and innovation. as such, it could become an important factor in stimulating the development of small and medium-sized enterprises in many member states, which is the only path to economic prosperity.
in writing. − i support the adoption of these rules as they form a single flexible framework to simplify participation and encourage the involvement of small and medium-sized enterprises which are a significant source of innovation and growth in europe. the rules will reduce the administrative burden for the beneficiaries and ensure open access of all research to the developing countries in order to tackle global challenges such as health. as croatia’s economy greatly relies on small and medium-sized enterprises which create new products, services and jobs, this will enable easier participation for companies and commercial application of the discoveries made in research, stimulate business development and create jobs. one such bright example is the croatian company with its highly accomplished glycobiom project. the success of this small company, together with a more efficient and transparent horizon 2020, will speed up innovations and realisations of ideas on the global market, encouraging other croatian smes to become more involved, contributing to croatia’s economic growth, as genos alone pays more than 100 000 kunas of taxes and contributions into the state budget every month.
written. − eu member states hit by the financial crisis are recording large economic losses across all sectors, with the biggest impact on small and medium-sized enterprises. these companies have been slow to recover and develop since the crisis. research and innovation programmes need to be put in place as soon as possible to help speed up recovery and ensure stable economic growth for smes, while ensuring the competitiveness of eu member states on the global market. the cosmo programme should provide all eu member states with a suitable 'background' for stable economic development. the reduction of administrative costs and the simplification of procedures would facilitate and increase cooperation between member states, which would re-encourage and re-encourage investors to invest. the second sector most affected by the crisis is tourism. the introduction of the visa regime, the reduction of the financial power of consumers and the decrease in the culture of travel have significantly reduced the number of tourist arrivals in croatia. the commission has also received comments from the croatian authorities, which have not provided any evidence that the measures in question are imputable to the state. croatia must take the necessary measures offered within the eu programme for economic recovery as soon as possible. this is why i support all eu initiatives to accelerate economic recovery and sustain economic growth in eu countries.
in writing. − this programme is an important step forward as it strikes a balance between excellent science, industrial leadership and societal challenges. the programme provides a mechanism which strives to cover the entire innovation cycle, from the earliest stages to the passage from concept to market. as excellence is the main driver for horizon 2020, it should be defined independent of any geographical precondition. participation in projects should be widened in order to stimulate excellence across europe with emphasis on small research groups while highly innovative start-ups as smes are central to reinforcing the competitiveness of european industry. this simpler, faster and more efficient mechanism will enable and encourage european civil society to become even more engaged in developing economies across the globe, offering innovations and real solutions to existing societal challenges. similarly, it will reduce bureaucratic and administrative obstacles for research results to allow them to be more effectively converted into innovative products that reach the market.
written. − the european administration, responsible for the development, production and dissemination of european statistics, coordinates the statistical activities of the institutions and bodies of the european union. as eurostat is in fact the only relevant provider of statistical information at european level and the data it has at its disposal are as harmonised as possible, we must support the proposed changes. i consider it crucial that the national statistical institutions are completely free from all political interference at national and european level. national and european statistics should be reliable and accurate, as well as comparable and accessible, and should be regularly updated to ensure that union policies and financial initiatives take full account of developments in europe, in particular as regards the consequences of the economic crisis, which are severe. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council adopt a common position on the proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european statistical office. the commission should be empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with article 290 tfeu concerning the format and content of the data to be transmitted to the commission. the objective of european statistics is to provide relevant statistics at european level that allow for relevant comparisons between countries and regions.
written. − i voted in favour of this proposal because i believe that there is a need to allocate the available resources more efficiently in order to alleviate social problems in member states which are implementing radical economic reforms. i fully support the two positive components of the programme, which relate to the creation of small businesses by allocating certain small amounts of money to people who are unemployed or at risk of losing their jobs and to the funding of a wide range of projects led by organisations and public administrations in the areas of employment, social inclusion and equal opportunities. however, i am also concerned about the part of the programme that relates to the funding of mobility projects, as the insistence on such programmes could lead to a demographic, social and economic breakdown of poorer member states, including croatia.

in writing. − this action plan will strengthen assistance measures for business, particularly for smaller ones by promoting business creation and entrepreneurial aptitudes. this will include all types of enterprises and bring together initiatives for entrepreneurial education in national programmes while encouraging self-employment. programmes will be set up to facilitate guidance and assistance by more experienced entrepreneurs to new ones. the programme will also encourage smes to employ youth as they are especially vulnerable in the labour market due to their lack of experience and practical skills, causing them to face long-term unemployment. this is where initiatives such as ‘new skills for new jobs’ and the youth guarantee will help develop skills and entrepreneurial aptitudes, making young individuals more competitive in the labour market. as youth unemployment is croatia’s biggest problem, with 51.8% of unemployed people under the age of 25, such measures will facilitate business creation and help create jobs for the 104 774 currently unemployed youth in croatia. furthermore, incentives to employers who offer training for low-skilled or unskilled individuals as well as opportunities to acquire practical experience directly at the workplace are highly necessary.

mr president, while supporting the provisions on avoiding and minimising unwanted catches, i would stress that the implementation of these measures requires a particular strengthening of the specific character of fisheries in the adriatic and the mediterranean. this is a fishery that exploits a large number of species without a single target fishery and is therefore more difficult to target. i support the proposed strengthening of the rights of producer organisations in the context of the common organisation of the market, but i would like to take account once again of the fact that producer organisations are not a common form in the mediterranean. although they are found in italy and greece, their function is not the same as that of the northern countries. in croatia, fisheries cooperatives are a typical form of producer organisation, established with the aim of improving the working conditions of their members. as producer organisations are the main channel for implementing the cmo measures, it is evident that cooperatives in croatia, for example, do not have the same instruments at their disposal. in order to realise the full potential of the beneficial measures proposed in both reports, differences between member states also need to be taken into account and i believe that the provisions mentioned will be adapted to the examples given.

in writing. − i voted for the justice programme 2014 to 2020 because i consider the proposed programmes to be important instruments for supporting the implementation of eu policies in the fields of justice, rights and citizenship. its aim is to promote effective, comprehensive and consistent application of union legislation in the area of judicial cooperation in civil and criminal matters. it also aims to foster access to justice and to prevent and reduce drug demand and supply. the proposal is based on articles 81(1) and (2), and article 82, which allow for the implementation of judicial cooperation in civil and commercial matters. the proposal is also based on article 84, which provides for the establishment of measures to promote and support action by member states in the field of crime prevention. creating a european judicial culture, which fully respects the principles of subsidiarity and judicial independence, is fundamental to guaranteeing that a european judicial area functions correctly. croatia will also benefit from this because it will involve local and regional authorities in implementing the programmes, particularly in developing the annual work programmes.
written. − i strongly support the commendable initiative to combat all forms of violence against all social groups and all forms of discrimination, and i particularly welcome the efforts to protect consumers and businesses in the internal market. however, i believe that the way in which the report proposes to address certain problems in society should be left to the member states, since they themselves can and should be in a position to best diagnose the problems and find the appropriate solutions. that is why i abstained.
mr president, i voted in favour of this report which proposes to invest almost 440 million euros in activities aimed at promoting fundamental human rights. it is significant that we voted on international human rights day today, when the universal declaration of human rights was signed in 1948. with reports like the rights and citizenship programme 2014-2020, which will fund eu activities aimed at tolerance, non-discrimination, respect for fundamental values and human freedoms, i hope that we will continue to protect all fundamental human rights in the future, including, of course, the right to life from conception, the right to conscience, and it was the vote here in this house a few minutes ago on the so-called ... (contemporary) report that sent a clear message that europe is for these freedoms and against gender ideology.
mr president, i voted, of course, for the report by our rapporteur, mrs kinge göncz, whom i thank for her efforts in producing this excellent report on the new programme on rights and citizenship for the next multiannual financial framework, which allocates substantial funds to programmes in the field of european citizenship and fundamental rights. i believe that the programmes will be complementary to those implemented under the justice programme and it seems to me that the european union must strengthen all the mechanisms relating to the protection of fundamental rights, the implementation of the charter of fundamental rights and also all the principles of non-discrimination, equality between women and men, also the protection of personal data, the rights of children and all the rights that citizens have a chance to protect in the context of consumer protection. i believe it is good that these programmes will be open to third countries. croatia has benefited from this while it was not a member and i hope that others will draw from this a good experience in adapting to european standards.
written. - i agree with the proposed regulation, which is comprehensive and that is why i would like to highlight the points which i consider most important. given that the report promotes a mindset of think globally, act locally, i would stress the importance of the same. the moment an individual, a citizen of the european union, realises the added value that the union brings to him, the parliament and the other institutions of the european union benefit because only then can he truly behave as a responsible citizen, enjoying the rights and benefits guaranteed by the constitution of his country, but also by the fundamental treaties of the european union. every euro invested in promoting civic responsibility is a well-invested euro that is sure to return in multiples. what we must not allow is the relativisation of values. when everything is valuable, nothing is valuable, and that is why i think it is necessary for the european parliament to take care of this and similar issues.
the european union wants a single system of the european area of justice because it strengthens the judicial policy which aims to strengthen the foundations of the union. the links within the european union are becoming stronger and its citizens more mobile. the union's citizens are affected by the same laws in force in different member states and this programme aims to strengthen them wherever people live, work, travel, holiday or spend their leisure time. in such a system, the rights deriving from european citizenship are the principles of non-discrimination, equality between men and women, the right to the protection of personal data, the rights of the child, the rights of consumers and the freedom to conduct a business in the internal market. although democracy encourages diverse expressions of difference, in the context of the strengthening of extremist options caused primarily by the economic crisis, there are moments when it is necessary to set limits to demands that clearly seek to limit the values of democracy. the citizens of the union are the ones who will benefit most from the new seven-year framework, the single legal system within the union, and that is why i am giving my support to this programme at the end of the european year of citizens.

written. − i support the adoption of this convention as it aims at greater safety at work and the protection of workers' health. more than 100 000 types of chemical compounds are known to have adverse effects on the health of workers. it is therefore of the utmost importance that laws and regulations regulate the rules so that both workers and employers know what the hazards are in the workplace and what they are required to do in dealing with them. this is important primarily from the point of view of human life and health, but also from the economic point of view, as it is estimated that about 4% of gdp is spent annually on treating diseases that arise as a result of workplace harm. some positive steps have been taken so far, such as raising the occupational exposure limit values. but i stress that along with the enactment of laws and regulations, it is equally important to strictly monitor their implementation, so that everything does not remain a dead letter on paper.

mr president, i would like to point out that i have supported the innovations and reforms in the common fisheries policy because the emphasis is on ensuring sustainable fisheries. the two important segments of the proposed reform are the prohibition of discarding of unused catches and the achievement of maximum sustainable catches. the commission has also proposed that the maximum sustainable yield be set at the level of the maximum allowable catch (msy) for each species in the mediterranean sea. the commission has also identified a number of areas where the union should be able to take action to address the issue of the discard ban. the financing mechanisms we will soon decide on should certainly include measures to ensure the implementation of reforms with an emphasis on encouraging farming and protecting small traditional fisheries, which is especially important in the republic of croatia for which over 200 million euros are being spent on the implementation of fisheries policy in the perspective of 2014-2020.

written. - i support this report because it seeks to achieve a system that is suited to the needs of modern society, and is prepared to respond in the most effective way to natural and human challenges. in particular, i support the increase in funding for cross-border cooperation which will facilitate the continuation of existing programmes such as the cooperation of croatian field offices for protection and rescue with neighbouring countries or the assistance provided to neighbouring countries and greece by the armed forces of the republic of croatia in firefighting in order to improve the effectiveness of interventions. i would like to take this opportunity to highlight the work of the voluntary and non-profit organisation croatian mountain rescue service, which is partly financed from the croatian budget and partly from eu funds. the organization, with its 700 volunteers, has as many interventions a year that save 1,500 lives. the protection and rescue system requires constant adaptation, education, exchange of experience between countries and investment in prevention. the european union has recognized this, and is therefore strengthening the mechanism to protect its citizens and those of neighbouring countries. croatia, along with 22 other european countries, has joined the creation of a national platform to exchange information, experiences and opinions on disaster risk reduction. the croatian system of the state administration for protection and rescue is fully integrated into the eu civil protection mechanism and through this croatia can offer assistance to any country in europe, but also request assistance.

written. - while i welcome the efforts to prevent disasters and to remedy their possible consequences as effectively as possible, i voted against this report. the commission has not provided any evidence that the subsidy is compatible with the internal market. the eerc should be able to provide the necessary information to the commission and to the member states in order to facilitate the exchange of information between the eerc and the member states.
mr president, i support this proposal because it aims to protect consumers, which i consider to be the basis for an open and fair functioning of the market. i am fully committed to the fight for a free market without excessive regulation, but some basic rules including consumer protection must be put in place in order to achieve that goal. in croatia, the uncontrolled rise in the swiss franc's exchange rate has left some 100,000 swiss borrowers in near-insoluble financial straits. i believe that we should not be open enemies of big business, which of course includes the banking sector, but rather establish a partnership. but it is unacceptable for banks to operate without any risk as it is in croatia. offering credit to over-indebted citizens in a country with a weak economy is a business risk and banks must learn to deal with a negative outcome. instead of putting citizens out on the street overnight and leaving them with nothing, after which they become an unbearable burden on the state welfare system, that's why it's called risk and it has to become risk in the true sense of the word.
mr. president, i voted for this directive because i believe it is very important to our families. it's about giving and taking credit responsibly, and we need to protect our families in a time of global crisis. the directive, which aims to create an efficient and competitive pan-european mortgage market, should be supported. the introduction of conditions for creditors and intermediaries to ensure a high level of professionalism in the provision of mortgage loans is a matter of fundamental importance. some of you are aware of the problem that croatia has had, or still has, with swiss franc loans and it is necessary to do everything to avoid similar unpleasant situations in the future. consumers and their interests must come first. the obligation of the creditor and, where necessary, the credit intermediary is to provide appropriate information to consumers who need to be provided with the necessary explanations at the pre-contractual stage. accordingly, consumers must also be protected by the introduction of general principles for marketing and advertising. if this directive is implemented, it will significantly reduce the possibility that one of the members has problems like croatia has with the swiss franc.
mr president, i supported the proposal for a directive on mortgage credit agreements because i believe that the provisions of this directive are extremely important for better protection of citizens, of credit users, but also for banks, which are required to have higher standards in relation to their customers. the directive requires that users be provided with education and advice on services before signing contracts and that they be given sufficient time to consider before committing to a credit, so that they do not accept services that are not in their interest. the directive also introduces a european union-wide standard in the calculation of annual interest rates, and, what is particularly important, it encourages member states to decide what safeguard measures to take in relation to the foreclosure of homes to those who are unable to pay credit obligations. the directive also introduces the possibility for borrowers to repay their loans earlier.
written. − europe has been in a severe financial and economic crisis for years for a number of reasons, one of the main ones being the collapse of the mortgage market in the united states in 2007. spain and ireland were brought to their knees by this, and their governments had to bail out failing banks with taxpayer money. croatia had its own version of this problem in the form of a huge growth in the swiss franc credit war, which put thousands of citizens' existence in question. the croatian government has taken steps to prevent this in the future, but we also need a european level of consumer protection. the common market should also have common rules, but with respect for the rules of subsidiarity. for this reason, i welcome the adoption of this directive as it will strengthen consumer rights and information and, in part, prevent irresponsible lending. some of the new features include a ban on misinformation of consumers through advertising and the right to early repayment of loans if the debtor is able to do so. in the future, creditors will have to investigate in more detail the ability of potential borrowers to repay loans, which, although necessary, will not be popular with some citizens.
written. − i support the report on the proposal for a directive of the european parliament and of the council on loan agreements relating to residential immovable property. it is of the utmost importance, especially in the context of the economic and social crisis, to create a responsible and efficient european credit market oriented towards the benefit of citizens. this directive ensures responsible borrowing and lending in the field of housing loans, which are the most important and longest financial commitment a family undertakes. the importance of the advisory function of the lenders and their responsibility to inform and educate clients in the pre-contractual period is emphasised. although cross-border lending is possible, it is almost not used due to the fragmentation of the housing credit market and this directive therefore aims at the completion of the internal market in housing credit. the commission has also received comments from the croatian authorities on the application of the new rules on the prudential supervision of banks. the commission notes that the bank has not provided any information on the amount of the capital injections. i believe that croatia is already making good progress towards adapting its legislation to the future directive.
mr president, as a member of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, i want to take part in this discussion and explain my negative vote. the ets system is recognised as a useful tool for reducing industrial emissions in the eu in a cost-efficient way. the aim of this policy is to stimulate carbon prices through market-based measures that would positively influence investment in renewable energy and low-carbon technology, leading to the reduction of carbon emissions. the ets created a price-based market which stimulates the carbon market. however, due to unexpected macro-economic developments, industrial emissions decreased and consequently created an imbalance in the market as the increase in supply plus the reduction in demand created low prices for emission certificates, thereby stunting competition, disabling sufficiently sustainable scientific and technological innovation and, in turn, reducing investment in the carbon market. though there are no long-term solutions, short-term preventive measures cannot be efficient at this moment.
in writing. − i voted in favour of this proposed regulation on defining criteria determining when recovered paper ceases to be waste. pursuant to directive 2008/98/ec on waste, certain specified waste ceases to be waste once it has undergone a recovery operation and complies with specific criteria to be developed in line with legal conditions laid down in the directive. as a member of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, this topic interests me greatly because if we do not take care, improperly treated waste can become a hazard for the environment and peopleʼs health. on the legal side, there needs to be a requirement that the producer and each subsequent holder issue a statement of conformity for each consignment of recovered paper. furthermore, the producer must implement specific control measures to demonstrate compliance with criteria referred to.

mr president, the european parliament has today sent a very strong message by rejecting the edita estrela report. the report, which is radical in nature, is also comparable to the policy pursued in some member states, which seeks to impose a worldview that is alien to the majority of european peoples. today the european parliament also denounced all those who declared croatian citizens backward, anti-european, because they voted for the initiative "in the name of the family" in the referendum on december 1. today the european parliament rejected any radicalism and stressed the principle of subsidiarity, that each member state can continue to ensure the right of conscience of those citizens who do not want to participate in abortions in hospitals throughout europe. today the european parliament said that member states can continue to defend the right of parents to raise their children in accordance with their own worldview, rather than having the state, certainly not the european union, impose it on them. and that is why i was against this report, mrs estelle, and i am very pleased that the european parliament rejected it by a majority and once again stressed the principle of subsidiarity.
mr. president, i want to make something very clear, and that is that those who have read this report without any a priori prejudice, have surely found in it a whole range of good quality recommendations for improving our public policies on sexual and reproductive rights. and for that i want to congratulate my colleague estelle. and i'm going to tell you two untruths that have been told many times. it is incorrect to say that this report does not respect the principle of subsidiarity. on the contrary, it represents a single non-binding recommendation for all member states and, therefore, it clearly respects the right of each member state to define the sexual and reproductive rights of its citizens. i repeat, alone. the second big untruth is that the estrela report encourages abortion. anyone who has, again, read the report clearly sees that the report opposes abortion as a method of family planning. finally, the report respects the conscience of every doctor or medical professional, but it stresses that such a conscience should not be codified by the governments of the member states as a binding collective principle but should be promoted for what it is: an individual right. this is a very good report and i was happy to support it.

written. − i welcome this resolution as an incentive to volunteering and agree that national governments should introduce formal systems to start assessing the knowledge, skills and competences acquired through volunteering. volunteering as a voluntary investment of personal time, effort, knowledge and skills by performing services or activities for the benefit of another person or for the general good has a direct impact on society. it promotes solidarity between generations, and as a form of social cohesion and awareness-raising for citizens to do good, it should certainly be valued. in croatia there is also a volunteering act which came into force in 2007 as the first normative act that approaches volunteering in croatia in a holistic and comprehensive way. it was introduced to ensure a social environment conducive to the development of volunteering, to prevent possible abuses, and to protect volunteers and volunteering organisers. i believe that any form of community service should be valued and praised, and volunteers should be able to make the knowledge and skills they have acquired through their volunteering work accepted by both educational institutions and employers.
written. − the rapporteur understands the notion of volunteering as an activity covering all forms of unpaid work carried out by an individual on a formal or informal basis, based on his or her free will. i strongly support volunteering, which i consider to be a fundamental form of active citizenship, broadening horizons and democracy, but also of personal development through which individuals involved in volunteering activities acquire the knowledge and social skills necessary to facilitate their inclusion in the labour market. i recognise the need to promote volunteering, especially among young people, and i consider the rapporteur's call to member states to continue to create the conditions for volunteering, in particular by establishing a legal framework, if one does not already exist in a given member state, to be a positive call. however, i strongly oppose excessive and unnecessary interference by the commission in the field of volunteering with regulations such as tax incentives for voluntary organisations, compulsory insurance for volunteers and the establishment of a european voluntary development fund, as i believe that such measures are not appropriate for voluntary and free volunteering and may ultimately do more harm than good to the same. that's why i abstained.

president-in-office of the council. − madam president, the 2013 progress report on albania makes positive reading compared to previous years and it is on this basis that the recommendation by the commission is to grant candidate status to the country. the council is due to decide whether to agree to the granting of candidate status at its meeting on 17 december, subject to confirmation by the european council. among the positive elements were the way in which the general elections were conducted in june 2013. there was solid progress compared with previous elections. as stated in the council conclusions of last year, this was a crucial test for the functioning of the democratic institutions. there was also a smooth transition of power. yet another positive element is the position of the new government as regards cooperation and dialogue with the opposition. it is the intention of the new government to improve relations substantially. inclusiveness is the key word we hear. so far, we have seen these good intentions bear fruit on a number of occasions. we welcome the high level dialogue on key priorities launched by the commission and prime minister edi rama on 12 november. the aim is to help the country maintain focus and consensus on eu integration. this will certainly prove a useful tool for continuing dialogue and monitoring developments on the key priorities that were listed in the commission܀s opinion in 2010. furthermore, on a positive note, i believe it is worth underlining the constructive role albania plays in the region, being fully engaged in a number of regional initiatives such as the regional cooperation council and the south east european cooperation process. however, the council conclusions of last year were clear that, in order to be granted candidate status, albania needs to complete key measures in the areas of judicial and public administration reform and revision of the parliamentary procedure. moreover, further action on the fight against corruption and organised crime will also be required. this huge task has been initiated by the new government. a number of actions have taken place and more are in a preparatory phase. for instance, we understand that the creation of structures to fight corruption is well advanced and that there is an increase in investigations being opened in the areas of corruption and drug trafficking. i do not want to prejudge the decision of ministers on 17 december. the government of albania certainly seems to be determined towards further progress in key areas. at the same time, sustainability and credibility based on an established track record remain important considerations for member states. i am grateful to your rapporteur, nikola vuljanić, and will listen with interest to your views on this issue.
member of the commission. − madam president, i would like to thank parliament and the rapporteur, mr vuljanić, for his excellent report on albania. i welcome its findings and recommendations which are very much in line with our 2013 progress report. in particular i welcome the clear support of this house for our recommendation to grant candidate status to albania. 2013 has been a good year for albania’s european union integration. firstly, the government and opposition cooperated to adopt a number of important legal acts in parliament, including the three measures that were required for the granting of candidate-country status. secondly, the parliamentary elections in june were orderly and competitive and were followed by a smooth transfer of power. thirdly, the 2013 progress report underlined a number of important developments confirming progress towards meeting the copenhagen political criteria for european union membership and addressing reforms in the key priorities as identified by the council in its 2012 conclusions on albania. fourthly, the first meeting of the high-level dialogue on the key priorities with albania was held in november, providing valuable support for key reforms. the assessment of the commission is that all this merits candidate status, hence the unconditional recommendation made in the 2013 enlargement package. we want albania to continue delivering and we see the granting of candidate status as a further incentive for the country to continue its reform efforts. we expect continued follow-up and delivery of results. the support for albania’s european union integration is widespread across the entire country. government and opposition alike have confirmed their commitment to european union related reforms. i am impressed by the way the new government is prioritising its efforts in the fight against corruption and organised crime and i am equally impressed by the results achieved in the months since it has taken office which confirm the positive trend established earlier. granting candidate status is an important step, not only for encouraging albania to pursue the far-reaching reforms required, but also for the credibility of the european union, which set clear conditions for obtaining the candidate status at its 2012 council. enlargement policy is only credible when both sides live up to their side of the bargain. albania has delivered and so should we.
on behalf of the gue/ngl group. – madam president, i would first like to thank everybody who has helped in constructing this document, primarily the people in the committee on foreign affairs secretariat, the people in my group’s secretariat, my assistants and all the colleagues who have contributed to this form of the resolution. the resolution was supposed to come to the plenary in january. i insisted that it be brought earlier for two reasons: one is to help the country, and the other is to help the parliament. i think the parliament should have a strong stand on such an important issue as enlargement. the council has to decide, but the parliament should have a strong stand. it seems that it has worked here in the parliament. the initial draft was primarily the result of the good starting point given to me first by the commission and its report on the progress of albania, then by my visit to albania and the talks i had with the officials there and with some ordinary people whom i succeeded in meeting, and finally, just personally, by my knowing the mentality of the people and my knowledge of the history of the balkans. the result was a positive draft report. numerous amendments came – some 122 – and 21 compromise amendments which i hope made the text more readable and more acceptable to everybody. the resolution stresses primarily the achievements the country has made in the period, what has happened over a year, but it also stresses and points out the various problems and issues that should be solved in the future. they include political, social, economic, environmental protection and numerous other issues. it should be clearly stated that this is not about albania becoming a member in some fairly foreseeable future; it is about its candidacy status, and candidacy status means putting the country under closer scrutiny, under a stronger microscope. i think the country deserves it and i am completely sure that, with the support they have, the albanians can overcome all the obstacles standing in their way. the importance of albania as a country in europe should not be and need not be repeated; but let me say once again that it is the youngest nation in europe and we are not so rich in young people in the rest of the continent. i suppose it will be supported on thursday.
madam president, i would like to begin by congratulating my colleague, rapporteur vuljanic, on a job well done, not just in preparing this report. i think it is extremely important that we discuss this report today before the decision, that is, before the european council meeting in december. it may be wise to do the same with the other countries of south-eastern europe, which will also be decided on at this european council. i would also like to welcome the work of commissioner füle and the european commission in this regard. we see that in albania the main progress is that a consensus has been created for europe, that this has been shown in well-organised elections and in that sense it is important to note here that this process is long, but if there is consensus, if both sides of the political spectrum can work together, that there will be a reform process that will produce results. and that is why i think that the role of the opposition must also be emphasised on this occasion in a constructive tone for europe.
madam president, i would like to congratulate mr vuljanic on his substantive and balanced report, and in the light of his recent visit to albania, allow me to make a few brief remarks. the behaviour of the political parties following the recent parliamentary elections and the peaceful handover of power should be commended, but also called for the continuation of the same relationship in order to establish a sincere political dialogue and achieve constructive cooperation between all involved. accession to the union must be a comprehensive process involving not only political parties but also civil society. i therefore call on the competent authorities to inform stakeholders and the general public in a timely manner about the progress of the wider eu integration process. i welcome the efforts and contribution of the albanian institutions in strengthening regional and international police cooperation on the problem of smuggling, but at the same time i stress the need to implement further reforms in the fight against organised crime. in conclusion, i believe that albania will maintain its constructive position in the region by continuing to communicate with the other western balkan countries, sharing the knowledge and experience gained during their eu integration processes, and thus contributing to intensifying cooperation and further stabilising the region.
mr president, i congratulate mr kukana on the quality of the report on human rights in the world in 2012 and the policy of the european union, especially since today, 10 december, is the day on which we mark the 65th anniversary of the universal declaration of human rights, and in the light of the fact that the european union itself received the 2012 nobel prize for its contribution to promoting peace and trust, democracy and human rights. the european union's work on the promotion and respect of human rights in a global context is one of our priorities. it is part of our foreign policy, it is part of our international treaties, and it is also part of our ongoing effort to highlight the strategic importance of universal human rights protection through the comprehensive documents that we have brought together for the first time in 2012, and in doing so i believe that the special representative for human rights and his activities, mr lambrinidis, will also give us greater visibility. in this context, i appreciate the european parliament's great activity in strengthening human rights and promoting them through the activities of a number of parliamentary committees.
madam president, i would like to welcome commissioner andor and thank him in advance for his efforts to solve the problems of croatian freight forwarders. and i'd like to thank our reporter. as one of the rapporteurs of the implementation of the european globalisation adjustment fund, i can say that in the case of the valencia region, it is precisely the speed of response aspect that has made an important step forward in this instance. it seems to me that it is good that we will have this fund again for the next seven years with a fairly substantial amount of resources and that the range of persons to be covered has been extended, particularly to temporary workers, to the selfemployed, to young people, especially those who are not employed, are not in education or training in regions where unemployment is high. the data we have seen recently, that the fund helped over 15 700 workers last year and 73.5 million euros and an additional 51 million euros from state funds, is important to achieve a result, and i am convinced that croatia, which has not had the opportunity to use it so far, will also be able to use this fund in areas such as shipbuilding and other vulnerable sectors of the economy.
i support, madam president, these amendments to the directive and the extension of the scope of the automatic exchange of information between member states' tax administrations to income from dividends, capital gains and other forms of income from financial assets as from 1 january 2015. the system for the exchange of information between tax administrations is an important instrument for preventing tax evasion and combating fraud. the obstacles to its full implementation are often banking secrecy and the protection of personal data, which should be taken into account. however, the losses in the member states due to tax evasion amount to €1 trillion a year. in these times of fiscal consolidation, which means cutting wages, laying off workers, reducing pensions and social benefits on the expenditure side and increasing tax rates on the revenue side, it is extremely important to focus on the full collection of prescribed taxes, rather than introducing new ones and increasing existing ones. this amendment to the directive is part of the fulfilment of the objectives of the action plan to combat tax fraud and tax evasion adopted in december 2012. the action plan stressed that, in addition to the exchange of information, the exchange of experience and knowledge between member states' tax administrations is important for administrative strengthening in the fight against tax evasion, and i particularly support this.
mr. president, manfred worner said that security is the oxygen of democracy. but like oxygen, we often take security for granted. as one of the main objectives of the european union is to promote peace and support democracy on the international stage, i would like to express my support for the further development of the european security and defence sector. international regional security is threatened by challenges such as extremism, the proliferation of weapons of mass destruction and global and regional networks of organised crime. but security today has increasingly social, economic, energy and environmental dimensions. the instability of the european union's southern and eastern neighbourhood is a concern and for this reason we need to work in partnership to strengthen security cooperation in europe and our neighbourhood. croatia has contributed to this objective even before its formal accession to the union and is actively involved in peacekeeping missions, with an emphasis on training and mentoring. the current global context reflects the need to further develop a european defence strategy. i would therefore like to welcome the next european council meeting in december, devoted to security and defence.
written. − while the euro is not an overly counterfeit currency because of its design and the structures that fight it, maximum efforts should be made to reduce counterfeiting. large-scale counterfeiting can have a significantly adverse impact on the social and economic implications of citizens' lives. the pericles programme is effectively combating this phenomenon but some provisions are unclearly defined and this is something that needs to be corrected with a more efficient use of the funds allocated to this programme. articles 7 and 8 cover a large number of target groups and the resources must not be spread but multiplied whenever and as much as possible. i agree that, when it comes to article 12, the various phases of the consultations carried out by the commission must have a fluid and two-way exchange of ideas in order to learn lessons for the future fight against counterfeiting. finally, i stress that the eu and the member states must encourage close cooperation to strengthen the fight against euro counterfeiting.
written. − the report is fully consistent with the principle of subsidiarity with activities carried out at national level and with the principle of union added value in terms of a high level of cooperation between member states and third countries. i support any cooperation and exchange of information between member states and the institutions of the union aimed at combating euro counterfeiting that produces a better effect than that which would be achieved at national level. furthermore, given that the importance of the euro as a world currency requires an appropriate level of protection at international level, i welcome the provision encouraging cooperation with third-country participants where their presence is important for the more effective protection of the euro. such provision will result in the release of funds for the purchase of equipment intended for third country agencies dealing with the investigation of euro counterfeiting. i also emphasise the segment of the report that shows the savings in the implementation of the programme, thanks to jointly organised activities and procurement compared to possible individual national initiatives. i consider the report positive in every segment, and therefore i voted in favour.
madam chairman, there is probably no job that is so underestimated and so taken for granted as housework. unfortunately, the majority of workers in this business are women, who are more vulnerable in the labor market. in my language, there's not even a word for this job other than "woman". doing work in a home brings with it the challenges of exploitative work and black-collar work, since it takes place in the employer's home. that's why those workers need to be protected, and employers need to be motivated to report workers, not hold them in the dark, and the only way to do that is to reduce the tax burden. i know that tax policy is a matter for individual member states, but i believe that tax relief is the only effective way to motivate employers to provide decent work for these workers.
written. − i voted in favour of the recommendation on the draft council decision authorising member states to ratify, in the interest of the european union, the 2011 ilo convention on decent work for domestic workers. according to eurostat, there are currently 2.6 million domestic workers in the eu, 89% of whom are women and immigrants. the situation on the labour market in croatia shows a disproportionate number of women over men, and the differences in men's and women's salaries for the same job are evident, therefore i consider it necessary to further reduce the tax burden on women, and especially mothers. in many eu member states, as in croatia, domestic workers are employed on a short-term basis and without a contract and earn significantly less than average. this time i have put a special emphasis on the unequal position of women in the labour market, which has also been shown in the current financial crisis facing the eu member states, and it is important to emphasise that people employed in households are mostly women or immigrants. this is about the most vulnerable groups in society who are often the victims of abuse, including serious human rights violations, and the proposal by my colleague ines cristina zuber regulates all these important issues, which is why i supported it.
written. − this report is a response to a proposal by the european commission, authorised by the council, calling on eu member states to ratify the international labour organisation's convention on the right to decent work for domestic workers. as a member of the committee on employment and social affairs, i welcome the fight to promote the rights of domestic workers and the guarantees offered to workers in this segment through the convention. however, i believe that the question of which convention should govern should remain the exclusive competence of the eu member states, as this is a matter of the internal regulation of the labour market. to date, no eu member state has fully ratified the convention and only two member states (italy and germany) are in the process of putting the convention into practice. since the decision on ratification is taken solely at the level of the member states of the union, i voted against because i consider that the council recommendation undermines the principle of subsidiarity and unduly encroaches on the competences of the member states of the union.
written. the european union cares for the weakest in its society, but it also tries to care for the world's most disadvantaged. although the level of donations by individual member states has fallen during the crisis, the union remains the world's largest donor, especially for the african region and parts of asia. it is important to encourage the development of the capacities of these countries so that they can themselves steer further development and be equal partners of the european union in the future. i support this report, especially the part on the importance of involving non-traditional donors, private sector investment and philanthropy. with their assistance, donations will continue to be the driving force behind the less developed parts of the world.

written. − as the balanced development of the union is one of the imperatives of integration, i consider the importance of this report to be crucial to the survival and success of the union as conceived by its founders. particularly striking is the part of the report on 'the cost of a disunited europe', where astonishing figures are given that up to eur 800 million could be saved annually by cutting transaction costs provided that eu donors focus their aid on smaller countries and activities, and that an additional eur 8.4 billion could also be saved annually if the country allocations were fully aligned and focused solely on poverty reduction, ultimately resulting in a transformation of the 'aid efficiency plan' into a 'development efficiency plan'. as a representative of croatia, a new member state with very unfavourable economic indicators, i particularly welcome the proposal that the union should pursue a differentiated approach in this segment based on multidimensional development indicators; indicators that do not stop at gdp but take into account poverty, inequality and vulnerability in each country individually. the commission shall, by means of implementing acts, adopt the necessary measures to ensure that the union's financial contribution to the union's development cooperation programmes is limited to the amounts set out in the relevant financial instruments.
madam president, parliament in its report, the council's annual report on the common foreign and security policy, stresses that the european union must defend the interests of its citizens in a decisive and united way and always base its policy on promoting the fundamental values on which the union is based. sounds good for advertising materials, but is it being implemented? in my experience, no, because in reply to commissioner fülea's question i received a clear answer that the commission would do nothing to protect the rights of croats in bosnia and herzegovina, but that it would leave it to the competent authorities of bosnia and herzegovina. how is the european union protecting its citizens in third countries if the commission is washing the hands of 600 000 croats in bosnia and herzegovina, who are in effect citizens of the republic of croatia and therefore citizens of the european union? how can we promote the values on which the union is founded if we allow eu citizens in a potential candidate country to elect political representatives from other nations and then stand by while croatian refugees are blocked from returning to their hotbeds? it is therefore useless for us to establish common mechanisms for external action when the institutions of the european union themselves do not agree on the interpretation of the content and scope of these mechanisms.
