madam president, the adoption of the stability instrument on the basis of the report proposed by our colleague mr bütikofer will of course contribute to strengthening the role of the european union in resolving crises and conflicts at global level and to our role in strengthening security and stability in the world. conflicts are often linked to the stability of states and are additionally linked to weak governments and poverty. increasingly large and frequent climate change is a multiplier of existing threats and adds a new dimension to human negligence and security risks. i therefore consider the instrument of stability to be extremely important for the european union to use and to use its global potential both in financing various projects and in ensuring stability and peace throughout the world. what is particularly important is that this instrument has almost three billion euros at its disposal over the next seven years and i am convinced that in cooperation with other international organisations it will strengthen our foreign policy position.
written. - recent history has shown that extreme natural disasters can be more easily countered by joint efforts. joint forces often imply solidarity after a disaster strikes a country, so i believe that systematic protection against natural disasters is needed to avoid catastrophic consequences. i think it's a good idea to use the croatian example here. every year in the summer months, croatia sends its canadian firefighters to neighboring countries to help them put out fires. although there are some initiatives to build a system that would act preventively, some neighbouring countries cannot participate in that system on an equal footing because of poor infrastructure. croatia can and will show solidarity and help its neighbours, but because of the unstable way of working, it often happens that during the biggest fires in croatia, croatian canadians have to fly in and put out fires in the neighbourhood. i therefore believe that there should be an instrument at european union level that would operate systematically, as this would have a greater effect without imposing additional burdens on individual countries. it is our duty to the member states, to our neighbours, and ultimately, it is our duty to the world.
written. − i welcome the continuation of the instrument for stability launched in 2007, which has significantly intensified the union's activities in the field of conflict prevention, crisis management and peacebuilding. the instrument for stability and peace shall enable the building of long-term international, regional and national capabilities to address threats, both at regional and global levels. i would therefore like to stress in particular the importance of eu peacekeeping missions and common security and defence policy operations. as a partner country during the negotiations for membership of the european union, croatia has already begun to contribute to peacekeeping missions. the eu has also been involved in the implementation of the un security council resolution on the situation in the democratic republic of the congo. i believe that a certain level of capability of the armed forces should be maintained with the aim of supporting and assisting the civilian population and society in cases of crisis, elementary, natural disasters and possible technical and technological accidents. the eusr shall be responsible for the implementation of the mandate of the high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. for this reason, croatia has a responsibility to contribute to the preservation of peace in these areas through cooperation with neighbouring countries. i hereby welcome the initiative to add the word 'peace' to the title of the instrument as it underlines the strategic objective of stability which is also one of the primary objectives of the european union.
written. - i welcome the report by mr reinhard butikofer on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council establishing a stability instrument. this instrument has been in use since 2007 and will allow the european union to engage more strongly and effectively in the response to global crises in the new financial perspective 2014-2020. as the world's largest economic bloc, the european union must maintain its ambition to contribute more strongly to world peace and security, in line with relevant united nations resolutions but also with the eu's core values. the stability instrument is precisely what makes it possible to implement such a more ambitious common foreign and security policy. the european union's common defence policy, in alliance with the united states and in cooperation with nato, must also be developed, defining in a clearer and more precise way the role and level of european cooperation in defence and in responding to crises in the world.
written. − the creation of the instrument for stability in 2007 was an important innovation in facilitating the eu's response to the crisis. since then, this instrument has been used effectively to enable development and create security and has proved to be a very important strategic tool for the european union. today, the european union can offer a solution to a crisis situation within 8 weeks, and it is possible to finance the necessary measures for up to 18 months. however, i believe that there is room for improvement and therefore i support the commission's proposal the main features of the instrument must be maintained, while at the same time some provisions must be adapted to enhance the flexibility characteristic of this instrument. i also agree with the 42 percent budget increase, although it remains questionable whether that will be enough. recent activities in the arab spring have shown that the eu is still less prepared to take effective short- and medium-term measures to stop the conflict and reduce violence in transitional phases. the commission proposal provides for 65% of the budget for responding to rapidly developing conflict situations, which is less than at present. this is an indication that everything is going in favour of the long-term component and, finally, i would like to stress that the provisions of the proposal should reinforce the facilitation, development and rehabilitation.
madam president, i want to support the establishment of a european neighbourhood instrument because it will serve as the support to be provided to neighbouring developing countries. of course, the european neighbourhood instrument should operate within the framework established by the european neighbourhood policy. the union's macro-financial assistance should be directed to the union's macro-financial assistance, including the union's macro-financial assistance, and should be consistent with the objectives and principles of the union's external policy, in particular the union's development and common security external policies. cooperation between the european union and non-member states through this resolution can be achieved in several areas, for example in the fields of economy, energy and other resources, but also in strengthening democratic principles and rights such as gender equality, the rights of persons belonging to minorities and the fight against discrimination. these are all good reasons why i support this directive. as a member of the european union, whose neighbours are mostly non-eu members, croatia is also involved in building good neighbourly and partnership relations, which contributes to the stabilisation of relations in the region, especially after the tragedies that have hit us in the recent past.
madam president, the european union has for many years been one of the main factors for stabilising the european continent and beyond. a quality and productive relationship with our neighbours should be supported in every way because in this way we promote the universal values that drive the democratization process. shared prosperity commits us to cooperation based on peace and stability and mutual responsibility. i would place particular emphasis on cooperation with smaller countries, which are not able to carry out the transition process on their own and which need the protection of the european union more when it comes to promoting peace policies, combating poverty, fighting corruption and promoting human rights, etc. i expect our neighbours to take the european union's intentions seriously and to make wise use of this initiative, which will surely encourage better cooperation, the results of which will be visible above all in their national frameworks. i therefore give my full support to the establishment of the european neighbourhood instrument.
madam president, i voted for this report by mr kukana, and of course for the other reports on the new instruments for the european external action service. i think that, as far as the neighbourhood policy is concerned, it was extremely important to choose this middle way, that we should not go the way of those who advocated the complete abolition of the transformational dimension of the neighbourhood policy, nor the other extreme that demanded a completely new neighbourhood policy, with a new list of countries. i have chosen this middle way, proposed by my colleague mr kukan, supported by parliament. it is actually about one more effective way of implementing this policy, in particular by emphasising the principle 'more for more', so that those countries covered by the neighbourhood policy who want to move faster with reforms will also get more support from the european union and thus help in their transformation.
madam president, today's instrument on the european neighbourhood policy is crucial for the credibility of the european union's foreign policy. i have already said on several occasions in this house that it is precisely our relationship with the countries around us, whether our eastern neighbours or those that are grieving the mediterranean, that is of the utmost importance for the international positioning of the european union. our activism today, and the resolution we will be voting on tomorrow on ukraine, in the light of the events we have been following overnight, is also a testament to the credibility of both the union and the european parliament itself. i think that the funds provided for in the next multiannual financial framework, which is about 13 billion euros, will contribute to our policy of adapting to the standards of cross-border cooperation, of harmonising legislation, to help all the countries in our neighbourhood to reach those european standards which are the motivation for these events that we have seen in the streets of kiev in the last few days. the principle of 'more for more' and mutual accountability are the keywords of the european union's neighbourhood policy's effectiveness.
written. − the european union offers its neighbours a privileged relationship based on a mutual commitment to shared values. the european neighbourhood policy goes beyond the current relationship between the eu and its neighbours and offers them a deeper political relationship and economic integration with the aim of achieving sustainable democracy. for these reasons, i support this instrument to finance cooperation with the southern and eastern partners. i particularly support security measures and conflict prevention and suppression. i would like to stress the importance of cross-border and regional cooperation projects. regional cooperation and maintaining good neighbourly relations with all partners, and in particular with the western balkan countries, are of exclusive importance not only for croatia but also for all member states. croatia is developing partnerships with key partner countries through development cooperation and thereby strengthening its foreign policy position. the croatian development cooperation policy is aimed at poverty reduction and at achieving sustainable and inclusive development, with a special approach to countries affected by conflict. in addition to the eastern partnership, croatia also cooperates with the mediterranean. the croatian network for euro-mediterranean cooperation promotes dialogue, knowledge and diversity among the cultures of the euro-mediterranean region through the development of tolerance and openness to new knowledge, religions, cultures and values. in conclusion, i support this report which, in addition to promoting political cooperation, also promotes human rights, freedom, equality and the fight against corruption.
madam president, i fully support this initiative and look forward to the enlargement programme of the european union, because i believe that enlargement and the accession of new members simultaneously strengthens both peace and democracy and stabilises europe. i also agree that the means of access assistance should be directed towards users, taking into account their needs and capacities. of course, progress on the path to accession depends on respect for the union values of each applicant and his or her ability to undertake the necessary reforms, but i believe that friendly cooperation in the form of assistance and exchange of experience between member states and countries on the path to european integration can be achieved. croatia, as a new member state of the european union, is also seeking to establish such cooperation with a number of countries to which this regulation will apply, thereby contributing to good neighbourly relations and to strengthening regional stability.
madam president, as one of the representatives coming from the republic of croatia, i know very well how much the funds from the european union's pre-accession funds have been and still are crucial to a better and more successful economic transition that my country is still slowly but surely concluding. just as it was useful and important for croatia to have the european union as the main objective of our foreign policy in mind all along, it was equally important, if not even more important, to have the constant support of the european union along the way, especially in the form of funds from the ipa programme. as we croats know well, adjusting and entering a new political system is never an easy path. the strengthening of administrative and institutional capacities in this respect is a necessary prerequisite for adapting as efficiently and as well as possible to the institutional framework of the european union. the success of the ipa programme is not only important for the candidate countries, but it seems to me even more important for the european union. a well-prepared candidate country means a good and competitive new member state which will soon become a full-fledged player in the community. and that is why i strongly support the report by my czech colleague libor roucek and congratulate him on a job well done.
madam president, i supported the proposal for a regulation on the instrument for pre-accession assistance, particularly because of the new approach to planning being introduced for the beneficiary countries. the republic of croatia has a great deal of experience in using the ipa and it is an instrument that prepares the country well for membership and particularly for participation in cohesion policy. the shortcoming of the first generation ipa was the preparation of specific strategic documents for ipa funding, and the ipa was always perceived as something additional and separate from national priorities, which is far from the truth. the ipa funds are intended to prepare the country for membership of the european union to meet the accession criteria, which are a national strategic priority for the country seeking membership and the ipa should be only one source of funding. this is precisely the approach that the new ipa regulation introduces. the three neighbouring countries of the republic of croatia are ipa beneficiaries and i therefore particularly welcome the fact that the envelope for the period 2014-2020 remains financially significant and amounts to eur 11,7 billion. i am confident that ipa funds will make a significant contribution to the reforms and adjustments that the accession countries need to make.
madam president, i believe that no other colleague could have done a better job of preparing the instrument for pre-accession assistance than mr rouček, who, after mr svoboda, was the last rapporteur on the european parliament's final document on croatia's accession to the european union. this experience is reflected in the main assessments of the proposed instrument for pre-accession assistance, which symbolises the financial strength of the european union in supporting the countries of our neighbourhood that want to be members of the european union tomorrow. i regret that the funds for the next seven years have been cut slightly from the commission's initial proposal, but there is still almost €12 billion to be spent over the next seven years to ensure that all the reforms on the agenda are implemented and that the support is strategically oriented, medium-term, that the ipa helps these countries to keep track of the key issues on the agenda in the european union both in terms of economic criteria and in terms of the judiciary, the schengen system, to be ready when the moment comes in the accession process to become members of the european union. i support this report, of course.
written. − as a new eu member, croatia can best attest to the importance of pre-accession funds on the path to european union membership. i consider the enlargement policy of the eu to be one of the most successful and productive policies of the eu. it must also continue to be seen, in relation to all western balkan countries, on the basis of their individual achievements, as the best option for long-term stability in the region. croatia therefore cooperates closely with its neighbours on their european path. the commission shall, inter alia, through regional and cross-border cooperation projects resulting from the ipa. i am therefore particularly pleased that the appropriations are being allocated for the next financial period 2014-2020, for a broad list of purposes. in addition to the financial benefits, the provision of these funds promotes european values and builds a democratic and prosperous society.
written. − i support the establishment of the ipa ii instrument because a specific financial instrument is very much needed to continue to support the eu's enlargement policy. the new instrument emphasises the values of freedom, democracy, equality, the rule of law, human rights and makes the candidate countries ready to implement the acquis at the time of their accession. in this respect, i welcome the cooperation programme and the provision of support and assistance to candidate and potential candidate countries for membership through the dissemination of experiences on the integration process. the eu-croatia association council (eu-croatia) is a forum for the exchange of information and best practices on the eu's accession process. i particularly welcome the support for cross-border cooperation with a view to promoting good neighbourly relations. the territory of croatia is, due to its geographical position, almost entirely eligible for participation in cross-border programmes with regions of neighbouring countries financed from european union funds. of the 21 croatian counties, 18 are acceptable because they directly border neighbouring countries. 6 crossborder programmes are being implemented under the eu instrument for pre-accession assistance: 3 with eu member states slovenia, hungary and italy (adriatic crossborder cooperation) and 3 with potential candidate countries for eu membership bih, montenegro and serbia. croatia also participates in two transnational cooperation programmes - the south east european area and the mediterranean programme.
written. - i welcome the report by mr libor roucek on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on preaccession instruments. as a former rapporteur of the european parliament for the republic of croatia, my colleague rouček was able to witness firsthand the process of preparing for membership of the newest member of the european union and, on the basis of that experience, to prepare this high-quality report on pre-accession assistance instruments for albania, bosnia and herzegovina, iceland, kosovo, montenegro, serbia, turkey and macedonia. in line with the new approach to enlargement policy, the use of pre-accession funds must be focused on the priority areas of building the rule of law and developing market economies in the candidate countries. according to the latest commission report, no country in south-east europe currently meets the criteria for being considered a functioning market economy. pre-accession funds are therefore needed to help implement the necessary structural reforms, which of course must be accompanied by political reforms.
madam president, the committee has recommended that the european parliament should amend the commission's proposal. the partnership instrument should support measures that respond flexibly but effectively to challenges in relations between the european union and third countries. the european union's interests must be extended, but so must the cooperation with global partners. of course economic cooperation is something we need to promote, to establish mechanisms for economic cooperation. this would facilitate access to foreign markets and provide business opportunities that can be exploited in particular by small and medium-sized enterprises. it is also necessary to work on the image and branding of the european union through public diplomacy and education and general opening up to society. the promotion of economic cooperation, people-to-people cooperation, so-called networking, joint research efforts in the field of renewable energy sources, and the encouragement of joint projects are just some of the aspects which could have a positive impact on the european union's relations with third countries. i would like to thank my colleague, antonio lopez, for this report.
madam president, i support this report because the european union should be clear about its objectives and policies. the external policy is definitely one of the most important aspects of the union's external action. the policy must therefore be consistent, always taking account of global developments, but without abandoning its fundamental objectives. the european parliament, as the only institution of the union elected by the people, is responsible for guiding the union's strategic objectives. today kiev is a clear proof that every government, even a supranational one, must ask itself: "what does the people want?" europe can learn from the current events in ukraine why the authorities have decided what and why the people are divided on the issue of cooperation with europe. we must be able to promote the union's values in the most acceptable way and always to our mutual benefit. the ultimate objective of the partnership for cooperation with third countries is not only the benefit of the union but also the advancement of the other party, i.e. the third countries, because movements in third countries often affect the union itself and its citizens. the responsibility must be mutual.
madam president, the partnership instrument for cooperation with third countries is one of the european union's instruments that enhances our cooperation with developed and economically influential actors on the global stage. it enables us to put into practice the principle of the european security strategy, which we established back in 2003, and that is the principle of implementing a policy of effective multilateralism, whether it is joint action with individual countries in multilateral fora or cooperation between the european union and other international organisations. it is of the utmost importance to us, for the international dimension of the implementation of our europe 2020 strategy, and in this way we are trying to implement the basic guidelines and principles of european policy, including energy policy and competitiveness and research policy, both in economic terms and in terms of climate change.
written. - i support this report above all because it extends the application of the instruments that have led to a reduction in conflict, better crisis management and peace building in the world. namely, the mechanisms that expire at the end of this year are extended for the next 6 years. as these mechanisms have proven to be of vital importance for peace in the european environment and, consequently, for security within the eu itself, since their introduction in 2007, i welcome this decision. the value of this european action is in the help we give to others, but also, with a budget of €13 billion and insisting on the principle of 'more for more', the eu is positioning itself on the international stage as an important factor in maintaining peace and security. the republic of croatia began to contribute actively to eu peacekeeping missions and operations as a partner country during the accession negotiations. we are currently involved in three missions: eu navfor atalanta, eupol afghanistan and eulex kosovo.
written. − i support the partnership instrument which is designed to make up for the shortcomings of the previous instrument for cooperation with industrialised and other high income countries and territories. i welcome the important role of this instrument, which is to contribute to promoting not only the interests of the eu, but also mutual interests and relations with third countries. it is in the union's interest to develop relations and dialogue with countries where we have a strategic interest in relation to foreign policy and the international economy. the objective of cooperation and partnership is of paramount importance and for this reason i support the union's desire to develop relations and build partnerships with third countries. i would like to stress the importance of regional cooperation. in this regard, croatia participates in several regional cooperation programmes, namely the south east european cooperation process (seecp), the regional cooperation council (rcc), the central european initiative (cei), the adriatic-ionian initiative (aii) and the alpine-adriatic working group. also, croatia has an active role in other regional initiatives and organizations, such as the regional initiative on migration, asylum and refugees (marri), the law enforcement centre in south-eastern europe, the initiative for prevention and preparedness of disasters in south-eastern europe, the danube commission and the sava commission.
written. - i welcome the report by mr antonio lópez isturiz on the partnership instruments for cooperation with third countries. the european union must recognise new modalities of cooperation with countries that are increasingly falling short of the category of developing countries and are becoming very active players in the global economy. in defining european policy towards countries such as china, russia, india, brazil, as well as other latin american countries, it is necessary to develop, alongside the economic dimension, mechanisms to strengthen political cooperation and promote the values of democracy, the rule of law and respect for human dignity and human rights in general. in this respect, this report contributes to the development of such a common foreign and security policy, which will also have at its disposal concrete partnership instruments.
madam president, i want to say that i supported this committee's proposal because the development, consolidation and promotion of democratic principles are the foundation of any society. the most important objective is the respect for fundamental human rights as set out in the declaration of human rights, their protection, promotion and implementation through the various regional and international instruments in this field. it is necessary to strengthen civil society and provide protection to victims of repression, but it is also necessary to stand up for human rights defenders. the commission should also be able to provide information on the results of the evaluation of the implementation of the union's macro-financial assistance to third countries. there also has to be professionalism during the election process which has to be brought to a higher level. so democracy at the local level needs to be strengthened, and what also needs to be addressed is promoting equal participation of people with disabilities in decision-making.
madam president, i support the report by mr lambsdorff on the instrument for financing the promotion of democracy and human rights in the world. because it is the respect for human rights and fundamental freedoms that is enshrined as one of the fundamental values in our founding treaties. as part of our external policy, respect for human rights by the countries with which the european union develops relations must be and is a continuing priority both in the contractual relations we have with third countries and in the measures and programmes we implement in the framework of the partnership relations we develop with them. i believe that this regulation, which is now better than it was before, with more than one billion euros over the next seven years, will help us to implement our policy of respecting human rights and cooperation with civil society, with those groups in certain countries where the levels and standards of human rights protection are not the same as in the european union. i think we also saw recently, when our saharov prize winner malala was in this house, how important it is to help democratic processes and human rights around the world.
written. − in order to ensure that civil society promoting democratic values is truly supported by the european union, it is necessary to simplify the administrative procedures that must be followed in order for applicants to receive the financial support they request. companies that need this support often lack the human resources to comply with the increasingly demanding procedures that brussels puts before them. we must aim to make these funds more accessible, while taking into account that financial data must be presented in a transparent manner and accessible at all times. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the eu's financial instruments and to monitor the implementation of the union's financial instruments. the european union won the nobel peace prize precisely because it has, over the past decades, successfully promoted democratic values and helped the countries of the former communist bloc and former military dictatorships in europe to establish democracies, freedom of thought, speech and choice. these values are universal and we must insist on promoting them throughout the european continent.
madam president, the development cooperation financial instrument defines in a certain way the political responsibility of the european union as a club of highly developed and advanced countries and projects the principle of solidarity for those developing countries which are far from our level of economic and social development. the funds provided, 17 billion euros, over the next seven years will enable us to donate our development aid by geographical or thematic partnership with recipient countries. the european union's development aid is so large that we are the world leader in this respect and i believe that in cooperation with other major donors we will enable both poverty eradication and support to developing countries to achieve a higher level of development and thus improve the living standards of their citizens because this, essentially linked to the instrument for strengthening human rights and democracy, is the essence of this kind of external action of the european union.
written. − in view of the global economic and political circumstances, i believe that the establishment of a financial instrument is the right move for the eu to make. the european union's development cooperation will be enhanced by combining a number of previous geographical and thematic mechanisms into one instrument. i think this instrument will reduce poverty in developing countries. sustainable development is also a hugely important issue that we have to deal with more and more. by introducing this instrument and providing functional mechanisms for it, significant progress can be made in developing new technologies, materials and thinking that will bring us closer to the essence of sustainable development. similarly, i believe that this instrument can be a real tool for promoting human rights and respect for the law. human rights violations are a major problem and the means to combat them must be provided. the instrument should also be flexible to cope with current problems, but this should not affect its effectiveness.
madam president, the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up to improve the european union's instruments to help workers who became surplus and was later extended to deal with the technological surplus resulting from structural change and the global economic and financial crisis. the fund provides new opportunities for cooperation and communication between the competent institutions, given that croatia, like other european countries, is affected by the economic crisis. it is therefore of the utmost importance to continue this initiative in order to tackle this problem together. in addition to a general approach and help in caring for workers, i believe that an individual approach is essential in order to understand the needs of each individual and the possibility of retraining. besides the blow of losing a job to the individual and the family, it reflects on all other areas of life. that is why i want to support this initiative and i advocate the implementation of measures from the very moment of receiving the application, consultation with the workers in order to know, encourage and provide visibility to the measures that this fund makes possible.
madam president, the european globalisation adjustment fund is co-financing measures to enable workers who have lost their jobs as a result of the global crisis or global changes in the sector to return to the labour market. during 2012, the fund helped 15,700 displaced workers find new employment opportunities with eur 73,5 million in aid in 11 member states. for croatia, this fund is particularly relevant as the funds can be used for sectors such as shipbuilding for retraining or employing workers. the new 2014-2020 period will see the fund for the first time available to temporary and self-employed workers and young people not in employment, education or training. the amount of assigned revenue in accordance with article 21 (3) of the financial regulation is estimated at eur 100000. the scope of the fund is therefore expanding and the speed and the utilisation will certainly be higher in the 2014-2020 perspective, which is why i support this report and the new regulation.
madam president, i recently put a question to the european commission on this subject, specifically referring to the shipbuilding workers in croatia, who have been declared surplus. shipyards in croatia are now privately owned and workers are treated as the owner wants, and this is helped by the new law that the current social democratic government is trying to install. with unemployment this high and the yard's massive layoffs, the owners are asking for 4,500 permits for foreign workers. as expected, the european commission did not reply to me in any particular way, but i was informed that the amount of 500 million euros had been reduced to 150 million in the hope that this amount would be sufficient. despite the hope, the european union that has been the reason or the incentive for these incentives has not taken any concrete action to mitigate the consequences. besides the shipyards in croatia, numerous other jobs were flooded across europe, but who knows, maybe that was the plan.
written. − the european globalisation adjustment fund was set up as a solidarity project for workers who have lost their jobs as a result of major structural changes in the world market due to globalisation. in principle, i support certain segments of this project, such as aid to dismissed workers, aid to self-employed, and aid to unemployed young people in the amount of 60% co-financing. the most positive segment of the fund, in my view, is the provision that if redundancies occur in a region eligible for the 'youth initiative' programme, this will enable the member state and the region to offer services to an equivalent number of unemployed young people from the neets group. however, i do not consider that the fund is an effective and systematic instrument for reducing the impact of globalisation on the economic crisis. this instrument represents a unilateral solution that does not in any way strengthen active employment measures to enable the eu labour market to support all those who are at risk of becoming unemployed and those who have already become so. i also object to the exclusion of farmers from the category of eligible for co-financing from that fund because of the alleged lack of funds in the fund, as i consider it discriminatory and unfair. i believe that the union can and must offer a much better and more comprehensive solution in this important segment and i therefore abstained.
madam president, i voted in favour of this proposal because i support the commission's intention to create a more transparent exchange of information in data including capital gains, dividends and other financial income in one instrument which will be a common measure for the automatic exchange of information. in times of economic turbulence, it is very important to find a common solution to ensure a transparent and wide exchange of information to help member states avoid tax and tax fraud. in many countries, a large percentage of government revenues is lost to tax evasion and fraud. therefore, i support the implementation of these systemic changes because they will help national budgets and will also help to restore citizens' faith in the efficiency of the tax system both at the national and international level. as the reporter said, a lot is lost every year, a trillion euros. it is very important to establish a common standard in europe that will be in line with european legislation and that will help to prevent such events from happening in the future and to avoid such destructive behaviour.
written. − the problem of tax fraud and tax evasion has dramatically advanced in recent years and has acquired the status of a leading concern within the union and in the world, further aided by the emergence of the economic crisis. as a representative from a member state of the union which unfortunately shows no sign of emerging from the crisis, i share with the rapporteur my deep concern about undeclared and untaxed revenues which result in a significant reduction of state tax revenue and, more importantly, create black market conditions and unfair competition. while i strongly support the move towards a more efficient and effective system of tax collection, i am concerned that the commission's plan is based on intervention in yet another area that is not within the exclusive competence of the union. the commission has also provided a report on the implementation of the directive on the automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation, which is to be submitted to the european parliament and to the council by 31 december 2016. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament and the council should be given the right to adopt a decision on the conclusion of an agreement on the automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation.
madam president, i have endorsed this report. various transitional regulations, including conflict and post-conflict situations, have placed high demands on the european union in its efforts to successfully overcome challenges while preserving human rights and democracy in 2012. in line with this and the european union's annual report on human rights and democracy 2012, which highlights the importance, consistency and flexibility of the european union's policies, and the diversity of the european union's founding principles. in such efforts, it is important that the governments of the member states cooperate well and efficiently with each other and with the european union, in particular the ministries of foreign affairs and european affairs. as croatia is also one of the countries that has gone through a transition, i understand the importance of demanding a flexible policy in different situations, but also encouraging the integration of human rights and democracy and the pursuit of compliance with these european union measures. i welcome all further efforts and i also welcome the amendment we adopted today concerning the release of all political prisoners and a just political solution for western sahara in accordance with a united nations resolution.
madam president, i voted in favour of the report by mr kukana on the annual report on human rights and democracy in the world for 2012. the protection of human rights, the protection of the dignity of every person, is at the very heart of the european union and must be the main guideline in the development of the common foreign policy. today, this means first and foremost the active engagement of the european union in support of ukrainian citizens who have taken to the streets of kiev and other cities across the country in defense of ukraine's freedom, independence and european future. i therefore welcome the deployment of the european parliament delegation to kiev and the active role of the european external action service, as well as high commissioner ashton. a consistent policy of respecting and promoting democratic values also requires a new approach by the european union towards bosnia and herzegovina, where both the constituent minority and citizens who do not belong to any of the constituent peoples in that country are currently discriminated against. the eu must set clear criteria in this respect if it does not want a new crisis in its own backyard.
written. − i support the european union's annual report on the state of human rights and democracy in the world for 2012. it reflects the necessary efforts and policy solutions that the eu institutions are putting in place to promote democracy, human rights, minority rights, the rule of law, freedom of speech and the media at european and third country level. it is of great importance that these values are fundamental and unified for the shaping of future external policies in the institutions of the european union, especially when we take into account the european parliament eurobarometer data where a public opinion survey in the 27 member states in november and december 2012 showed that citizens of the european union continue to consider the protection of human rights as the highest priority of the european union's policy. in the implementation process, it is essential to enable civil society, national parliaments, local authorities and the media to work together in order to ensure the best possible implementation of european human rights provisions in all member states and to ensure genuine ownership of development strategy policies. it is also important to emphasise the role of the european union in the democratic transitions of third countries where more compromise should be sought in situations of cross-border interests in order to achieve as quickly as possible a 'more for more' approach to common policies that will ensure support for democratisation and the protection of human rights as an unquestionable priority of the european union's external policy.
written. − while the european union has received the nobel prize for its contribution to promoting peace and reconciliation, democracy and human rights in europe, it must strive to continue to be a model in the field of human rights for other countries in europe and the world. it must use its proven instruments, such as the eu special representative for human rights, but also introduce new instruments, such as the practice of incorporating legally binding human rights provisions into eu international agreements. the new challenges that have emerged in the world over the past year require a new approach to the european union's foreign policy, and the 'more for more' approach has the potential to be a good diplomatic instrument. within its borders, the union must continue to improve the situation of all minorities, the lgbt community, women's rights and gender equality. this report has confirmed the good practices that the union has had so far, but it has also recognised the enormous potential that lies within it for promoting human rights at the global level in the future, which is why i support it generously.
mr president, as one of the fundamental objectives of the european union is to develop an effective competition policy, allowing member state governments to get involved if markets are not satisfactory to consumers or businesses, and then to promote innovation and uniform standards, i welcome the continuation of cooperation and work in this area. i stress the importance of ensuring stronger cooperation between the commission and national competition authorities in the application of the principle of subsidiarity and the allocation of cases to the appropriate institution. the aim is to enable the public sector to increase the number of independent regulatory bodies that will ensure that labour markets operate in a fair and non-discriminatory manner. we must remove all the obstacles that have impeded progress, and i would particularly like to stress the transport sector, rail and air transport. the first step is to harmonise national laws in order to avoid incorrect and incomplete application of european union rules. i voted for this year's report.
madam president, competition policy and the smooth functioning of the single market are key to tackling the crisis, boosting growth and achieving sustainable development. the commission notes that the commission has not provided any evidence that the aid granted to the beneficiary of the measure is compatible with the internal market. the report highlights the need to modernise state aid so that limited public resources are more specifically targeted at fostering growth and development. in the context of modernising state aid, priority shall be given to aid for research and development, aid to facilitate access to financial instruments for smes, aid for the development of the digital economy, and aid to attract investment in less-developed regions. the 2012 report is comprehensive, complete and informative, with a strong emphasis on the need to modernise support, which is why i supported it today.
madam president, i also supported the 2012 report on the european union's competition policy, which was prepared by my colleague ramon tremosa and balcells on the basis of the european commission's report. what is of course important is that competition policy is at the very heart of the functioning of the european union, at the very heart of the functioning of the european union's single market. it is the basis for strengthening competitiveness for economic growth, for exploiting all the opportunities that the freedoms of the common market offer us. in this context, i consider particularly relevant the part concerning the effects of the economic and financial crisis and competition policy, in particular on state aid in the banking and financial sector, which has constituted a significant part of the activities in the past period. this is precisely the aspect that should be of additional interest to the european commission's activities and it seems to me that future activities should be focused on ensuring that competition policy, or the protection of competition, drives economic growth in the european union. for the croatian competition agency as a national authority, i consider that it is ready to join the european union and has adapted over the past few years and will be a very active participant within the network of national competition agencies.
written. − i support the european parliament's report on the 2012 annual report on the european union's competition policy. although the report does not yet take into account the state of competition in croatia, it provides valuable insight into the requirements that the european union will place on member states in future periods. the commission has also taken into account the fact that the commission has not yet adopted a new approach to the assessment of the compatibility of state aid with the internal market. in 2012, croatia reduced the amount of state aid paid by 3% and this trend continues due to the increase in the possibility of financing from european union funds. state aid in croatia is increasingly geared towards increasing investment activity and sustainable growth. the commission has also received comments from the applicant, which has not provided any evidence to support its claim. i welcome the rapporteur's call on the commission to make efforts to ensure the competitiveness of the european shipbuilding sector by promoting shipbuilding in the eu, which is facing increasing competition in the international environment. finally, i support the implementation of the commission's antitrust measures in the energy sector. croatia with its liberalised energy market can serve as an example to the eu accession countries as a successful example of adaptation to the acqis.
in writing. - 20 years after the birth of the single market, many european consumers are still seriously harmed by anti-competitive practices in a variety of economic sectors. the text voted on today welcomes the commission's report on eu competition policy and its contribution to eliminating barriers, abuse of dominant positions, collusive agreements and distorted state aid in favour of a single market which takes account of the evolution of the global economy, without neglecting the necessary convergence of competition rules.
written. − i wholeheartedly support this proposal, which promotes the ideal of fair competition as the driving force and bearer of the idea of a common market for the union. the rapporteur welcomes the contribution of competition policy in removing barriers and abuses of dominant positions and believes that it is a driver of economic growth and job creation, especially in the crisis times we are witnessing, which i can only agree with. i also agree with the finding that too many sectors are still significantly divided by national borders and artificial barriers in the public or private sector and that competition policy has a major role to play in combating such fragmentation and in creating a level playing field in all sectors of the single market. i also support the call for transparency in the work of national and competition regulators by requiring that all relevant case and official decision data be clearly visible and accessible on the internet through an open database.
written. − the report aims to provide milk producers in mountain, inaccessible and special-care areas with a specific framework for operating within the milk quota system. the commission notes that the milk quotas in the united kingdom are not set at the same level as in the other member states. in this context we can also observe the area of gorsky kotar in croatia. in gorsky kotar, the population fell by a whopping 12 percent from 26,120 to 23,011 in the 2001 census. population density per square mile is 18 and falling, while the national average is 75. households have declined by 7 percent. such worrying data indicate the need for specific mechanisms to improve the economic conditions in such areas in order to prevent depopulation. since people in such areas live mainly from agriculture, i think this initiative is welcome.
written. − the abolition of production quotas in the dairy sector will lead to market disturbances which the european union will try to mitigate by a gradual increase in quotas by 2015. the liberalisation of the market will certainly have a stronger effect on rural areas that lack the capacity and mechanisms to adapt to demand, which i see as a danger for croatian producers and express my concern especially for croatian mountain areas, poor areas and remote regions. i therefore support the rapporteur's proposal which seeks to protect dairy farming in sensitive areas of the european union. i believe that farmers must be guaranteed a sustainable working process even after the abolition of quotas because of the cost disadvantages of production in these areas. the commission should also be able to request the member states to provide information on the implementation of the measures provided for in this regulation. it is important to focus on the most sensitive areas, in particular because the cap has not fully identified potential difficulties.
written. − as milk production is often the only source of income in rural and remote areas, it is of the utmost importance for the local community to prevent possible market changes after 2015. in order to secure the future of the agricultural sector, while avoiding the inevitable exodus of the population from rural areas, adequate steps need to be taken at european level, and i therefore support the initiative to ensure a decent and dignified life for farmers and to secure the future of rural areas. in croatia, many measures have been taken to prevent depopulation and the departure of young and educated people from such areas, including islands, and implementing regulations are adopted to ensure the survival of such areas throughout the year, not just during the summer months. in croatia, the under-exploitation of such areas is a major problem, because the entire interior is extremely fertile and rich land, but despite all efforts, imports still exceed exports, and these areas are not sufficiently exploited. that is why i am in favour of this initiative, which aims to make the best use of natural resources, to create new employment opportunities and thus to achieve economic growth.
written. − despite the continuing support and programmes of european countries in attempts to prevent and reverse the consequences of climate, economic and other factors, in the areas most affected by such crises, namely the horn of africa and the sahara, further efforts are needed. therefore, i fully support this initiative to build resilience and reduce disaster risk in developing countries (dcs). as croatia has also suffered the disastrous consequences of war suffering, i know how necessary and important such assistance is, and therefore i am fully in favour of the implementation of this initiative. i continue, as a member of the committee on the environment and human health, to stress the importance of raising awareness in europe and working with governments of developing countries to put the initiative into action. the commission will continue to support the resilience and sustainability initiative for developing countries, as the proposal aims at continuous investment, as opposed to the current short-term investment in the form of humanitarian aid. the commission has also concluded that the aid is compatible with the internal market within the meaning of article 107 (3) (c) of the treaty.
written. - in the spirit of current events and attempts to restrict human and minority rights in the republic of croatia, my country of origin, i would like to refer to the report on women with disabilities. insisting on gender equality implies a sustained effort to achieve full gender equality. women, although not a numerically minority group in many societies, are still not effectively equal in society because of deeply rooted stereotypes and traditional social models. within this social group, there is a segmentation of a group that needs extra attention in protecting their rights, namely women with disabilities who are exposed to multiple discrimination in society. in times of economic crisis, when many costs are being restricted, the financing of public health and social services for women with disabilities must not be limited, and investment in all forms of education that enable women with disabilities to participate actively in society, including the right to employment, must not be reduced. the principle of an inclusive society is the only approach that brings long-term progress and well-being for all citizens, and therefore we need to continuously strive for the secure and functional realisation of the rights of persons with disabilities.
written. - as many as 80 million disabled people live in the european union. of these, 46% are women, which is 16% of the total number of women in the eu. in croatia, women account for 39.8% of disabled persons and, as in europe, face the problem of double discrimination. women with disabilities have lower levels of education than men with disabilities, which results in a disadvantageous position in the labour market. this is the result of untrained teachers, poor technical conditions in schools and, above all, low investment in science and education by eu member states. the root of the problem lies in the position of such women in the family and in society. they are often abused, neglected, socially isolated, and denied quality health care. the integration of disabled people into society is a long-term process that needs to be systematically addressed, and that is why i welcome the special aspect of this report concerning the care of girls with disabilities.
written. - i want to commend this report which deals with a very sensitive issue. i want to look specifically at my country where the situation is not much different from other countries in terms of discrimination. women and girls with disabilities are three times more likely to be discriminated against than women and girls without disabilities. statistics also show that in croatia one in three women with disabilities is subjected to violence, most often in the family circle. international and national conventions, and all the reports of the european institutions can remain just a letter on paper if we do not actively involve people with disabilities in the community. in croatia, a pilot project is currently being implemented where about 700 of the most severely disabled people have a personal assistant and are thus involved in the community. the austerity measures implemented in many european countries must not affect such projects. women with disabilities should be more involved in political and public life. croatia was an example in this regard until two years ago, as it had two women with disabilities as mps in the croatian parliament. unfortunately, we no longer have a single person with a disability in the croatian parliament, which definitely needs to change if we want the parliament to be a place where all social groups are equally represented.
written. − there are 518 389 persons with disabilities in croatia, of whom 40% are women. of the total number of disabled persons employed in croatia, only 33 percent are women. they are disadvantaged even before entering the labour market, since most of them have a lower level of education. women and girls with disabilities can have equal rights and opportunities with valid gender policies and access to public administration, education, medical care, information and movement in the physical and social environment without barriers. with the right and good use of european funds, these objectives can be achieved. in addition to a better and more quality life for women and girls with disabilities, their full economic and social participation is crucial for the europe 2020 strategy to succeed in achieving smart, sustainable and inclusive growth, which benefits society as a whole. i consider this report to be an excellent step towards improving the position of women with disabilities and therefore i fully support it.
written. - given that 16% of the total female population in the eu is made up of women with disabilities, it is important to pay special attention to their care, and i therefore welcome the report by my colleague angelike werthmann. special emphasis is placed on the media, whose role should be to raise public awareness of the needs of women with disabilities. furthermore, young women and girls need to be informed of their rights so that they can be as independent as possible. the commission should also be able to provide information on the impact of the new rules on the functioning of the internal market, in particular on the impact of the new rules on the functioning of the internal market and on the impact of the new rules on the functioning of the internal market. croatia also recognised the need for better access to education and work for people with disabilities. the register of persons with disabilities records the parameters for 519 181 persons with disabilities, of whom 206 550 (39,8%) are women with disabilities. the project 'promoting more intensive inclusion of persons with disabilities in the labour market' is one of a series of projects implemented by croatia under the ipa's component iv 'development of human potential to provide support in overcoming communication barriers'. financial support was granted to 13 projects of associations of deaf and hard of hearing people through which 17 interpreters/translators were employed in sign language, which certainly has a positive impact on women with disabilities.
written. − i welcome this directive because the right to equality is one of the fundamental human rights and therefore the human being should not be perceived in terms of his difficulties but in terms of the achievements in the different roles that life brings. with more than 15% of the european population being people with disabilities, statistics show the importance of taking action and creating a friendly environment on equal terms. i support every step forward in terms of understanding and providing equal opportunities, challenges, advancement, and providing equal opportunity for women with disabilities as well as women without disabilities. in croatia, this issue is given special attention in terms of the integration of women with disabilities into society, and i therefore support the initiative to provide for a longer period of maternity leave due to special circumstances, in order to enable them to live a comfortable life.
written. - this report has given me mixed impressions, as i believe that all people with disabilities are equal in terms of the hardships their disability creates for them and that they should all be given equal protection. while i warmly welcome the part of the resolution which advocates the protection of women with disabilities against violence, sexual abuse and the harsh punishment of such acts, i must condemn the part of the report which stresses the need to support women of immigrant origin in order to develop their skills and potential through vocational education because i am strongly opposed to any ideological approach to this issue. so i abstained in the vote.
mr president, as the representative of croatia, the last country to join the union, i pay particular attention to this issue. for many years, the croatian public has been left in doubt: should croatia join the european union? now that we have become a full member state, it is time to ask the question: what is the union for croats? i will never support a federal europe, a superstate that will destroy centuries of identity and the desire for freedom. a europe of sovereign nation states has proved to be the best solution, and the well-known rule is that everything that works well does not need to be fixed. the model of cooperation between sovereign european states should be perfected and not replaced by the proven utopia of a united european state, which is increasingly openly contradicted on the wings of supposedly greater efficiency, ignoring the will of the citizens of the european union. the rapporteur, the parliament and the commission must finally understand that we are not making europe to our own standards, but to the standards of the citizens. it would therefore be fair to ask them to express their views on the union they really want in a referendum.
mr president, i welcome the report of colleagues gualtieri and trzaskowski and this is a very ambitious document. in this light, i would quote my colleague trzaskowski: 'europe must increasingly deal with big things and less and less with small things.' this report definitely reflects that view. it is of the utmost importance to maintain the european legal path, and this can only be achieved by differentiation. different approaches to different countries are necessary, but always with a proportionate effect. because in order to survive, the european legal order must first of all be consistent, and consistency is preceded by binding measures for all and, of course, mutual responsibility. different countries progress differently, each with its own specific path, but we should all meet on the common european path. a differentiated system will almost certainly also have a positive effect on the participation of its citizens, which is currently a major problem for europe. representation is one of the most important foundations of democracy and that's why we representatives are at all times obliged to take care of the image, i would say the strength of this house of ours where we sit. the european parliament is the only elected body of the european union and as such should always be given the corresponding powers in relation to other bodies. but differentiation itself must never become the goal. it is conceived exclusively as a means and as such must remain a means for more unity, a means for more european added value and ultimately a means for more europe.
mr president, first of all i would like to congratulate my co-rapporteurs on a job well done. there is a tendency to interpret the european union with its institutions as not being effective enough because it is not only the countries that are in the eurozone and therefore additional institutional involvement is needed. the european parliament, as one of the institutions, is the parliament of all 28 member states, and equally the parliament of the citizens of all 28 countries, and cannot take decisions that concern the euro area alone. we need to reconcile the principles of differentiation and integration by finding the best possible solution, we need to decide whether we will treat equal situations equally, or unequally, unequal situations. we want to create an integrated europe. article 136 of the eu treaty provides the basis for cooperation and flexibility. in addition, attention should be paid to the sustainability of the european social union in the long term, as well as to strengthening the social dimension of emu through the social pact and the building of a social pillar, emphasising that employment and social policy are the real policies of the european union.

vice-president of the commission. − mr president, honourable members, i also would like to welcome the report of mr lamassoure on the relations between the european parliament and the institutions representing the national governments, and in particular the european council. close cooperation between all the institutions of the european union in the pursuit of the treaty objective is vital. the commission looks positively upon the initiatives to further improve on these working relations within the framework of the treaties. that includes of course any initiatives to strengthen democratic legitimacy and accountability. in particular, the commission welcomes the plenary debates in parliament prior to european council meetings. the organisation and structure of these and other debates is the prerogative of the parliament. since the report touches upon the annual state of the union address by the president of the commission, i would like to emphasise that this address reflects the role and prerogatives of the president of the commission and his unique relationship with the european parliament. in the future you will be electing the commission president. i therefore find it natural that he or she once a year addresses this house with a wide-ranging analysis of the state of the union – far beyond the annual legislative programme. i think that we have come to appreciate this as one of the high points of the year in terms of our special relationship.
mr president, it is clear from all the evidence that the council very often acts as a kind of government that is not in heaven or on earth, a government that answers to no one. unfortunately, citizens know very little about the institutions of the european union, and if any are at the centre of interest, it is this parliament, and many decisions are obviously taken elsewhere. i would say that informing the public is also extremely important in establishing effective relations between the institutions of the european union. institutional changes are needed to strengthen communication and mutual respect of decisions, so that decisions in one body are not repealed in another body without any specific reason, without explanation or even without notification. written dialogue is not constructive, nor is it wanted, nor is it much needed. the european parliament is the only legally elected institution. the others are set. we should be here now to answer the question of whether we want more europe, a democratic europe, or this is enough. if that's enough, let's stop this discussion.
mr president, first of all, i would like to congratulate mr lamassoure, chairman of the committee on budgets and a member of the committee on constitutional affairs with a very long experience in this economic terms has contributed to the strengthening of the trend towards intergovernmental cooperation at the level of the european union. this was what i thought was natural in terms of the speed of reaction, but it is certainly not a good trend in terms of strengthening the democratic legitimacy of the european institutions, and i am thinking in particular of the full involvement of the european parliament in all key decision-making processes. the european council has, that is, entered the legislative sphere of the council's own activities, but i think that the activities of both the mr president, i would like to thank mr lamassoure for his good analysis of the situation and for his good analysis of the relations between parliament and the council after the treaty of lisbon, which has been in force for almost four years. the fact is that the council of europe has the status of an institution, but it is also clear that it does not have a legislative role, but because of the situation, because of the crisis, it has been meeting more often and little by little, in my opinion, it has gone beyond the scope of its role. we as parliamentarians definitely want to consume what the treaty of lisbon has given us and that is why i agree with these five points made by mr lamasourre concerning the council meetings which should precede the debates in the european parliament. i also agree with the calendar of meetings that the council will meet between plenary sessions and not at the same time. it would also be good if the state of the union were to be debated every year in the presence of the council and the commission. also, why don't we have an action plan? i understand that sometimes it's not possible because of the current emergency situations, but you should have some kind of a tentative calendar. and finally, the latest innovation, which is the democratization of the european union in such a way that the president of the commission is elected after the elections and next year in may we will be able to test in practice for the first time the question of how the commission will be set up. so what we should conclude is that we should formalise the relations between the council and parliament and that we should finally apply the spirit and the letter of the lisbon treaty, improve the relations between the council and parliament, that is to say give parliament the role that we really got with the lisbon treaty. thank you again to mr lamasourré for his report.
mr president, in view of the detailed consideration of certain situations and problems and proposals for their solution in the european parliament's relations with the institutions representing national governments, allow me to express my support for this motion for a resolution, particularly in relation to the following: it was necessary to take account of the temptation of heads of state or government to resort to intergovernmental means which jeopardises the 'community method', in breach of the treaties. it was also necessary to take into account the fact that the lack of a structured dialogue between parliament and the european council prevented parliament from fully fulfilling its role as co-legislator established by the treaties. i welcome the improvement and strengthening of working relations between the european council and parliament; and the way in which the same can be achieved by interinstitutional agreement. i also support the view that each european council meeting should be preceded by a debate before the european parliament, which would allow the adoption of a resolution, and finally that the president of parliament should participate in the european council when interinstitutional issues are being dealt with.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commission representatives, mr vice-president, i welcome the report by mr alain lamassoure, which highlights the importance and role of the european parliament as a body that is necessarily involved in the negotiation and decision-making processes. parliament, as a body whose members are directly elected by the citizens of the member states, by the nature of its function must be transparent, consistent and fully involved in its processes which have a legislative dimension, irrespective of the body from which the legislative initiative comes. this certainly implies the presence of the president of the european parliament at all council meetings on the subject of the union's interinstitutional relations. only in this way can the principles and standards of decision-making, which have their basis in the treaty of lisbon, be fully realised.
mr president, i believe that we are facing a very important report and i am convinced that it is essentially strengthening the foundations of the european union. the freedoms on which we built the european union, namely the free movement of people, goods, services and capital, here particularly in the area of financial transactions of all citizens, will be further strengthened by the adoption of this report. the need to use bank accounts has long been unavoidable and all the more worrying is the fact that almost 58 million citizens of the union do not have one, which is why it is good that this report also proposes better financial education for our citizens, which will certainly strengthen the consumer's sense of security when making decisions about the choice of financial services. the report also supports the development of innovation in retail financial services, and we know that innovation increases both competitiveness and development. as we want a union without borders and barriers, this report is one of the key elements to move towards that goal. our workers, students, entrepreneurs, even our closest collaborators, our assistants, our trainees face problems of opening accounts in other member states and various unnecessary and incomprehensible fees. it should be the opposite, it should be efficient, orderly and fast. the act of removing this obstacle is important for the whole of the european union, and more importantly, it is important for the commitment to its very essence, which is freedom.
written. mr president, i consider it extremely important that the rules on payment accounts fully respect the principle of protecting all consumers, regardless of the purchasing power of the individual user. in the european single market, it is important to ensure at the same time the development of innovative technologies which are today a prerequisite for the efficient functioning of payment transactions. in this context, the development of security technologies in the area of electronic commerce is also important, which implies the continuous improvement of security protection tools. in order to fully equalise access to the single market for each individual user, it is necessary to insist on a unified, harmonised and uniform set of conditions and information on payment transaction fees.
mr. president, i would like to welcome commissioner mimic. i want to say that i fully support this initiative because i believe that the european union should play a leading role in developing the blue economy, but also in preserving coastal and marine ecosystems. this can only be achieved through integrated and coherent maritime spatial management. with some 88 million people working in the eu's maritime regions, sustainable growth is needed and i believe that this will certainly increase. the maritime domain is still not sufficiently explored and by investing in the maritime economy, such as the exploitation of oil, gas and other raw materials and other resources, maritime and coastal tourism and other similar sectors, the union has the potential to ensure competitiveness in the
