mr president, as i myself come from a coastal area, i am aware of the challenges we face in reconciling maritime and coastal activities and ensuring sustainable development. i believe that integrated coastal management is the key to the survival of coastal areas in croatia because we cannot allow the further development of tourism to disrupt other activities that people live off of during the summer season. the achievement of some of the union's main objectives in this regard, such as encouraging the development of marine energy sources, promoting the accessibility of ports and enhancing the sustainable development of the fisheries and aquaculture sector, would bring great benefits to this community and thus to the republic of croatia. coordination between the competent authorities of the member states is necessary for the management of the coastal zone to be truly integrated. however, i stress that this issue should remain entirely within the competence of the member states without any possibility for the union to influence spatial planning in the member states from a central level. i also believe that we will only achieve integrated mediterranean governance by declaring exclusive economic zones in the mediterranean member states, because the current state of reduced control leaves too much room for those who do not put sustainable development first. we must finally put an end to them and give the member states the opportunity to protect our resources and secure the future of coastal areas by establishing exclusive economic zones in coordination with each other.
mr president, commissioner mimica, i am pleased that i too, as someone who is originally from the adriatic coast and partly from the islands, can speak today on this very directive which concerns maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal management. first of all, i would like to congratulate our colleague, rapporteur meißner, on her excellent report. the fact that up to 50% of the european union's population lives in coastal areas speaks volumes about the importance of member states, with the support of the european level, the support of the european union, planning spatial planning in the maritime area and managing maritime areas. it is also important that that cooperation is appropriately decentralised in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, where member states have a key role in decision-making. it is also important that coastal management strategies also take into account the use of natural resources, in particular energy, infrastructure development, energy, ports, maritime works, take into account fisheries, take into account aquaculture and ecosystem management, and their protection in coastal sites that are sometimes vulnerable to climate change. for croatia, which has 1244 islands, 50 of which are inhabited, this directive could help us to focus on the development of our islands and ports, especially since we have found on the basis of the last list that there is even a trend of population increase on the islands, where we have as many as 344 settlements, and i think it is important that this directive is part of the broader efforts for economic growth and development of the european union member states.
mr president, i would like to congratulate rapporteur meißner and to welcome commissioner mimica. the demand for maritime and coastal space is growing, but this space is often used in an uncoordinated way. that is why i support the initiative to discuss maritime spatial planning and integrated coastal management, an extremely important topic for croatia whose coastline is over 5000 km long with more than 1000 islands. most eu countries do not have legislation defining integrated maritime spatial management, but rely on regional and sectoral strategies. the croatian legislation does not contain a policy of integrated coastal management either, but despite this, the territory of croatia is often covered by european projects, such as blue growth, which promote integrated management. an important step towards achieving integrated governance in croatia is the adoption of a macro-regional adriatic strategy in 2014, which will in part form the backbone of our maritime integrated governance. the adoption of the adriatic strategy will provide the basis for the sustainable development of maritime and coastal economic activities in the adriatic sea. these are maritime transport, tourism and fisheries, and there are opportunities to attract foreign investment such as the construction of an lng terminal in the northern adriatic.
mr president, i support this approach in any case, but i would like to speak from another angle, since it is all about funding and commissioner mimica himself said that we should support an innovative financial mechanism. i'll give you an example. we're all advocating for new green spaces and for parks and for integrated management of those spaces to save the globe from this situation that's happening with climate change. i'm aware that it would create new jobs in construction, that there would be great health benefits and so on. but it's all about the money. i was mayor for a long time, so you always had to build the infrastructure that was needed for basic living, water, sewage and so on. and at the same time, we were a city friend to the city of monterey, california. i'm not saying this is a repeat, so i've chosen this way of discussing it on purpose. they demolished an entire city block to build a park. so the city has to be rich enough, the local government and the self-government, the cities, the municipalities have to have enough money to be able to use and to be able to invest in this so-called green infrastructure. it's not possible without the means. we have said here that we have a regional development fund, but it is nice to talk about this at the resolution level, but we need to find a way how this can be implemented and how this can be a priority policy for cities, municipalities and regions. otherwise, if we only discuss this at the european parliament level and do not find mechanisms for implementation, green infrastructure will remain a dead letter on paper.
mr. chairman, i would like to welcome commissioner rhen, mr. draghi, and congratulate him on his hard work and quality. i thank the rapporteur, mr pittelli. the role of the european central bank in the context of the situation in the euro area, but also in the european union as a whole, is of particular importance, and the european central bank is expected to take further measures aimed at creating favourable conditions for economic growth, job creation and financial stability. the euro area recorded a decline in gross domestic product of 0,6% in 2012 and an estimated 0,4% in 2013. the commission also notes that the commission has not provided any information on the volume of imports from the prc during the investigation period. in particular, the need for joint action by member states through the coordination of economic, fiscal and financial policies was felt during 2012 in order to avoid unsustainable movements in individual countries that also threaten the monetary union as a whole. this is why it is important to involve the european central bank in the european semester mechanism, in the formulation of recommendations to member states, which indicate the structural reforms to be implemented. a common obstacle to growth and development is the availability of sources of finance to smes, which are the drivers and engines of growth and new employment, as we have heard from your presentations. this is one of the risks for achieving growth and development in the republic of croatia. in addition to ensuring the availability of credit, the interest rates at which they obtain these credits, which vary significantly across member states, are also an important issue. this creates inequalities in the single market and puts undertakings at a disadvantage. it is of the utmost importance that in the coming periods we find ways to respond to this challenge in all member states, not just within the euro area.
mr. chairman, preventing tax evasion and tax avoidance is extremely important for several reasons. tax evasion and tax avoidance distort the single market and threaten fair competition, reduce budget revenues and have a negative impact on the fair distribution of revenues. they create a mistrust of the state and its institutions and a sense of insecurity. in the context of fiscal consolidation, governments cut spending, some take drastic measures of layoffs, wage cuts, pension cuts and social benefits, deliberately impoverishing the population because they have no other choice. on the revenue side, they're introducing new taxes and increasing existing ones. these measures will not drive growth and development. at the same time, billions of euros of existing taxes remain unpaid. in view of the above, it is of the utmost importance to address the issues of preventing tax evasion and tax avoidance not only at a strategic level but also in a strategic manner. that is precisely what this resolution seeks and that is why i fully support it. the commission should be able to assess the impact of the aid on the environment and the environment protection of the union's forests, including the impact of the aid on the environment. it is also particularly important to encourage the exchange of knowledge and experience in addition to the exchange of data, and i particularly support this, as the resolution calls for the commission to publish examples of good practice on the prevention of tax evasion and avoidance on the internet, which is very important.
mr. president, paying taxes is an important segment of civic responsibility and i personally consider it an act of true patriotism. but i want to point out that in addition to the civic responsibility to the state there is a reverse relationship in which the state must be careful not to harm its own citizens. excessive taxation is a direct act of aggression by the central government against the financial freedom of citizens and as such it directly encourages tax evasion and avoidance. i believe the only logical course of action is to reduce the tax burden and to punish those who refuse to pay the reduced tax. this would make tax evasion unprofitable and encourage some who do not pay taxes today to give the government what it owes. in times of crisis, when tax collection as a method of financial consolidation of the state is gaining additional importance, we would do a service to our citizens and our states by such a measure.
mr president, of course the member states must be involved in the fight against tax evasion. it's a criminal offense in every country and should be prosecuted as such. particular pressure must be put on commercial banks that protect such criminals under the principle of banking secrecy. on the other hand, member states must approach the problem in the same way. the citizens of a member state cannot be allowed to go unquestioned about how they acquired the property they own, but it is taxed and legalized. property that has no provenance is the result of some criminal activity, which should be confiscated, not taxed. if this is not the case in all member states, the call for cooperation is meaningless.

"the president interrupted the speaker.)
mr president, i have already stressed several times in my addresses to this house the importance of innovation for growth and jobs throughout the european union. the kadenbach report makes a good suggestion to turn to innovation in one of the most important areas of human activity, namely environmental innovation, which in our time is and will be the key to achieving smart and sustainable growth and constant investment in new jobs. here i want to recognize the strong potential that my country has in this area and i deeply hope that we in croatia will also know how to recognize the importance of economic benefits in this area of environmental innovation, especially in small and medium-sized enterprises where there are obvious opportunities for new specialized jobs in this field. in the difficult economic times in which many countries of the european union find themselves, particularly those in our east and our south, environmental innovation, investment in renewable energy sources, is a refreshment that we should wholeheartedly support. and that is why i am very happy to support this excellent report because it sets a good direction for the strategy of smart, stable and sustainable development.
in writing. − the council and parliament agreed to increase the number of judges, yet we need to find a solution in the second part of the proposal for a regulation that will best represent the views of the member states when choosing the selection criteria and the number of additional judges to be appointed to the general court. a decision is needed that will create structural solutions which will ease the general courtʼs workload and in turn help diminish the number of pending cases. though there is debate on the exact number of judges and how they are to be appointed, there is nevertheless an urgent need for the number of general court judges to be increased if we are both to prevent the current strain on the rule of law and to safeguard it. i am in full support of the rapporteur’s opinion where she states that a solution is needed urgently as quickly providing additional judges for the general court is surely more important than the question of the selection method used to appoint them.
written. − the increasingly difficult functioning of the general court, caused by the expansion of its jurisdiction and thus the number of cases, calls into question the purpose of its existence. such an important institution that cannot do its job properly becomes a hindrance and creates a sense of mistrust and insecurity among citizens. in order to avoid this and thus ensure greater legal certainty and improve the legal protection of eu citizens, i am obliged to support this proposal. that's why i voted yes.
written. - in today's times of crisis, ecology and employment are often two opposing concepts. how to enable development while preserving the environment is a question that many governments across europe are asking themselves. some of the answers can be found in this high-quality report entitled 'the eco-innovation: growth and jobs through environmental policy'. eco-innovations can be a multitude of things: a new filter for wastewater treatment, thinner and stronger steel to reduce waste material, or a business model that sells repair services instead of products. the latter is particularly interesting because consumer organizations often warn against consumer products that are designed to break down over time. europe's burning problem, massive youth unemployment, can also be mitigated through eco-innovation and its implementation in the economy. these are the technologies that will be most quickly adopted by the young, the generation that has had the opportunity to be educated in new directions and occupations related to the environment.
written. − europe as a leader in the development of new technologies is responsible for implementing a policy that supports eco-innovation as a future means of achieving economic growth, and i therefore support the proposed eco-innovation report. in the context of economic crisis, we have a unique opportunity to reshape the way sustainable development is achieved. this report calls on the commission to continue to work towards the vision of a european union based on an economy that is resource-efficient and low in co2 emissions. therefore, i believe it is important to design legislation that will be incentive towards creating demand for green technologies. the commission will continue to support the development of the eu ecolabel, which is a key component of the eu ecolabel. the policy of encouraging eco-innovation opens up opportunities for small innovative producers, not just for established corporations, which is why i once again support this report.
written. − this resolution is another good indication that, in addition to preserving the environment and the quality of life, it is also an opportunity for economic growth. i see green growth as an opportunity to reduce unemployment within the european union and to increase competitiveness in the global market through eco-innovation. i also agree that green technology jobs should not be limited to renewable energy production; energy efficiency improvements should also be targeted at the transport sector. green growth is an opportunity for all sectors to explore development opportunities and raise consumer awareness of the importance of buying 'green' products. i support this directive and, as a member of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, i welcome the programmes implemented by the eu to research eco-innovation.
written. − i support the concept of eco-innovation, which combines environmental protection with growth, competitiveness and job creation in a way that balances reducing greenhouse gas emissions and reducing the use of water and raw materials in production. as a representative from croatia, i am excited by the prospect of using this still relatively undeveloped concept in my own country. however, at the same time, i am concerned about the part of the report that calls for eu incentives for companies engaged in this activity, as i believe that the only correct way to regulate the work of these same free markets and healthy competition. however, i still consider the most controversial part of the report to be the one that calls for an increase in the allocation through the cohesion fund to further improve the concept of eco-innovation. i therefore voted against, as i strongly oppose the interference of eu regulations in decisions where the member states of the union should have exclusive competence, because i believe that each member state will best decide for itself what its investment priorities are in using union funds.
written. - today it is difficult to imagine economic and financial life without an open bank account for the majority of the population. therefore, i am voting in favour of this proposal because it brings mutual benefits to the satisfaction of citizens and public institutions. however, i would point out that there are many problems that make it difficult or impossible for citizens to open a bank account in one of the eu member states. the commission notes that the bank's capital needs are not reflected in the total amount of capital needs of the bank. i also believe that the current treatment of citizens who are not permanently resident because of their way of life and activities and jobs should be adapted, because such obstacles ultimately lead to the blockage of the system itself. i welcome the commission's text and believe that this is essential for a normal life and that stigmatisation in society must be prevented. we must allow for the opening of accounts in a country other than the one in which the individual resides and there should be no obstacles. because my constant concern is the young, and because we constantly emphasize mobility, this will be one of the key examples of social inclusion. it is regrettable that 26 million europeans do not have a bank account, and the biggest problem with opening accounts is students, and that is one of the reasons why we support this initiative.
written. - i voted against this report because i believe that the wide differences in bank account services in the member states of the european union may be the main obstacle to the proper implementation of the proposal. while i support certain aspects of this initiative, such as data protection, administrative sanctions, anti-money laundering and terrorist financing policies, i strongly believe that member states should decide whether individual banks or all banks will offer a payment account to customers. it is important to emphasise that the market for retail banking, its level of development and the maturity of electronic and card payments vary considerably between member states. accordingly, standardised european terminology would not be able to provide citizens with accurate information.
written. − as a representative of a country with a long and wide coastline, i fully support the aims of the maritime spatial planning directive. if we are to achieve sustainable management of our coastal and marine resources, coordination and cooperation between member states is essential. what i do not support is the original proposal of the european commission which interfered too much with the powers of the member states. our countries did not discover that they had a sea and a coastline when the commission thought of proposing this directive, but they have had functional solutions and regulations on spatial planning for decades. furthermore, it is not the same to make spatial plans in croatia with over 1200 islands and some country that has a flat coastline. for that reason, i have proposed a number of amendments to this directive and some of them have been accepted. i am pleased that my colleagues on the sectoral committees have shared this view and have changed the document to give member states greater freedom in implementing the provisions of this directive.
written. − i fully support this initiative because i believe that the eu should play a leading role in developing the 'blue economy' and in preserving coastal and marine ecosystems. this can be done through integrated and coherent maritime spatial management. with 88 million people working in the eu's coastal regions, sustainable growth needs to be ensured, as i believe that number will surely increase. the maritime space is still under-explored and by investing in the maritime economy, such as the exploitation of oil, gas and other raw materials and resources, and in maritime and coastal tourism and other similar sectors, the eu has the potential to ensure competitiveness in the global world. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's macro-financial assistance to the countries of central and eastern europe, in particular through the european maritime and fisheries fund (emff). the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new maritime policy and will continue to monitor the implementation of the new maritime policy. but as the representative of a country with one of the most beautiful coastlines in the eu and certainly a lot of natural resources, and as a member of the committee on the environment and public health and food safety, i must also stress the importance of preserving ecosystems, which means that it is also necessary to find a balance between economic, social and natural environmental factors and to preserve coastal and marine ecosystems for future generations.
written. − i welcome the initiative to change mayotte's status from an overseas country or territory to an outermost region or territory, and the application of eu law to mayotte from 1 january 2014. in view of the specific social, environmental and economic situation in mayotte, certain measures are necessary to bring it into line with the standards of the european union, but i agree that this process of adaptation should be gradual. i consider it impossible to make a sudden leap from one system to another, nor to ensure the full application of union law from the outset. a number of transitional periods and arrangements are needed, in particular in the area of waste water treatment, water management, the replacement of non-enriched cages for laying hens, but also to satisfy minimum health and safety requirements relating to the exposure of workers to risks and the rights of patients in cross-border healthcare.
written. - this directive is certainly welcome, as the vast majority of eu member states have a fisheries sector. this sector, like the others, needs to be invested in to improve the living standards of those who depend on fishing, by guaranteeing better working conditions for fishermen, investing in ports, renewing the fishing fleet, and thus contributing to competitiveness. i also support a project to encourage young people and the unemployed to take part in vocational training and education in fisheries and maritime affairs. their inclusion in the fisheries sector would not only reduce unemployment but also have an impact on the sustainability of fisheries. i would also like to stress the importance of investing in the conservation of nature and its resources and the protection of the maritime heritage.
written. - ladies and gentlemen, i wish to express my concern at the impasse in the negotiations between the european institutions on the european capital of culture programme. the commission will also be able to assess the impact of the new rules on the application of the rules on the selection of cities. the deadline for applying is six years in advance. we shall adopt this report today, but according to the information i have, negotiations between parliament, the commission and the council are at a standstill because of the dispute over who will be declared the winner. while i agree that this is an important issue, i hope we don't have to explain to our citizens that this is why we didn't get the 2020 capital of culture. the title of european capital of culture is an honour recognised in europe and the world, and cities that hold it derive tangible cultural, economic and social benefits. i appeal to the negotiators to find a solution as soon as possible so that the programme promoting european culture and spirit since 1985 can continue. i also hope that the cities of the youngest eu member states will have the opportunity to share their rich culture and heritage with the world.
written. − with this report, we are one step closer to solving the question of which european cities will be the capitals of culture in the period 2020-2033. as a new member state, the republic of croatia will be able to nominate one of its cities for the european capital of culture in 2020, together with the republic of ireland. the timeframe for the high-quality preparation of the project and strategy is an important factor determining the success of this project for the local communities and cities that apply. for this reason, i am positive that the date set for croatia and ireland has not been called into question. the financial possibilities of many local communities are not at a high level and at this moment it is difficult to predict how many candidates for the european capital of culture croatia could have. these are the towns and cities that are the cradle of european civilization and culture, but affected by the economic and financial crisis and by the high savings of the central authorities, they may not be able to participate in this project. we still have a lot of work to do if we really want to see european cultural centres under the title of european capitals of culture.
written. − cultural identity is the link that binds the european union together in diversity. it has emerged from a turbulent shared history and is changing today under the influence of globalisation trends. in times of economic crisis, additional efforts are needed to create new generations of europeans and to deepen the european identity. i particularly support the section of this report which stresses the importance of maintaining close links with regional and local authorities, with a view to promoting social inclusion and the importance of civil society, equal opportunities, involving young people in volunteering and linking up with other cultural cities. while i support the report, in the context of this debate i would like to point out the need to take a decision as soon as possible on the continuation of this action. the commission will also be able to use the newly created 'city of culture' programme to support the development of the cultural heritage of the region.
written. − i believe that the project, which dates back to 1985 and has maintained its reputation over the years as the most appreciated cultural initiative in europe, should be welcomed and further work supported to strengthen cultural and economic links between the cities of the european union. the european capital of culture shall highlight the cultural richness and diversity of europe, promote mutual acceptance among european citizens and foster a sense of belonging to the community of european peoples. the epc project is a valuable opportunity to revitalize the cultural life of cities, increase their international visibility, stimulate economic and tourist activities, and general urban development. croatia has many beautiful cities with a rich cultural heritage and i am extremely happy that from 2014 croatian cities, including dubrovnik, will be able to enter the competition for the european capital of culture.
written. − i voted in favour of this proposal because i believe that the european capital of culture project has been successfully promoting cultural diversity and intercultural dialogue for many years in the context of growth and employment and as a builder of social bridges. i am very pleased to note that croatia and ireland are proposed as candidate countries for the european capital of culture 2020. the european capital of culture is not just chosen on the basis of what the city is, but also on the basis of the plans it wants to realise in the coming years. the selection criteria should therefore be more clearly defined in order to allow candidate cities to set their long-term objectives more clearly and to achieve them successfully. in addition, cities should continue to be able to include their rural, neighbouring and cross-border areas, whose participation can highlight cultural life and cultural-historical heritage, in order to reach the wider public and stimulate the development of less developed areas.
written. - the fact is that today roma, in relation to the rest of the population, are discriminated against throughout europe. anti-roman attitudes still prevail in the daily discourse. i have taken the position that roma must be given the rights they should enjoy as human beings, first and foremost socioeconomic rights, rights of movement and rights to education. in a word, the right to equality! there are numerous flaws in government regulations that put the roma in the position they are in. we need to introduce regulations that will more effectively register births, then educate them, and form a strong civil society that will deal with easier, and eventually natural, integration of roma into society. the commission has also been asked to provide funds to address the many problems facing the roma community. a qualitative framework and strategy are key determinants in solving this problem. i call on all member states to make every effort to create instruments that will ensure a favourable social position for roma inclusion in society and to find a solution to this problem.
written. − i believe that ukraine's place is in the european union and i express the wish that bilateral relations between brussels and kiev be channelled towards respecting the will of the people. that will certainly be expressed in the protests in independence square. the eu and ukraine have agreed to cooperate more closely in the fight against terrorism and the fight against organised crime. the european union should be attentive to the political situation of the citizens of ukraine and accordingly the will of the people should be realized through some form of democracy. similarly, care should be taken to avoid escalation of conflict during protests. i strongly condemn any use of force and the need to investigate and prosecute those who have done wrong because such a course of action is clearly against the right to protest. the european union must show goodwill and offer a real european perspective through the ukraine case to eastern partnership countries that show readiness and determination to implement reforms and adopt european union values.
written. - i am very pleased that more and more countries are expressing their desire to join european integration. in addition to honor, this has meant in some countries radical changes involving all segments of political, social and social life, which is very commendable. the european union is the best example of today's democracy, and pursuing it is tantamount to pursuing the strengthening of human, political and social rights, and the strengthening of peace and security. albania has made a step forward with parliamentary elections in june 2013 and a peaceful handover of power. this is certainly having a positive effect on the process of democratization of the country and improving its international reputation. but in addition to political and legal reforms, i would also emphasise social reforms which are equally important, drawing attention to the living conditions of the roma population in albania which are terrible and urgently need to be improved. there is still a lot of work to be done, a lot of effort, a lot of cooperation and support, but i support every step forward and the desire for albania to sail towards the eu.
mr president, the next european council devoted to a common defence policy comes at a good time, as our american allies are increasingly turning to new priorities and security challenges in asia, and the european union must therefore take on greater responsibility in the field of defence. in this regard, in addition to the already defined 'pulling and sharing' approach, it is important to establish a new coordination mechanism through the meetings of the council in the format of defence ministers. this will improve coordination and cooperation and enable the adoption of a so-called white paper, with clear missions and tasks for the european union in defence policy. the strategic orientation of common european defence must therefore in no way be understood as a form of opposition to nato. the north atlantic alliance remains for many of our member states the cornerstone of the european security architecture and the framework for transatlantic cooperation and partnership. however, it is precisely in this euro-atlantic spirit that the level of european union's capability and participation in the field of international security needs to be increased.
written. - i need not remind you that one of the primary objectives of the european union is to promote peace, european values and the well-being of its peoples. i believe that the common security and defence policy serves these aims, and that is why defence should be given the attention it deserves. i also believe that member states should give their support to a strong european defence, technological and industrial base, as defence policy is a member state competence. in addition to defence policy, the importance of sustainable economic growth should also be emphasised. this can be achieved through enhanced economic cooperation, which should go hand in hand with the eu's incentive mechanism. these are issues that should be addressed by the main institutions of the union, and create good cooperation and communication between them to better implement eu policies.
written. − i support this report because it shows progress in terms of democracy and the legality of the eu institutions, while at the same time reflecting the consistency and dynamism of the european structure. as the separation of powers is one of the foundations of legitimacy and democracy, i am in favour of clear frameworks and powers for the eu institutions. i consider it essential to introduce a structured union policy and order in order to ensure the effectiveness of measures and actions taken and to respect the democratic functioning of european integration. in the light of the economic crisis at the moment, with everyone busy dealing with the eu's monetary policy and new rules and measures being created to deal with the new problems, it is extremely important to focus on the central issue, which is the powers of the institutions and the procedures they implement in the eu. i support the commission's approach, which aims at cooperation, but we must know what can be done at european level and what at other levels. i also support the balance between the institutions, and we want to preserve the unity of the union, but also the diversity within that unity. we are still left to act within the framework of the existing treaties, but we must not leave out the control of the european parliament.
mr. president, i believe that there is a definite need to formalize the way of dealing with open issues, especially in turbulent times. the question is: what does parliament want, or what do the people it represents want? it is clear that in times of crisis there is a greater need for more frequent meetings of the council and it is clear that this is no longer just a functional level of relations, but also a political one. we must be aware that relations between the institutions of the union are being modified on a daily basis. times are turbulent and accordingly we must not only react, but be prepared in advance. i therefore welcome this report on a subject which should be given the greatest possible attention.
written. − the european council gives the union the necessary impetus for its development and sets the general political guidelines and priorities for its development. i therefore believe that the working relationship between the council and parliament should be strengthened. however, i would point out that the council does not have a legislative function, unlike parliament, and that parliament must be aware of its activities in order to achieve a kind of indirect control and the importance of joint communication and cooperation. this allows the democratic nature of the provisions on the parliament's supervisory function to be strengthened. as there are many ways of implementing and implementing interinstitutional communication, i believe that interinstitutional agreements or exchanges of letters or joint statements are the most effective. this is how the eu's most important principles of transparency and consistency are achieved. i believe that the president of the council, like the president of the european commission, should be present in person and present the agenda items to parliament, in order to allow them to be debated in parliament before the resolution is adopted. the president of parliament should also attend fully the meetings of the council when interinstitutional issues are being dealt with and align his or her own agenda in order to be consistent in the choice of his or her representatives. i welcome this directive and believe that it can help to establish better working relations and more successful cooperation between two very important institutions of the union.
written. − green infrastructure is a very responsible mechanism, and is particularly important in cities where it makes a major contribution to air quality and the creation of recreational areas. in addition, it makes a significant contribution to the key eu policy objectives of increasing employment and job creation, economic benefits and biodiversity. the commission should also be able to provide information on the impact of the measures taken on the environment and the environmentally sustainable use of energy. i support this proposal primarily because it's good for human health, reducing heat waves in cities where 60% of the population lives, and also helping to fight climate change. given the funding difficulties of this programme, i support an innovative financing mechanism, on the grounds that it would contribute to the creation of new jobs in the building industry. it is necessary that all levels at which such decisions are taken, namely national, regional and local authorities, are actively involved in addressing this issue. the commission will also continue to monitor the implementation of the union's environmental policy and its implementation in the member states. the commission notes that the bank's capital needs are not limited to the capital needs of the beneficiary, but are limited to the capital needs of the beneficiary. so i fully support this proposal.
in writing. − i am pleased to see this motion for a resolution on the call for a measurable and binding commitment against tax evasion and tax avoidance in the eu, principally because anything that will help create more efficient and effective tax collection at a national or eu level will be beneficial. we were all affected by the recent economic crisis when a systemic error by one country caused a collapse in countless others. this global domino effect shows us the need for a binding commitment at both global and national level. such a commitment needs to include more transparent exchange of information in the field of taxation. accordingly, transparency in information exchange will help countries combat tax evasion, tax fraud and tax havens more efficiently. this proposal also urges member states to cooperate with the commission and the ecb on improving laws and regulations on taxation. this will help member states revise bilateral agreements on tax matters, help them be more cautious and help them combat tax fraud and tax evasion more assertively and effectively.
